Towards the classification of Fano 4 4 -folds with b 2 ≥ 7 b_{2}\geq 7
C. Casagrande

Abstract
. We study (smooth, complex) Fano 4 4 -folds X X with Picard number ρ X ≥ 7 \rho_{X}\geq 7 . We show that if ρ X > 9 \rho_{X}>9 , then X X is a product of del Pezzo surfaces (Th. 1.1 ), thus improving results in [ Cas24 , CS24 ] ; the statement is now optimal. In the range ρ X ∈ { 7 , 8 , 9 } \rho_{X}\in\{7,8,9\} we show that if X X is not a product of surfaces, and has no small elementary contraction, then it is the blow-up of a cubic 4 4 -fold along a special configuration of planes (Th. 1.2 ). When instead ρ X ≥ 7 \rho_{X}\geq 7 and X X has a small elementary contraction, we study X X depending on its fixed prime divisors, giving explicit results on the geometry of X X in the framework of birational geometry. In particular for the boundary case ρ X = 9 \rho_{X}=9 we show that either X X is a product of surfaces, or X X belongs to two explicit families, or there is a sequence of flips X ⇢ X ′ X\dasharrow X^{\prime} where X ′ X^{\prime} is a smooth projective 4 4 -fold with an elementary contraction onto a 3 3 -fold (Th. 1.4 ). In the paper we also give several results on rational contractions of fiber type of Fano 4 4 -folds, and more generally of Mori dream spaces; in particular we use some properties of del Pezzo surfaces over non-closed fields, applied to generic fibers.



1.
Introduction

Since the classification of Fano
3
-folds in the 80’s, there has been a lot of interest in the study of higher dimensional Fano varieties, starting from dimension
4
. With the introduction of the Lefschetz defect (see below), the author has developped a program to study (smooth, complex) Fano
4
-folds
X
with large second Betti number
b_{2}(X)

via birational geometry, in the framework of the Minimal Model Program. Let us recall that, since
X
is Fano, we have
b_{2}(X)=\rho_{X}
where
\rho_{X}
is the Picard number of
X
.

In this paper we focus on the explicit study and classification of Fano
4
-folds
X
with Picard number
\rho_{X}\geq 7
.
Our first result is the following.

Theorem 1.1

(Cor.
10.5
)
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{X}>9
. Then
X\cong S_{1}\times S_{2}
, with
S_{i}
del Pezzo surfaces.

Let us note that the statement above is proven in
[
Cas24
]
for
\rho>12
, and in
[
CS24
, Cor. 1.4]
for
\rho=12
, therefore the theorem is new for the cases
\rho=10
and
\rho=11
. It is now optimal, as we know one family of Fano
4
-folds with
\rho=9
that are not products of surfaces.

We recall that, so far, there are only 5 known families of Fano
4
-folds with
\rho\in\{7,8,9\}
that are not products. Three such families, with
\rho=7,8,9
, are given by the Fano models of the blow-up of
\mathbb{P}^{4}
at
6,7,8
general points respectively (see §
11.1
), and two additional families with
\rho=7
are constructed in
[
CS24
, Prop. 1.9]
(see §
11.2
). Some candidates are also given in [
ibid.
, Questions 7.6 and 7.25];
in this range of Picard numbers we expect few families.

Besides Th.
1.1
, in this paper we perform a detailed study of Fano
4
-folds
X\not\cong S_{1}\times S_{2}
with
\rho_{X}\in\{7,8,9\}
; we obtain many explicit results on the geometry of
X
, including some classification results, and new candidates (see Questions
11.2
,
11.5
,
11.6
).
In particular we give a complete characterization of the case where
X
does not have small elementary contractions, as follows.

Theorem 1.2

(Th.
8.1
)
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{X}\geq 7
, not isomorphic to a product of surfaces, and without small elementary contractions.

Then
\rho_{X}\leq 9
and there are a cubic
4
-fold
Z\subset\mathbb{P}^{5}
with at most isolated ordinary double points, and
s=\rho_{X}-1
distinct planes
A_{1},\dotsc,A_{s}\subset Z
intersecting pairwise in a point, such that
X
is obtained from
Z
by blowing-up one plane
A_{i}
and successively the transforms of the other ones.
1
1

1

The resulting
4
-fold does not depend on the order of the blow-ups, see Lemma
2.10
.

We do not know whether a Fano
4
-fold as in the statement above does exist, see §
11.7
and Question
11.6
, so on one hand we get more candidate families of Fano
4
-fold as blow-ups of cubic
4
-folds, on the other hand it may very well be that this case does not happen. Let us note that Th.
1.2
builds on the study in
[
Cas24
]
, where under the same assumptions, the bound
\rho_{X}\leq 12
was proven. We refer the reader to
8.4
for an overview of the proof of Th.
1.2
.

Consider now the remaining case of a Fano
4
-fold
X\not\cong S_{1}\times S_{2}
, with
\rho_{X}\geq 7
, and having small elementary contractions.
Here we rely on the classification of
fixed prime divisors
2
2

2

A prime divisor
D\subset X
is fixed if
D=\operatorname{Bs}|mD|
for all
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
.

D\subset X
from
[
Cas13a
]
(see p.
2.3
and Section
5
). Given such
D
,
there
are a sequence of flips
X\dasharrow X^{\prime}
with
X^{\prime}
smooth, and an elementary divisorial contraction
\sigma\colon X^{\prime}\to Y
, such that

D
is the transform of
\operatorname{Exc}(\sigma)
, and
Y
is Fano. Moreover we have only four possibilities for
\sigma
: it is
the blow-up of a smooth point, or of a smooth irreducible curve, or
of
an isolated, locally factorial, terminal singularity with

\operatorname{Exc}(\sigma)
a quadric, or finally of an irreducible surface.
We call
D

of type
(3,0)^{\text{\it sm}}
,
(3,1)^{\text{\it sm}}
,
(3,0)^{Q}
, or
(3,2)
accordingly.

When
X
has a small elementary contraction, it must contain some fixed prime divisor
D
of type
(3,0)^{\text{\it sm}}
,
(3,1)^{\text{\it sm}}
, or
(3,0)^{Q}
(Rem.
9.1
).
We study
X
depending on the type of fixed prime divisors that it contains; in particular in the first case, when
D
is of type
(3,0)^{\text{\it sm}}
, we show the following.

Theorem 1.3

(Th.
9.2
)
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{X}\geq 7
, having a fixed prime divisor of type
(3,0)^{\text{\it sm}}
. Then one of the following holds:

(i)

\rho_{X}\leq 9
and
X
has an elementary rational contraction onto a
3
-fold;
3
3

3

A
rational contraction
of
X
is a rational map
f\colon X\dasharrow Y
that factors as a sequence of flips
X\dasharrow X^{\prime}
followed by a contraction
f^{\prime}\colon X^{\prime}\to Y
, namely a morphism such that
f^{\prime}_{*}\mathcal{O}_{X^{\prime}}=\mathcal{O}_{Y}
with
Y
projective,
see §
2
;
f
is
elementary
if
\rho_{X}-\rho_{Y}=1
.

(ii)

\rho_{X}=7
,

h^{0}(X,-K_{X})\leq 15
,
and there is a sequence of flips
X\dasharrow X^{\prime}
such that
X^{\prime}=\operatorname{Bl}_{6\mskip 1.0mu\text{\it pts}}Y
,
Y
a smooth Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{Y}=1
.

Th.
1.3
refines the study in
[
Cas17
]
, where under the same assumptions, the bound
\rho_{X}\leq 12
was proven. We refer the reader to
9.3
for an overview of the proof of Th.
1.3
.

The remaining possibility is that of a Fano
4
-fold
X
with
\rho_{X}\geq 7
, having a fixed prime divisor of type
(3,1)^{\text{\it sm}}
or
(3,0)^{Q}
, and no fixed prime divisor of type
(3,0)^{\text{\it sm}}
; we study this case
by refining the results in
[
Cas22
]
. This allows to complete the proof of Th.
1.1
, and also
to give a partial result on the case of Picard number
\rho=9
, as follows.

Theorem 1.4

(Cor.
10.5
)
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{X}=9
.
Then one of the following holds:

(i)

X\cong S_{1}\times S_{2}
, with
S_{i}
del Pezzo surfaces;

(ii)

X
has an elementary rational contraction onto a
3
-fold;

(iii)

X
is the blow-up of
W
along a normal surface
S
,
where
W
is the Fano model of
\operatorname{Bl}_{7\mskip 1.0mu\text{\it pts}}\mathbb{P}^{4}
, and
S\subset W
is the transform of a cubic scroll or
a cone over a twisted cubic in

\mathbb{P}^{4}
, containing the blown-up points;

(iv)

X
is a blow-up of a cubic
4
-fold as in Th.
1.2
.

We note that our only example of Fano
4
-fold
X\not\cong S_{1}\times S_{2}
with
\rho_{X}=9
has an elementary rational contraction onto a
3
-fold (see §
11.1
), while we do not know whether cases
(iii)
and
(iv)
do happen (see
[
CS24
, §7.2]
for
(iii)
, and §
11.7
for
(iv)
).

Our general approach is to study Fano
4
-folds via their contractions and rational contractions, and
there are a few tools and results that we use systematically and
are crucial in proving the previous theorems; let us introduce them.

Rational contractions of fiber type.
We recall that a
rational contraction
is a rational map
f\colon X\dasharrow Y
that factors as a sequence of flips
X\dasharrow X^{\prime}
followed by a
contraction

f^{\prime}\colon X^{\prime}\to Y
, namely a morphism such that
f^{\prime}_{*}\mathcal{O}_{X^{\prime}}=\mathcal{O}_{Y}
, with
Y
projective;
f
is
of fiber type
when
\dim Y<\dim X
.
We study in detail rational contractions of fiber type
f\colon X\dasharrow Y
of Fano
4
-folds (more generally of Mori dream spaces),
see also
[
Cas08
,
Cas20
]
.

The case where
\dim Y=3
is treated in
[
CS24
]
, where a sharp bound on
\rho_{X}
is given, together with a partial classification for the range
\rho_{X}\geq 7
, as follows.

Theorem 1.5

(
[
CS24
]
, Theorems 1.3, 1.10, and 1.11)
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{X}\geq 7
, having a rational contraction onto a
3
-fold,
and not isomorphic to a product of surfaces.
Then
\rho_{X}\leq 9
, and one of the following holds:

(i)

X
has an elementary rational contraction onto a
3
-fold;

(ii)

X
is the blow-up of
W
along a normal surface
S
,
where
W
is the Fano model of
\operatorname{Bl}_{\rho_{\scriptscriptstyle X}-2\mskip 2.0mu\text{\it pts}}\mathbb{P}^{4}
, and
S\subset W
is the transform of a surface
A\subset\mathbb{P}^{4}
containing the blown-up points. The surface
A
is either
a cubic scroll, or
a cone over a twisted cubic, or a sextic K3 surface with
A_{1}
or
A_{2}
singularities at the blown-up points; if
A
is a K3 surface then
\rho_{X}=7
.

We note that, in the setting of Th.
1.5
, the case where we do not yet have a classification is when
X
has an elementary rational contraction onto a
3
-fold.

Consider now a rational contraction of fiber type
f\colon X\dasharrow Y
with
\dim Y\in\{1,2\}
. We can choose a factorization of
f
as
X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\varphi}}{{\dasharrow}}X^{\prime}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f^{\prime}}}{{\to}}Y
where
\varphi
is a sequence of flips,
X^{\prime}
is smooth, and
f^{\prime}
is a
K
-negative contraction of fiber type. Let
F\subset X^{\prime}
be a general fiber; then
F
is smooth and is either a del Pezzo surface, or a Fano
3
-fold.

Let
\mathcal{N}_{1}(X^{\prime})
be the vector space of one-cycles in
X^{\prime}
, with
\mathbb{R}
-coefficients, modulo numerical equivalence, and
let us consider
\mathcal{N}_{1}(F,X^{\prime}):=\iota_{*}(\mathcal{N}_{1}(F))\subset\mathcal{N}_{1}(X^{\prime})
, where
\iota\colon F\hookrightarrow X^{\prime}
is the inclusion. Then
d_{f}:=\dim\mathcal{N}_{1}(F,X^{\prime})
is an invariant of
f
and does not depend on the choice of the resolution
f^{\prime}
(Def.-Rem.
3.2
).
Moreover
d_{f}
is equal to the Picard number of the generic fiber
X_{\eta}
of
f^{\prime}
, which is a smooth Fano variety over the non-closed field
K=\mathbb{C}(Y)
of rational functions on
Y
(Lemma
3.5
).

When
d_{f}
is large enough, we show that
f
factors through a rational contraction onto a
3
-fold, as follows.

Theorem 1.6

(Th.
4.1
)
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold and
f\colon X\dasharrow Y
a
non-trivial
4
4

4

Namely
Y\neq\{\text{\it pt}\}
.
rational contraction of fiber type.
If
d_{f}\geq 5
, then

f
can be factored as
X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle g}}{{\dasharrow}}Z\stackrel{{\scriptstyle h}}{{\to}}Y
, where
\dim Z=3
.

To prove this, we use different strategies depending on the dimension of the base
Y
. When
\dim Y=2
, we consider the generic fiber
X_{\eta}
of
f^{\prime}\colon X^{\prime}\to Y
(notation as above),
which is a smooth del Pezzo surface over the field
K=\mathbb{C}(Y)
.
When
d_{f}=\rho_{X_{\eta}}\geq 5
, there is a morphism
X_{\eta}\to C
onto a curve over
K
(Lemma
4.2
), and we use results on spreading out to get the statement (see Lemmas
3.6
and
3.14
, and Section
4
).

Instead, when
Y\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}
, a general fiber
F\subset X^{\prime}
of
f^{\prime}
is a smooth, complex Fano
3
-fold with
\rho_{F}\geq d_{f}=\dim\mathcal{N}_{1}(F,X^{\prime})
. When
d_{f}\geq 5
, we use the monodromy action on
\mathcal{N}^{1}(F)
and
\operatorname{Nef}(F)
to extend a contraction
F\to S
with
\dim S=2
to a relative contraction over some open subset of
Y=\mathbb{P}^{1}
, see Prop.
4.4
.

Using the results from
[
CS24
]
on rational contractions onto
3
-folds, we deduce from Th.
1.6
the following result.

Theorem 1.7

(Th.
4.5
)
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{X}\geq 7
, not isomorphic to a product of surfaces, and
f\colon X\dasharrow Y
a non-trivial rational contraction of fiber type.
Then
d_{f}\leq 4
.

The bound
d_{f}\leq 4
is sharp, see the discussion after Th.
4.5
.

Theorem
1.7
is very much related to how
the faces of the cone of effective divisors can intersect the cone of movable divisors.
More precisely, let us consider
\mathcal{N}^{1}(X)
, the vector space of divisors with
\mathbb{R}
-coefficients up to numerical equivalence, and in it the cones
\operatorname{Eff}(X)
and
\operatorname{Mov}(X)
respectively of effective and movable divisors (see Section
2
); we have
\operatorname{Mov}(X)\subset\operatorname{Eff}(X)
, and since
X
is Fano, both cones are rational polyhedral.
If
\tau
is a face of
\operatorname{Eff}(X)
, we say that
\tau
is a
movable face
if
\tau\cap\operatorname{Mov}(X)\neq\{0\}
, otherwise we say that
\tau
is a
fixed face
(Def.
2.4
).

Given a non-trivial rational contraction of fiber type
f\colon X\dasharrow Y
, we have that
\tau_{f}:=\langle[D]\in\mathcal{N}^{1}(X)\,|D
is effective and
\operatorname{Supp}D
does not dominate
Y\rangle
is a movable face of
\operatorname{Eff}(X)
, of dimension
\rho_{X}-d_{f}
(Def.-Rem.
3.2
, Rem.
3.4
). Conversely, given a proper, movable face
\tau
of
\operatorname{Eff}(X)
, there exists a non-trivial rational contraction of fiber type
f\colon X\dasharrow Y
with
d_{f}\geq\rho_{X}-\dim\tau
(see Rem.
3.11
). This gives the following.

Corollary 1.8

(Cor.
4.10
)
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{X}\geq 7
, not isomorphic to a product of surfaces.
If
\tau
is a movable face of
\operatorname{Eff}(X)
, then

\dim\tau\geq\rho_{X}-4
. In particular the cone
\operatorname{Eff}(X)
is generated by classes of fixed prime divisors.

The Lefschetz defect.

Another essential tool
is the Lefschetz defect, an invariant of
X
defined as follows.
For any prime divisor
\iota\colon D\hookrightarrow X
, set
\mathcal{N}_{1}(D,X):=\iota_{*}(\mathcal{N}_{1}(D))\subset\mathcal{N}_{1}(X)
. Then we define the Lefschetz defect of
X
as

\delta_{X}:=\max\{\operatorname{codim}\mathcal{N}_{1}(D,X)\,|\,D\text{ a prime divisor in }X\}.

We refer the reader to
[
Cas12
,
CRS22
]
for results on the Lefschetz defect of Fano varieties of arbitrary dimension; in dimension
4
we have the following.

Theorem 1.9

(
[
Cas12
]
, Cor. 1.3,
[
CS24
]
, Th. 1.8)
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{X}\geq 7
, not isomorphic to a product of surfaces. Then
\delta_{X}\leq 1
.

Fixed prime divisors of type
(3,2)
.

A fixed prime divisor of type
(3,2)
is the exceptional divisor
E
of an elementary divisorial contraction
\sigma\colon X\to Y
such that
\dim\sigma(E)=2
(see Th.-Def.
5.1
). If
X\not\cong S_{1}\times S_{2}
and
\rho_{X}\geq 7
, we have
\delta_{X}\leq 1
by Th.
1.9
, therefore
\mathcal{N}_{1}(E,X)
has codimension at most one in
\mathcal{N}_{1}(X)
. We show the following.

Theorem 1.10

(Th.
7.1
)
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{X}\geq 7
, not isomorphic to a product of surfaces, and
E\subset X
a fixed prime divisor of type
(3,2)
such that
\mathcal{N}_{1}(E,X)\subsetneq\mathcal{N}_{1}(X)
.
Then
\rho_{X}\leq 9
and

X
has an elementary rational contraction onto a
3
-fold.

This improves the bound
\rho_{X}\leq 12
shown in
[
Cas17
, Prop. 5.32]
. Besides the bound
\rho_{X}\leq 9
, in Th.
7.1
we give several geometric properties of
X
, that could lead to an explicit classification of this case.

We refer the reader to
7.3
for an overview of the proof of Th.
1.10
.
In particular, we need a generalization of Theorems
1.6
and
1.7
in the case of
rational contractions
f\colon X\dasharrow S
with
\dim S=2
that are
quasi-elementary
, namely with
d_{f}=\rho_{X}-\rho_{S}
(see Def.
3.8
), as follows.

Theorem 1.11

(Th.
6.1
)
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{X}\geq 7
, not isomorphic to a product of surfaces, and
f\colon X\dasharrow S
a quasi-elementary rational contraction onto a surface. Then
\rho_{X}-\rho_{S}\leq 3
,
and if
\rho_{X}-\rho_{S}>1
, then
f
factors as
X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle g}}{{\dasharrow}}Y\stackrel{{\scriptstyle h}}{{\dasharrow}}S
where
\dim Y=3
.

The bound
\rho_{X}-\rho_{S}\leq 3
follows easily from the previous results, while the proof that, in the non-elementary case,
f
factors through a
3
-fold,
is rather long (see Section
6
).
We work by contradiction: by assuming that the statement does not hold, we get an explicit description of
X
as a sequence of flips and blow-ups from
\mathbb{P}^{2}\times\mathbb{P}^{2}
; finally we show that this construction never yields a Fano
4
-fold. See
6.5
for a more detailed overview of the proof.

Let us conclude this Introduction by noting that the condition
\rho\geq 7
arises naturally and is optimal for many of these results, like Th.
1.2
, Th.
1.5
, Th.
1.7
, Cor.
1.8
, Th.
1.9
, and Th.
1.11
; on the other hand
Fano
4
-folds with
\rho\leq 6
exhibit different behaviours,
as we show in Examples
4.6
,
4.7
, and
4.11
, and in §
11.4
.

The structure of the paper is as follows.
In Section
2
we fix the notation and terminology, and we recall some preliminary results needed in the sequel.

Section
3
contains several preliminary notions and results on regular and rational contractions of fiber type
f\colon X\dasharrow Y
of Mori dream spaces. In particular we define quasi-elementary and special rational contractions of fiber type and recall their main properties, and we introduce
the invariant
d_{f}
mentioned above.

In Section
4
we apply the previous results to Fano
4
-folds, and we prove Theorems
1.6
and
1.7
on rational contractions of fiber type, and Cor.
1.8
on movable faces of the cone of effective divisors.

In Section
5
we recall the classification of fixed prime divisors in Fano
4
-folds
X
with

\rho_{X}\geq 7
in four types, and give many related properties that are used in the sequel.

In Section
6
we prove Th.
1.11
on quasi-elementary rational contractions onto a surface. In Section
7
we prove Th.
1.10
on fixed prime divisors of type
(3,2)
, and give some applications needed in the sequel.

In Section
8

we prove
Th.
1.2
, that characterizes Fano
4
-folds with no small contractions (with
\rho\geq 7
, and not products) as suitable blow-ups of cubic
4
-folds.

In Section
9
we prove
Th.
1.3
on Fano
4
-folds with a fixed prime divisor of type
(3,0)^{\text{\it sm}}
. Finally
in
Section
10
we consider Fano
4
-folds with a fixed prime divisor of type
(3,1)^{\text{\it sm}}
or
(3,0)^{Q}
, and prove Theorems
1.1
and
1.4
; we also give a partial result on the case
\rho=8
(Cor.
10.6
).

In Section
11
we collect several examples. We first recall the known examples of Fano
4
-folds that are not products, with
\rho\geq 7
(§
11.1
and §
11.2
), and then give other relevant explicit examples; in some cases we do not know whether these
4
-folds are actually Fano for large values of the Picard number.

4
-folds containing a configuration of planes as in Th.
1.2
.

2.
Notation and preliminaries

If
\mathcal{N}
is a finite-dimensional real vector space and
a_{1},\dotsc,a_{r}\in\mathcal{N}
,
\langle a_{1},\dotsc,a_{r}\rangle
denotes the convex cone in
\mathcal{N}
generated by
a_{1},\dotsc,a_{r}
.
Moreover, for every
a\neq 0
,
a^{\perp}
is the hyperplane orthogonal to
a
in the dual vector space
\mathcal{N}^{*}
.
If
\alpha\in\mathcal{N}^{*}
and
\tau:=\langle a\rangle
, we write
\alpha\cdot\tau>0
(respectively
\alpha\cdot\tau<0
,
\alpha\cdot\tau=0
) if

\alpha\cdot a>0
(respectively
\alpha\cdot a<0
,
\alpha\cdot a=0
).
A facet of a cone is a face of codimension one. If
\mathcal{C}\subset\mathcal{N}
is a convex polyhedral cone, we denote by
\mathcal{C}^{\vee}\subset\mathcal{N}^{*}
its dual cone.

Lemma 2.1
.

Let
\sigma
be a convex polyhedral cone in a finite dimensional real vector
space
\mathcal{N}
. Let
\tau
be a one-dimensional face of
\sigma
, and let
\alpha\in\mathcal{N}^{*}
be such that

\alpha\cdot\tau<0
and
\alpha\cdot\tau^{\prime}\geq 0
for every one-dimensional face
\tau^{\prime}\neq\tau
of
\sigma
.
If
\eta
is a face of
\sigma
such that
\eta\subset\ker\alpha
, then
\tau+\eta
is a face of
\sigma
.

Proof.

This is proved in
[
Cas20
, Lemma 4.7]
when
\dim\eta=1
; the same proof applies to the general case.
∎

The
index
of a locally factorial Fano variety
X
is the divisibility of
-K_{X}
in
\operatorname{Pic}(X)
.

For the standard terminology and properties in birational geometry and about Mori dream spaces, we refer the reader to
[
KM98
,
HK00
]
, and we recall that smooth Fano varieties are Mori dream spaces by
[
BCHM10
, Cor. 1.3.2]
.

A
contraction
is a projective, surjective morphism with connected fibers, between normal quasi-projective varieties. It can be birational or of fiber type. A contraction
f\colon X\to Y
is
K
-negative if, for some
m\in\mathbb{N}
,

-mK_{X}
is Cartier and
f
-ample.

Let
X
be a projective, normal, and
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial Mori dream space.
As usual we denote by
\mathcal{N}_{1}(X)
the real vector space of numerical equivalence classes of one-cycles in
X
with real coefficients, and by
\mathcal{N}^{1}(X)
the dual vector space of numerical equivalence classes of
\mathbb{R}
-divisors in
X
.
If
D
is a divisor and
C
a curve in
X
, we denote by
[D]\in\mathcal{N}^{1}(X)
and
[C]\in\mathcal{N}_{1}(X)
their numerical equivalence classes, and we set
D^{\perp}:=[D]^{\perp}\subset\mathcal{N}_{1}(X)
and
C^{\perp}:=[C]^{\perp}\subset\mathcal{N}^{1}(X)
.

A movable divisor is an effective divisor
D
such that the stable
base locus of the linear system
|D|
has codimension
\geq 2
.
We will consider the usual cones of divisors and of curves:

\operatorname{Nef}(X)\subseteq\operatorname{Mov}(X)\subseteq\operatorname{Eff}(X)\subset\mathcal{N}^{1}(X)\hskip 10.00002pt\text{and}\hskip 10.00002pt\operatorname{mov}(X)\subseteq\operatorname{NE}(X)\subset\mathcal{N}_{1}(X),

where
all the notations are standard except maybe
\operatorname{mov}(X)
, which is the convex cone generated by classes of curves moving in a family covering
X
.
Since
X
is a Mori dream space, all these cones are rational polyhedral. An extremal ray
R
of
\operatorname{NE}(X)
is a one-dimensional face of this cone. Given a divisor
D
on
X
, we say that
R
is
D
-negative (respectively
D
-positive,
D
-trivial) if
D\cdot R<0
(respectively
D\cdot R>0
,
D\cdot R=0
). We also write
\operatorname{Locus}(R)
for the union in
X
of all curves with class in
R
.

Given a contraction
f\colon X\to Y
, we set
\operatorname{NE}(f):=(\ker f_{*})\cap\operatorname{NE}(X)
, a face of
\operatorname{NE}(X)
of dimension
\rho_{X}-\rho_{Y}
.
The contraction
f
is
elementary
if
\rho_{X}-\rho_{Y}=1
. An elementary contraction can be divisorial, small, or of fiber type.

An elementary contraction
f
is
of type
(a,b)
if
\dim\operatorname{Exc}(f)=a
and
\dim f(\operatorname{Exc}(f))=b
. If
n=\dim X
, we say that
f
is
of type
(n-1,b)^{\text{\it sm}}
if
f
is the blow-up of a smooth,
b
-dimensional irreducible subvariety
A\subset Y_{\text{\it reg}}
. Finally when
n=4
we say that
f
is
of type
(3,0)^{Q}
if
\operatorname{Exc}(f)
is an irreducible
3
-dimensional quadric with

\mathcal{N}_{\operatorname{Exc}(f)/X}\cong\mathcal{O}_{Q}(-1)
.

A flip is the flip of a small elementary contraction. Given a divisor
D
on
X
, a
D
-negative (respectively
D
-positive,
D
-trivial) flip is the flip of a small elementary contraction
f\colon X\to Y
such that
D\cdot\operatorname{NE}(f)<0

(respectively
D\cdot\operatorname{NE}(f)>0
,
D\cdot\operatorname{NE}(f)=0
). We do not assume that contractions or flips are
K
-negative, unless specified.

A
SQM

(small
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial modification)
is a birational map
\varphi\colon X\dasharrow X^{\prime}
such that
X^{\prime}
is again projective, normal, and
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial, and
\varphi
is an isomophism in codimension one; since
X
is a Mori dream space,
\varphi
always factors as a finite sequence of flips.

A
rational contraction
is a rational map
f\colon X\dasharrow Y
that factors as
X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{SQM}}}{{\dasharrow}}X^{\prime}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f^{\prime}}}{{\to}}Y
, where
f^{\prime}
is a contraction;
f
can be birational or of fiber type, and we say that
f
is non-trivial if
Y\neq\{\text{\it pt}\}
. We call
f^{\prime}
a
resolution
of
f
. We say that
f
is elementary if
\rho_{X}-\rho_{Y}=1
; if
f
is elementary and birational, then it can be divisorial or small, depending whether
\operatorname{Exc}(f^{\prime})
is a prime divisor or
\operatorname{codim}\operatorname{Exc}(f^{\prime})>1
.

There is a fan in
\mathcal{N}^{1}(X)
, called the
Mori chamber decomposition
and denoted by
\operatorname{MCD}(X)
, supported on
\operatorname{Mov}(X)
, whose cones are in bijection with the set of rational contractions of
X
(up to isomorphism of the target); the cone corresponding to
f\colon X\dasharrow Y
is
f^{*}(\operatorname{Nef}(Y))
.

Remark 2.2
.

Let
X
be a projective, normal, and
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial Mori dream space, and
f\colon X\dasharrow Y
,
g\colon X\dasharrow Z
two rational contractions. Then there exists a contraction
h\colon Z\to Y
such that
f=h\circ g
if and only if
f^{*}(\operatorname{Nef}(Y))\subset g^{*}(\operatorname{Nef}(Z))
.

A
fixed prime divisor
is a prime divisor
D
which is the stable base locus of the linear system

|D|
; then
[D]\in\mathcal{N}^{1}(X)
generates a one-dimensional face of
\operatorname{Eff}(X)
.

Remark 2.3

(
[
Cas13a
]
, Rem. 2.19)
.

Let
X
be a projective, normal, and
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial Mori dream space. The fixed prime divisors in
X
are the exceptional divisors of divisorial elementary rational contractions of
X
.

Definition 2.4
.

Let
\tau
be a face of
\operatorname{Eff}(X)
. We say that
\tau
is a
fixed face
if
\tau\cap\operatorname{Mov}(X)=\{0\}
, otherwise we say that
\tau
is a
movable face
.

Definition 2.5
.

We say that two fixed prime divisors
D,E\subset X
are
adjacent
if
\langle[D],[E]\rangle
is a fixed face of
\operatorname{Eff}(X)
.

Let
Z\subset X
be a closed subset. We set

\mathcal{N}_{1}(Z,X):=\iota_{*}(\mathcal{N}_{1}(Z))\subseteq\mathcal{N}_{1}(X),

where
\iota\colon Z\hookrightarrow X
is the inclusion.

Lemma 2.6
.

Let
f\colon X\to Y
be a contraction and
Z\subset X
a closed subset. Then:

\dim\mathcal{N}_{1}(Z,X)\leq\rho_{X}-\rho_{Y}+\dim\mathcal{N}_{1}\bigl(f(Z),Y\bigr).

Proof.

The pushforward
f_{*}\colon\mathcal{N}_{1}(X)\to\mathcal{N}_{1}(Y)
is a surjective linear map with kernel of dimension
\rho_{X}-\rho_{Y}
, and
f_{*}(\mathcal{N}_{1}(Z,X))=\mathcal{N}_{1}(f(Z),Y)
.
∎

Remark 2.7
.

Let
Z\subset X
a closed subset and
D\subset X
a prime divisor such that
Z\cap D=\emptyset
. Then
\mathcal{N}_{1}(Z,X)\subset D^{\perp}\subsetneq\mathcal{N}_{1}(X)
.

Indeed for every curve
C\subset Z
we have
D\cdot C=0
, hence
[C]\in D^{\perp}
.

An
ordinary double point
is an isolated singularity
x_{0}
of an
n
-dimensional variety
X
that is locally analytically isomorphic to the vertex of the cone over a smooth
(n-1)
-dimensional quadric.

Remark 2.8
.

Let
X
be a quasi-projective
4
-fold with an ordinary double point at
x_{0}
and
\operatorname{Sing}(X)=\{x_{0}\}
. Let
f\colon X^{\prime}\to X
be the blow-up of
X
at
x_{0}
. Then
X^{\prime}
is smooth,
\operatorname{Exc}(f)
is a smooth quadric, and
f
is a divisorial,
K
-negative elementary contraction of type
(3,0)^{Q}
; in particular
x_{0}
is a locally factorial, terminal singularity.

Let
A\subset X
be a smooth irreducible surface containing
x_{0}
, and let
g\colon X^{\prime\prime}\to X
be the blow-up of
A
. Then
X^{\prime\prime}
is smooth and
g
is a divisorial,
K
-negative elementary contraction of type
(3,2)
; we have
g^{-1}(x_{0})\cong\mathbb{P}^{2}
while the other non-trivial fibers of
g
are
\mathbb{P}^{1}
’s.

By a
family of curves
in a projective variety
X
we mean an irreducible closed subset
V\subset\operatorname{Chow}(X)
such that for general
v\in V
, the corresponding cycle
C_{v}
of
X
is an integral curve; then for every
v\in V
the cycle
C_{v}
is a connected curve in
X
.
The numerical equivalence class
[C_{v}]\in\mathcal{N}_{1}(X)
does not depend on
v\in V
, and we denote it by
[V]
.
The anticanonical degree of the family is
-K_{X}\cdot[V]
. We say that
V
is a family of rational curves if for general
v\in V
the curve
C_{v}
is rational. We denote by
\operatorname{Locus}(V)
the union of the curves
C_{v}
for
v\in V
, and we say that
V
is covering if
\operatorname{Locus}(V)=X
.

We work over the field of complex numbers, but we will occasionally also consider varieties defined over non-closed fields of characteristic zero, as follows. Let
X
be a complex projective variety and
f\colon X\to Y
a contraction of fiber type. The
generic fiber

X_{\eta}
of
f
is the fiber over the generic point
\eta
of
Y
, and it is a projective variety over the field
K=\mathbb{C}(\eta)=\mathbb{C}(Y)
of rational functions on
Y
. We will be interested in the case where
X_{\eta}
is a smooth del Pezzo surface;
we refer the reader to
[
Has09
]
for properties of del Pezzo surfaces over non-closed fields.

Preliminaries on Fano
4
-folds.

We finally recall some preliminary results on contractions and rational contractions of
4
-folds and Fano
4
-folds.

Lemma 2.9

(
[
AW98a
]
, Th. on p. 256;
[
AW98b
]
, Prop. 2.1)
.

Let
X
be a smooth projective
4
-fold and
f\colon X\to Y
a
K
-negative elementary contraction of type
(3,2)
. Then
Y
has at most isolated ordinary double points, in particular
Y
has locally factorial, terminal singularities, and
S:=f(\operatorname{Exc}(f))
has isolated singularities. If
y_{0}\in S
is a singular point for
Y
or for
S
, then
\dim f^{-1}(y_{0})=2
.
Moreover
f
is the blow-up of
Y
along
S
.
5
5

5

Namely the blow-up of
Y
along the ideal sheaf
\mathcal{I}_{S/Y}
;
S
is a reduced closed subscheme of
Y
that may be singular.

Lemma 2.10
.

Let
Y
be a smooth, irreducible, quasi-projective
4
-fold, and
S_{1},S_{2}\subset Y
two smooth, irreducible surfaces intersecting transversally in one point
y_{0}\in Y
. Then there is a commutative diagram:

\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tau_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\tau_{1}}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{{X_{1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma_{1}}
\textstyle{{X_{2}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma_{2}}
\textstyle{Y}

where
\sigma_{i}\colon X_{i}\to Y
is the blow-up of
S_{i}
, and
\tau_{i}\colon X\to X_{j}
is the blow-up of the transform
\widetilde{S}_{i}\subset X_{j}
of
S_{i}
, where
\{i,j\}=\{1,2\}
. The composition
f\colon X\to Y
is a
K
-negative birational contraction with
f(\operatorname{Exc}(f))=S_{1}\cup S_{2}
,
f^{-1}(y)\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}
for every
y\in(S_{1}\cup S_{2})\smallsetminus\{y_{0}\}
, and
f^{-1}(y_{0})\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
.

Proof.

Let us consider the blow-up
\sigma_{1}\colon X_{1}\to Y
of
S_{1}
. The surface
S_{2}
is blown-up at
y_{0}
, and its transform
\widetilde{S}_{2}\subset X_{1}
intersects the exceptional divisor
\operatorname{Exc}(\sigma_{1})
along a smooth irreducible curve
\Gamma
, which is the exceptional curve of the blow-up
\sigma_{1|\widetilde{S}_{2}}\colon\widetilde{S}_{2}\to S_{2}
.
We also have
\mathcal{N}_{\widetilde{S}_{2}/X_{1}}=(\sigma_{1|\widetilde{S}_{2}})^{*}\mathcal{N}_{S_{2}/Y}
, therefore

(\mathcal{N}_{\widetilde{S}_{2}/X_{1}})_{|\Gamma}\cong\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}^{\oplus 2}
.

Now let
\tau_{2}\colon X\to X_{1}
be the blow-up of
\widetilde{S}_{2}
, and
f:=\tau_{2}\circ\sigma_{1}\colon X\to Y
. We have
f^{-1}(y)\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}
and
-K_{X}\cdot f^{-1}(y)=1
for every
y\in(S_{1}\cup S_{2})\smallsetminus\{y_{0}\}
.

Moreover
f^{-1}(y_{0})=(\tau_{2})^{-1}(\Gamma)=\mathbb{P}_{\Gamma}(\mathcal{N}_{\widetilde{S}_{2}/X_{1}}^{\vee})_{|\Gamma}\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
, and
\operatorname{Exc}(\tau_{2})_{|f^{-1}(y_{0})}\cong\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}}(-1,0)
. If
C\subset f^{-1}(y_{0})
is a curve
\{\text{\it pt}\}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
with
\tau_{2}(C)=\Gamma
, then
\operatorname{Exc}(\tau_{2})\cdot C=0
and hence
-K_{X}\cdot C=-K_{Y}\cdot\Gamma=1
, so that
-K_{X|f^{-1}(y_{0})}\cong\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}}(1,1)
. In particular we see that
f
is
K
-negative, and
\operatorname{NE}(f)=\operatorname{NE}(\tau_{2})+\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}[C]
. It is not difficult to see that
C
is numerically equivalent to the transform of a general fiber of
\sigma_{1|\operatorname{Exc}(\sigma_{1})}
, and that the contraction of the extremal ray
\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}[C]
is
\tau_{1}\colon X\to X_{2}
as in the statement.
∎

Lemma 2.11
.

Let
X
be a projective
4
-fold with locally factorial and terminal singularities, and
f\colon X\to Y
a
K
-negative elementary contraction of type
(3,2)
; set
E:=\operatorname{Exc}(f)
and
S:=f(E)\subset Y
. Then

\chi(X,-K_{X})=\chi(Y,-K_{Y})-\chi(S,-K_{Y|S})
.

Proof.

We note that
Y
still has locally factorial and terminal singularities.

We have
R^{i}f_{*}\mathcal{O}_{X}(-K_{X})=0
for every
i>0

[
KM98
, Cor. 2.68]
, thus
\chi(X,-K_{X})=\chi(Y,f_{*}\mathcal{O}_{X}(-K_{X}))
.
Consider the exact sequence on
X
:

0\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{X}(-K_{X})\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{X}(-K_{X}+E)\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{X}(-K_{X}+E)\otimes\mathcal{O}_{E}\longrightarrow 0.

We have
-K_{X}+E=f^{*}(-K_{Y})
, thus using
f_{*}\mathcal{O}_{X}=\mathcal{O}_{Y}
,
R^{1}f_{*}\mathcal{O}_{X}(-K_{X})=0
, and the projection formula, via
f_{*}
we get

0\longrightarrow f_{*}\mathcal{O}_{X}(-K_{X})\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{Y}(-K_{Y})\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{Y}(-K_{Y})\otimes\mathcal{O}_{S}\longrightarrow 0,

therefore
\chi(Y,f_{*}\mathcal{O}_{X}(-K_{X}))=\chi(Y,-K_{Y})-\chi(S,-K_{Y|S})
.
∎

Theorem 2.12

(
[
Kaw00
]
, Th. 5.2;
[
Flo13
]
; Th. 1.2)
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold. Then

h^{0}(X,-K_{X})\geq 2
.

Let
X
be a normal projective
4
-fold. An
exceptional plane
is a surface
L\subset X_{\text{\it reg}}
such that
L\cong\mathbb{P}^{2}
and
\mathcal{N}_{L/X}\cong\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{2}}(-1)^{\oplus 2}
; we denote by
C_{L}\subset L
a curve corresponding to a line in
\mathbb{P}^{2}
. An
exceptional line
is a smooth rational curve
\ell\subset X_{\text{\it reg}}
such that
\mathcal{N}_{\ell/X}\cong\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(-1)^{\oplus 3}
. Note that
K_{X}\cdot C_{L}=-1
while
K_{X}\cdot\ell=1
.

Lemma 2.13

(
[
Kaw89
]
, Th. 1.1)
.

Let
X
be a smooth projective
4
-fold and
f\colon X\to Y
a small elementary
K
-negative contraction. Then
\operatorname{Exc}(f)
is a finite disjoint union of exceptional planes.

Lemma 2.14

(
[
Cas13a
]
, Rem. 3.6)
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold and
\varphi\colon X\dasharrow X^{\prime}
a SQM.
We have the following:

(a)

X^{\prime}
is smooth,
X\smallsetminus\operatorname{dom}(\varphi)=L_{1}\cup\cdots\cup L_{r}
where
L_{i}
are pairwise disjoint exceptional planes,
and

X^{\prime}\smallsetminus\operatorname{dom}(\varphi^{-1})=\ell_{1}\cup\cdots\cup\ell_{r}

where
\ell_{i}
are pairwise disjoint exceptional lines.

(b)

Let
C\subset X^{\prime}
be an irreducible curve, different from
\ell_{1},\dotsc,\ell_{r}
, and intersecting
\ell_{1}\cup\cdots\cup\ell_{r}
in
s\geq 0
points; then
-K_{X^{\prime}}\cdot C\geq 1+s
.

(c)

If
-K_{X^{\prime}}\cdot C=1
, then
C\cap(\ell_{1}\cup\cdots\cup\ell_{r})=\emptyset
.

Lemma 2.15

(
[
Cas13a
]
, Rem. 3.7)
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold and
f\colon X\dasharrow Y
a rational contraction. Then there exists a resolution
f^{\prime}\colon X^{\prime}\to Y

of
f
such that
f^{\prime}
is
K
-negative.

Lemma 2.16
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold and
g\colon X\to Z
a contraction of fiber type. Then
\rho_{Z}\leq\delta_{X}+2
.

Proof.

The proof of
[
Cas24
, Lemma 2.6]
gives the statement.
∎

3.
Preliminaries on rational contractions of fiber type

In this section we give many preliminary results on regular and rational contractions of fiber type of Mori dream spaces, that will be used throughout the paper.

Lemma 3.1

(
[
Cas13a
]
, Lemma 2.21)
.

Let
X
be a projective, normal, and
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial Mori dream space,
f\colon X\to Y
a contraction of fiber type, and set
F_{y}:=f^{-1}(y)
.

Then there is a non-empty open subset
Y_{0}\subset Y
such that the linear subspace
\mathcal{N}_{1}(F_{y},X)\subset\mathcal{N}_{1}(X)
is constant for
y\in Y_{0}
.
Moreover, for
y\in Y_{0}
,

\mathcal{N}_{1}(F_{y},X)^{\perp}\cap\operatorname{Eff}(X)

is a face of
\operatorname{Eff}(X)
, has dimension
\dim(\mathcal{N}_{1}(F_{y},X)^{\perp})=\rho_{X}-\dim\mathcal{N}_{1}(F_{y},X)
, and is the smallest face of
\operatorname{Eff}(X)
containing
f^{*}(\operatorname{Eff}(Y))
.

Definition - Remark 3.2

(
\bm{\tau_{f},d_{f}}
)
.

Let
X
be a projective, normal, and
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial Mori dream space, and
f\colon X\dasharrow Y
a rational contraction of fiber type. We denote by

\tau_{f}
the minimal face of
\operatorname{Eff}(X)
containing
f^{*}(\operatorname{Eff}(Y))
,
6
6

6

Equivalently,
\tau_{f}
is the minimal face of
\operatorname{Eff}(X)
containing
f^{*}(\operatorname{Nef}(Y))
.
and we set:

d_{f}:=\rho_{X}-\dim\tau_{f}.

We will use the following properties:

\bullet

for every resolution

f^{\prime}\colon X^{\prime}\to Y
of
f
, if
F\subset X^{\prime}
is a general fiber of
f^{\prime}
, we have
\dim\mathcal{N}_{1}(F,X^{\prime})=d_{f}
(see Lemma
3.1
);

\bullet

d_{f}\leq\rho_{X}-\rho_{Y}
, in particular
d_{f}=1
when
f
is elementary;

\bullet

d_{f}=1
when
\dim X-\dim Y=1
;

\bullet

\tau_{f}\cap\operatorname{Mov}(X)
is a face of
\operatorname{Mov}(X)
(because
\operatorname{Mov}(X)\subset\operatorname{Eff}(X)
) and contains
f^{*}(\operatorname{Nef}(Y))
, therefore
\dim(\tau_{f}\cap\operatorname{Mov}(X))\geq\rho_{Y}
, and
\tau_{f}
is a movable face of
\operatorname{Eff}(X)
if
Y\neq\text{\it pt}
;

\bullet

whenever
f
is regular, or more generally regular and proper on some non-empty open subset of
X
, if
F\subset X
is a general fiber, then
\tau_{f}=\mathcal{N}_{1}(F,X)^{\perp}\cap\operatorname{Eff}(X)
.
Indeed if
\varphi\colon X\dasharrow X^{\prime}
is a SQM such that
f\circ\varphi^{-1}
is regular, then
F
must be contained in the open subset where
\varphi
is an isomorphism, hence
\mathcal{N}_{1}(F,X)\cong\mathcal{N}_{1}(\varphi(F),X^{\prime})
under the natural isomorphism
\mathcal{N}_{1}(X)\cong\mathcal{N}_{1}(X^{\prime})

(see
[
Cas20
, Lemma 2.17]
).

Lemma 3.3
.

Let
X
be a projective, normal, and
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial Mori dream space,
f\colon X\to Y
a contraction of fiber type, and
F\subset X
a general fiber.
Let
W\subset\mathcal{N}^{1}(X)
be the linear subspace of classes of
\mathbb{R}
-divisors whose support does not dominate
Y
.
Then
W=\mathcal{N}_{1}(F,X)^{\perp}
.

Proof.

Let
Y_{0}\subset Y
be an open subset as in Lemma
3.1
, so that we can assume that
\mathcal{N}_{1}(F,X)=\mathcal{N}_{1}(F_{y},X)
for every
y\in Y_{0}
.

Let
D
be a divisor whose support does not dominate
Y
. Then there is
y\in Y_{0}
such that
(\operatorname{Supp}D)\cap F_{y}=\emptyset
, hence
D\cdot C=0
for every curve
C\subset F_{y}
, and
[D]\in\mathcal{N}_{1}(F_{y},X)^{\perp}
. This shows that
W\subseteq\mathcal{N}_{1}(F,X)^{\perp}
.

By Lemma
3.1

\mathcal{N}_{1}(F,X)^{\perp}\cap\operatorname{Eff}(X)
is a face of
\operatorname{Eff}(X)
of dimension equal to
\dim(\mathcal{N}_{1}(F,X)^{\perp})
, therefore
\mathcal{N}_{1}(F,X)^{\perp}
is generated by the one-dimensional faces of
\operatorname{Eff}(X)
contained in it.
On the other hand every one-dimensional face of
\operatorname{Eff}(X)
is generated by the class of some prime divisor, and we conclude that
\mathcal{N}_{1}(F,X)^{\perp}
can be generated by classes of prime divisors.

Now let
D\subset X
be a prime divisor such that
[D]\in\mathcal{N}_{1}(F,X)^{\perp}
. Then
D
cannot dominate
Y
, otherwise we could choose
y\in Y_{0}
such that
F_{y}\not\subset D
and
F_{y}\cap D\neq\emptyset
. Thus we could find a curve
C\subset F_{y}
such that
D\cdot C>0
, a contradiction. Therefore
[D]\in W
, and we conclude that
\mathcal{N}_{1}(F,X)^{\perp}\subseteq W
and finally
\mathcal{N}_{1}(F,X)^{\perp}=W
.
∎

Remark 3.4
.

In the setting of Def.-Rem.
3.2
, it follows from Lemmas
3.1
and
3.3
that

\tau_{f}=\langle[D]\,|\,\text{$D$ is effective and $\operatorname{Supp}D$ does not dominate $Y$}\rangle.

Lemma 3.5
.

Let
X
be a projective, normal, and
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial Mori dream space,
f\colon X\to Y
a contraction of fiber type, and
F\subset X
a general fiber. Let also
\eta
be the generic point of
Y
and
X_{\eta}
the generic fiber of
f
. Then
\rho_{X_{\eta}}=\dim\mathcal{N}_{1}(F,X)=d_{f}
.

Proof.

Recall that since
X
is a Mori dream space,

\operatorname{Pic}(X)
is finitely generated, and
\mathcal{N}^{1}(X)\cong\operatorname{Pic}(X)\otimes\mathbb{R}
.

Consider the restriction
r\colon\operatorname{Pic}(X)\to\operatorname{Pic}(X_{\eta})
and the induced homomorphism
r_{\mathbb{R}}\colon\operatorname{Pic}(X)\otimes\mathbb{R}\to\operatorname{Pic}(X_{\eta})\otimes\mathbb{R}
.
Since
X
is
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial,
r_{\mathbb{R}}
is surjective. Moreover
\ker r_{\mathbb{R}}
coincides with the linear subspace
W\subset\mathcal{N}^{1}(X)
of classes of
\mathbb{R}
-divisors whose support does not dominate
Y
. Therefore by Lemma
3.3
we have
\dim\ker r_{\mathbb{R}}=\rho_{X}-\dim\mathcal{N}_{1}(F,X)
, which yields the statement.
∎

Lemma 3.6
.

Let
X
be a normal quasi-projective variety
and

f\colon X\to Y
a contraction of fiber type. Let
\eta
be the generic point of
Y
,
X_{\eta}
the generic fiber of
f
,
Z_{\eta}
a normal projective variety over
K=\mathbb{C}(\eta)
, and
g_{\eta}\colon X_{\eta}\to Z_{\eta}
a projective morphism over
K
such that
(g_{\eta})_{*}\mathcal{O}_{X_{\eta}}=\mathcal{O}_{Z_{\eta}}
.

Then there exists an open subset
Y_{0}
of
\,Y
such that
f_{|f^{-1}(Y_{0})}\colon f^{-1}(Y_{0})\to Y_{0}
factors as the composition of two contractions
f^{-1}(Y_{0})\to Z_{0}\to Y_{0}
that extend
X_{\eta}\to Z_{\eta}\to\eta
.

Proof.

We apply the results on spreading out schemes and morphisms. By
[
Poo17
, Th. 3.2.1(i) and (ii)]
, there exist an open subset
Y_{0}
of
Y
and a quasi-projective variety
Z_{0}
, with a surjective projective morphism
h\colon Z_{0}\to Y_{0}
, that extend
Z_{\eta}\to\eta
.

Consider the
normalization
\nu\colon Z_{0}^{\nu}\to Z_{0}
; restricting to the generic fibers
we get
\nu_{\eta}\colon(Z_{0}^{\nu})_{\eta}\to Z_{\eta}
which is the normalization of
Z_{\eta}
. Since
Z_{\eta}
is normal,
\nu
is an isomorphism on the generic fibers, and up to composing with
\nu
we can assume that
Z_{0}
is normal.

Note that
(g_{\eta})_{*}\mathcal{O}_{X_{\eta}}=\mathcal{O}_{Z_{\eta}}
implies that every fiber of
g_{\eta}
is geometrically connected
[
Liu02
, Ch. 5, Th. 3.15 and Cor. 3.17]
.
Then by
[
Poo17
, Th. 3.2.1(iii) and (iv)]
, up to shrinking
Y_{0}
, we can assume that there exists a projective morphism
g\colon f^{-1}(Y_{0})\to Z_{0}
, with connected fibers, extending
g_{\eta}
, so that
f_{|f^{-1}(Y_{0})}=h\circ g
.
∎

Lemma 3.7
.

Let
X
be a projective, normal, and
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial Mori dream space. Let
X_{0}\subset X
be an open subset and
f_{0}\colon X_{0}\to Y_{0}
a contraction. Then there exists a rational contraction
f\colon X\dasharrow Y
such that
Y
contains
Y_{0}
as an open subset and
f_{|X_{0}}=f_{0}
.

Proof.

Let
Y_{0}\subset\mathbb{P}^{N}
be an embedding as locally closed subset. Then
f_{0}
gives a rational map
F_{0}\colon X\dasharrow\mathbb{P}^{N}
with
B:=X\smallsetminus\operatorname{dom}(F_{0})
closed subset of codimension
\geq 2
.

Let
H\subset\mathbb{P}^{N}
be a general hyperplane and consider the divisor
F_{0}^{*}(H)
on
X\smallsetminus B
.
Then
F_{0}^{*}(H)
extends uniquely to a Weil divisor on
X
, that we still denote by
F_{0}^{*}(H)
. Since
X
is
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial, there exists
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
such that
D:=mF_{0}^{*}(H)
is Cartier; then
D
is also movable, because its base locus is contained in
B
. Moreover, since
X
is a Mori dream space, up to taking a possibly larger
m
we can assume that the complete linear system
|D|
defines a rational map
F\colon X\dasharrow\mathbb{P}^{M}=\mathbb{P}(H^{0}(X,D))
such that, if
Y:=\overline{F(\operatorname{dom}(F))}\subset\mathbb{P}^{M}
, the induced map
f\colon X\dasharrow Y
is a rational contraction. Then
Y
contains
Y_{0}
as an open subset and
f_{|X_{0}}=f_{0}
.
∎

We will work with two particular types of rational contractions of fiber type, namely “quasi-elementary” and “special” contractions; let us recall the definition and a few properties, and refer the reader to
[
Cas13a
, §2.2]
and
[
Cas20
, §2]
respectively for more details.

Definition 3.8
.

Let
f\colon X\to Y
be a contraction of fiber type.

We say that
f
is
quasi-elementary
if
\dim\mathcal{N}_{1}(F,X)=\rho_{X}-\rho_{Y}
for every fiber
F\subset X
of
f
, namely if

d_{f}=\rho_{X}-\rho_{Y}
.

We say that
f
is
special
if
Y
is
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial and
\operatorname{codim}f(D)\leq 1
for every prime divisor
D\subset X
.

An elementary contraction of fiber type is always quasi-elementary.
A quasi-elementary contraction is always special, by the following.

Lemma 3.9

(
[
Cas20
]
, Prop. 2.2)
.

Let
X
be a projective, normal, and
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial Mori dream space and
f\colon X\to Y
a contraction of fiber type. Then
f
is quasi-elementary if and only if
Y
is
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial and
f^{*}(B)
is an irreducible (possibly non-reduced) divisor for every prime divisor
B\subset Y
.

If
Y
is a curve, then every contraction of fiber type
f\colon X\to Y
is special. If
Y
is a surface, then
f
is special if and only if it is
equidimensional

[
Cas20
, Lemma 2.7]
.

Consider now a
rational
contraction of fiber type
f\colon X\dasharrow Y
. We say that
f
is quasi-elementary, respectively special, if a resolution
of
f
is quasi-elementary, respectively special; this does not depend on the choice of the resolution.

Lemma 3.10
.

Let
X
be a projective, normal, and
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial Mori dream space and
f\colon X\dasharrow Y
a rational contraction. The following are equivalent:

(i)

f
is of fiber type and special;

(ii)

f
is of fiber type and
\dim(\tau_{f}\cap\operatorname{Mov}(X))=\rho_{Y}
(see Def.-Rem.
3.2
);

(iii)

there exists a proper face
\eta_{f}
of
\operatorname{Mov}(X)
such that

f^{*}(\operatorname{Nef}(Y))\subset\eta_{f}
,
\dim\eta_{f}=\rho_{Y}
, and
\eta_{f}=\tau\cap\operatorname{Mov}(X)
for some face
\tau
of
\operatorname{Eff}(X)
.

If these conditions hold and moreover
f
is regular with general fiber
F\subset X
, then
\eta_{f}=(\mathcal{N}_{1}(F,X))^{\perp}\cap\operatorname{Mov}(X)
.

Proof.

The equivalence of
(i)
and
(ii)
is
[
Cas22
, Lemma 5.3]
.

Assume
(ii)
. Note that
\eta_{f}:=\tau_{f}\cap\operatorname{Mov}(X)
is a face of
\operatorname{Mov}(X)
because
\operatorname{Mov}(X)\subset\operatorname{Eff}(X)
, it is proper because
\tau_{f}\subsetneq\operatorname{Eff}(X)
, it contains
f^{*}(\operatorname{Nef}(Y))
, and
\dim\eta_{f}=\rho_{Y}
, hence we get
(iii)
.

Conversely, assume
(iii)
. Since
\eta_{f}\subsetneq\operatorname{Mov}(X)
, we have
\tau\subsetneq\operatorname{Eff}(X)
, hence
f^{*}(\operatorname{Nef}(Y))\subset\partial\operatorname{Eff}(X)
, and
f
is of fiber type.
By definition
\tau_{f}
is the minimal face of
\operatorname{Eff}(X)
containing
f^{*}(\operatorname{Nef}(Y))
, therefore
\tau_{f}\subset\tau
, and
\tau_{f}\cap\operatorname{Mov}(X)=\tau\cap\operatorname{Mov}(X)=\eta_{f}
has dimension
\rho_{Y}
, and we get
(ii)
.

Finally, when
f
is regular,
\eta_{f}=\tau_{f}\cap\operatorname{Mov}(X)=(\mathcal{N}_{1}(F,X))^{\perp}\cap\operatorname{Mov}(X)
by Lemma
3.1
.
∎

Remark 3.11
.

Let
X
be a projective, normal, and
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial Mori dream space, and
\tau
a proper, movable face of
\operatorname{Eff}(X)
. Then there exists a special rational contraction of fiber type
f\colon X\dasharrow Y
with
\eta_{f}=\tau\cap\operatorname{Mov}(X)
and
\tau_{f}\subset\tau
, hence
\rho_{Y}=\dim(\tau\cap\operatorname{Mov}(X))
and
d_{f}=\rho_{X}-\dim\tau_{f}\geq\rho_{X}-\dim\tau
(notation as in Lemma
3.10
and Def.-Rem.
3.2
).

Indeed it is enough to choose a cone
\eta_{0}\in\operatorname{MCD}(X)
such that
\eta_{0}\subset\tau\cap\operatorname{Mov}(X)
and
\dim\eta_{0}=\dim(\tau\cap\operatorname{Mov}(X))
; then the rational contraction
f\colon X\dasharrow Y
such that
f^{*}(\operatorname{Nef}(Y))=\eta_{0}

has the desired properties, by Lemma
3.10
.

Proposition 3.12

(
[
Cas20
]
, Prop. 2.13)
.

Let
X
be a projective, normal, and
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial Mori dream space and
f\colon X\dasharrow Y
a rational contraction of fiber type.
Then
f
can be factored as
X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle g}}{{\dasharrow}}Z\stackrel{{\scriptstyle h}}{{\to}}Y
where
h
is birational and
g
is a special rational contraction of fiber type.

Lemma 3.13
.

Let
X
be a projective, normal, and
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial Mori dream space and
f\colon X\dasharrow Y
a rational contraction of fiber type that
is regular and proper on some non-empty open subset of
X
.

Then
f
can be factored as
X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle g}}{{\dasharrow}}Z\stackrel{{\scriptstyle h}}{{\to}}Y
where
h
is birational and
g
is a special rational contraction of fiber type
that is regular and proper on some non-empty open subset of
X
. In particular
f
and
g
have isomorphic general fibers.

Proof.

This follows from the same proof as
[
Cas20
, Prop. 2.13]
.
∎

Lemma 3.14
.

Let
X
be a projective, normal, and
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial Mori dream space and
f\colon X\to Y
a contraction of fiber type. Suppose that there exist a non-empty open subset
Y_{0}\subset Y
and a contraction of fiber type
g_{0}\colon f^{-1}(Y_{0})\to Z_{0}
such that
f_{|f^{-1}(Y_{0})}
factors through
g_{0}
:

\textstyle{{f^{-1}(Y_{0})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hskip 8.19447ptg_{0}}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{{Z_{0}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{Y_{0}}}

Then
f
can be factored as
X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle g}}{{\dasharrow}}Z\stackrel{{\scriptstyle h}}{{\to}}Y
, where
h
is a contraction, and
g
is
a special rational contraction that coincides with
g_{0}
on

g_{0}^{-1}(U)
for
some non-empty open subset
U
of
Z_{0}
.

Proof.

By Lemma
3.7
there exists a rational contraction
g^{\prime}\colon X\dasharrow Z^{\prime}
that restricts to
g_{0}
on
f^{-1}(Y_{0})
, in particular
g^{\prime}
is of fiber type and is regular and proper on
f^{-1}(Y_{0})
.

Now we apply Lemma
3.13
to
g^{\prime}
, and deduce that

g^{\prime}
factors as
X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle g^{\prime\prime}}}{{\dasharrow}}Z^{\prime\prime}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\alpha}}{{\to}}Z^{\prime}
where

\alpha
is birational and
g^{\prime\prime}
is a special rational contraction of fiber type that is regular and proper on some non-empty open subset of
X
. In particular
g^{\prime\prime}
has the same general fiber as
g_{0}
.

Let us consider
F_{f},F_{g}\subset X
general fibers of
f
and
g_{0}
respectively; we have
F_{g}\subset F_{f}
, so
\mathcal{N}_{1}(F_{g},X)\subset\mathcal{N}_{1}(F_{f},X)
, and hence

\mathcal{N}_{1}(F_{f},X)^{\perp}\subset\mathcal{N}_{1}(F_{g},X)^{\perp}\subset\mathcal{N}^{1}(X)
.

Let us set:

\eta_{g^{\prime\prime}}:=\mathcal{N}_{1}(F_{g},X)^{\perp}\cap\operatorname{Mov}(X)=\tau_{g^{\prime\prime}}\cap\operatorname{Mov}(X)

(see Def.-Rem.
3.2
), so that

\eta_{g^{\prime\prime}}
is a face of
\operatorname{Mov}(X)
, and it has dimension

\rho_{Z^{\prime\prime}}
because
g^{\prime\prime}
is special, by Lemma
3.10
.

We note that
\eta_{g^{\prime\prime}}
is a union of cones of
\operatorname{MCD}(X)
of dimension
\rho_{Z^{\prime\prime}}
, one being
(g^{\prime\prime})^{*}(\operatorname{Nef}(Z^{\prime\prime}))
.

The cone
f^{*}(\operatorname{Nef}(Y))
belongs to the fan
\operatorname{MCD}(X)
and is contained both in
\operatorname{Mov}(X)
and in
\mathcal{N}_{1}(F_{f},X)^{\perp}
, therefore
in
\eta_{g^{\prime\prime}}
. Hence there exists a cone
\eta\in\operatorname{MCD}(X)
of dimension
\rho_{Z^{\prime\prime}}
such that
f^{*}(\operatorname{Nef}(Y))\subseteq\eta\subseteq\eta_{g^{\prime\prime}}
.

Let
g\colon X\dasharrow Z
be the rational contraction of fiber type such that
\eta=g^{*}(\operatorname{Nef}(Z))
. Then
\rho_{Z}=\rho_{Z^{\prime\prime}}
and
there is a SQM
\psi\colon Z^{\prime\prime}\dasharrow Z
such that
g=\psi\circ g^{\prime\prime}

(see
[
Oka16
, Th. 1.2]
),
in particular
g
and
g^{\prime\prime}
have the same general fiber
F_{g}
.
Then
g
is special again by Lemma
3.10
, and it
coincides with
g_{0}
on

g_{0}^{-1}(U)
for
some non-empty open subset
U
of
Z_{0}
.

Finally
f^{*}(\operatorname{Nef}(Y))\subset g^{*}(\operatorname{Nef}(Z))
, therefore there exists a contraction
h\colon Z\to Y
such that
f=h\circ g
(see Rem.
2.2
).
∎

Lemma 3.15
.

Let
X
be a projective, normal, and
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial Mori dream space, and
f\colon X\dasharrow Y
a rational contraction of fiber type that factors as
X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle g}}{{\dasharrow}}Z\stackrel{{\scriptstyle h}}{{\dasharrow}}Y
where
g
and
h
are rational contractions,
h
is of fiber type, and
Z
is
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial.

If
f
is special (respectively, quasi-elementary), then
h
is special (respectively, quasi-elementary).

Proof.

Up to composing with SQM’s, we can assume that
f
,
g
, and
h
are regular.

Suppose that
f
is special; in particular
Y
is
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial. Let
D\subset Z
be a prime divisor such that
h(D)\subsetneq Y
.
Then
g^{-1}(D)
has pure codimension one in
X
and
g(g^{-1}(D))=D
, so there is an irreducible component

D_{X}
of
g^{-1}(D)
such that
g(D_{X})=D
. Since
f
is special,
h(D)=f(D_{X})
must be a prime divisor in
Y
, so that
h
is special.

Suppose now that
f
is quasi-elementary. We use Lemma
3.9
:
first of all
Y
is
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial. Let
B\subset Y
be a prime divisor. Then
f^{*}(B)=g^{*}(h^{*}(B))
is irreducible (possibly non-reduced), therefore
h^{*}(B)
must have a unique irreducible component, and
h
is quasi-elementary too.
∎

Lemma 3.16
.

Let
X
be a projective, normal, and
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial Mori dream space, and
f\colon X\dasharrow Y
a rational contraction of fiber type. We have the following:

(a)

f
factors as
X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle g}}{{\dasharrow}}Z\stackrel{{\scriptstyle h}}{{\to}}Y

where
h
is a contraction and
g
is an elementary rational contraction, either divisorial or of fiber type;

(b)

if
f
is special, then
h
is of fiber type or an isomorphism;

(c)

when
g
is divisorial:

\bullet

if
f
is special, then
\operatorname{Exc}(g)
dominates
Y
or a prime divisor in
Y
;

\bullet

if
f
is quasi-elementary, then
\operatorname{Exc}(g)
dominates
Y
.

Proof.

Consider the cone
f^{*}(\operatorname{Nef}(Y))\in\operatorname{MCD}(X)
.
Since
f
is of fiber type,
f^{*}(\operatorname{Nef}(Y))
is contained in the boundary of
\operatorname{Eff}(X)
, hence also in the boundary of
\operatorname{Mov}(X)
.

Let us
consider a facet
\eta
of
\operatorname{Mov}(X)
containing
f^{*}(\operatorname{Nef}(Y))
,
and

\eta_{0}\in\operatorname{MCD}(X)
of dimension
\rho_{X}-1
such that
f^{*}(\operatorname{Nef}(Y))\subset\eta_{0}\subset\eta
.
Then
\eta_{0}=g^{*}(\operatorname{Nef}(Z))
for an elementary rational contraction
g\colon X\dasharrow Z
and we have a factorization:

\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{{Z}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{h}
\textstyle{Y}

where
h
is a contraction (see Rem.
2.2
).
By construction
\eta_{0}
is contained in the boundary of
\operatorname{Mov}(X)
, therefore
g
cannot be small. This gives
(a)
, and
(b)
follows from
[
Cas20
, Prop. 2.10]
.

For
(c)
, suppose that
g
is divisorial. The first statement follows from the definition of special contraction. For the second statement,
up to composing with a SQM we can assume that
g
is regular; set
E:=\operatorname{Exc}(g)\subset X
, and let
F\subset X
be a general fiber of
f
.
If
f(E)\subsetneq Y
, then
F\cap E=\emptyset
, thus
\mathcal{N}_{1}(F,X)\subset E^{\perp}
(see Rem.
2.7
). We also have
E\cdot\operatorname{NE}(g)<0
, thus
\operatorname{NE}(g)\not\subset E^{\perp}
, in particular
\operatorname{NE}(g)\not\subset\mathcal{N}_{1}(F,X)
. On the other hand
\operatorname{NE}(g)\subset\ker f_{*}
, and we conclude that
\mathcal{N}_{1}(F,X)\subsetneq\ker f_{*}
and
f
cannot be quasi-elementary.
∎

Remark 3.17
.

In the setting of Lemma
3.16
, it follows from the proof
that the choices for
g
correspond to the facets of
\operatorname{Mov}(X)
that contain
f^{*}(\operatorname{Nef}(Y))
. More precisely, given a facet
\eta
of
\operatorname{Mov}(X)
containing

f^{*}(\operatorname{Nef}(Y))
, we costruct
g\colon X\dasharrow Z
as in the statement, with moreover
\eta=\operatorname{Mov}(X)\cap g^{*}(\mathcal{N}^{1}(Z))
. Then
g
is of fiber type if and only if
\eta
is contained in the boundary of
\operatorname{Eff}(X)
, otherwise
g
is divisorial.

Lemma 3.18
.

Let
X
be a projective, normal, and
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial Mori dream space of dimension
n
, and
f\colon X\dasharrow Y
a quasi-elementary rational contraction
with
\dim Y=n-2
and
\rho_{X}-\rho_{Y}=2
.
Then one of the following holds:

(i)

f
factors as
X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle g}}{{\dasharrow}}Z\stackrel{{\scriptstyle h}}{{\to}}Y
where
\dim Z=n-1
and
g
and
h
are elementary of fiber type;

(ii)

f
admits two factorizations

X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\alpha_{i}}}{{\dasharrow}}W_{i}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle h_{i}}}{{\to}}Y
for
i=1,2
,
where
\alpha_{i}
is elementary and divisorial with exceptional locus dominating
Y
,
h_{i}
is elementary of fiber type, and
\alpha_{1}^{*}(\mathcal{N}^{1}(W_{1}))\neq\alpha_{2}^{*}(\mathcal{N}^{1}(W_{2}))
.

Proof.

We note that since
f
is quasi-elementary, the cone
f^{*}(\operatorname{Nef}(Y))
has dimension
\rho_{X}-2
and is contained in a face of
\operatorname{Mov}(X)
of dimension
\rho_{X}-2
(see Lemma
3.10
). Therefore

f^{*}(\operatorname{Nef}(Y))
is contained
in exactly two facets
\tau_{1}
and
\tau_{2}
of

\operatorname{Mov}(X)
.

If one of these facets is contained in
\partial\operatorname{Eff}(X)
, then by applying Lemma
3.16
and Rem.
3.17
we factor
f
as in
(i)
.
Otherwise, if
no
\tau_{i}
is contained in

\partial\operatorname{Eff}(X)
, for
i=1,2
again by applying Lemma
3.16
and Rem.
3.17

we get a factorization of
f
as

X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\alpha_{i}}}{{\dasharrow}}W_{i}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle h_{i}}}{{\to}}Y,

where
\alpha_{i}
is a divisorial elementary rational contraction
with exceptional locus dominating
Y
, and
h_{i}
is elementary.
Moreover
\alpha_{1}^{*}(\mathcal{N}^{1}(W_{1}))\neq\alpha_{2}^{*}(\mathcal{N}^{1}(W_{2}))
because

\tau_{1}\neq\tau_{2}
.
∎

We conclude this section by recalling two results on special or quasi-elementary rational contractions of Fano
4
-folds, that are needed in the sequel.

Lemma 3.19

(
[
Cas20
]
, Lemma 4.3)
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold and
f\colon X\dasharrow S
a special rational contraction onto a surface. Then
S
is smooth.

Lemma 3.20

(
[
Cas22
]
, Lemma 5.9)
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{X}\geq 7
and
f\colon X\dasharrow S
a quasi-elementary rational contraction onto a surface. Then
S
is a smooth del Pezzo surface.

4.
Factoring rational contractions of fiber type of Fano
4
-folds through
3
-folds

In this section we consider rational contractions of fiber type
f\colon X\dasharrow Y
of Fano
4
-folds.
When
\dim Y=3
, the results in
[
CS24
]
give a good understanding of
X
and
f
, see Th.
1.5
.
We consider here the case

\dim Y\in\{1,2\}
. We show that when
d_{f}\geq 5
, then
f
always factors through a
3
-fold (Th.
1.6
from the Introduction, see Th.
4.1
below).
This has in turn important applications on the geometry of Fano
4
-folds with
\rho_{X}\geq 7
, given in Th.
4.5
and Cor.
4.10
.

Theorem 4.1
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold and
f\colon X\dasharrow Y
a non-trivial rational contraction of fiber type.

(a)

If
d_{f}\geq 5
, then

f
can be factored as
X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle g}}{{\dasharrow}}Z\stackrel{{\scriptstyle h}}{{\to}}Y
where
\dim Z=3
,
g
is a special rational contraction, and
h
is a contraction of fiber type.

(b)

If
d_{f}=4
and
\dim Y=2
, then either the statement in
(a)
holds, or

f
is regular with general fiber a del Pezzo surface of degree one.

As explained in the Introduction, for the proof of Th.
4.1
we will use different strategies depending on the dimension of the base
Y
.
We recall that
d_{f}=1
when
\dim Y=3
, therefore
d_{f}\geq 4
implies that either
\dim Y=2
or
Y\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}
.
For the case
\dim Y=2
, we will consider the generic fiber of a
K
-negative resolution
f^{\prime}
of
f
, and rely on Lemma
4.2
; for the case
Y\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}
, we will use Prop.
4.4
, that exploits the monodromy action on the nef cone of a general fiber of
f^{\prime}
.

Lemma 4.2
.

Let
K
be a field of characteristic zero and let
S
be a smooth, projective del Pezzo surface over
K
.
If
\rho_{S}\geq 4
, then one of the following holds:

(i)

there exists a surjective morphism
S\to C
onto a curve;

(ii)

\rho_{S}=4
and
K_{S}^{2}=1
.

Proof.

We assume that
S
has no surjective morphism to a curve, and show
(ii)
.

There exists a birational map
S\to S_{0}
where
S_{0}
is a minimal del Pezzo surface over
K
. By our assumptions
S_{0}
does not have a conic bundle structure, hence
\rho_{S_{0}}=1
(see
[
Has09
, Th. 3.9]
). Among the (finitely many) possible birational maps
S\to S_{0}
with
\rho_{S_{0}}=1
, let us choose one with
K_{S_{0}}^{2}
maximal. Note that
K_{S_{0}}^{2}\neq 7
(see [
ibid.
, Ex. 3.1.2]).

The map
S\to S_{0}
is the blow-up of
r
distinct (closed) points, of degrees
d_{1},\dotsc,d_{r}
;
7
7

7

The degree of a closed point
p
is dimension of its residue field
K(p)
over the base field
K
.
we have

(4.3)

4\leq\rho_{S}=1+r\hskip 10.00002pt\text{ and }\hskip 10.00002ptr\leq\sum_{i=1}^{r}d_{i}=K_{S_{0}}^{2}-K_{S}^{2}\leq K_{S_{0}}^{2}-1,

in particular
r\geq 3
and
K_{S_{0}}^{2}\geq 4
.

Let
i\in\{1,\dotsc,r\}
and let us consider
S_{1}:=\operatorname{Bl}_{p_{i}}S_{0}
. Then
S_{1}
is a del Pezzo surface with
\rho_{S_{1}}=2
, and
S_{1}
does not have a morphism onto a curve, therefore there is another birational map
S_{1}\to S_{0}^{\prime}
which blows-up a point of degree
d_{i}^{\prime}
. We have
K_{S_{1}}^{2}=K_{S_{0}}^{2}-d_{i}=K_{S_{0}^{\prime}}^{2}-d_{i}^{\prime}
, and
K_{S_{0}}^{2}\geq K_{S_{0}^{\prime}}^{2}

by the maximality of
K_{S_{0}}^{2}
, hence
d_{i}\geq d_{i}^{\prime}
.

The birational map

S_{0}\longleftarrow S_{1}\longrightarrow S_{0}^{\prime}

is called an elementary link of type II, see
[
Isk96
, (2.2.2)]
, and these links are classified in [
ibid.
, Th. 2.6(ii)].
It follows from this classification and from
d_{i}\geq d_{i}^{\prime}

that
d_{i}\geq 2
if
K_{S_{0}}^{2}\in\{4,6,9\}
, and
d_{i}\geq 3
if
K_{S_{0}}^{2}\in\{5,8\}
. By (
4.3
) this implies that
K_{S_{0}}^{2}=9
, therefore
(S_{0})_{\bar{K}}\cong\mathbb{P}^{2}_{\bar{K}}
, where
\bar{K}
is the algebraic closure of
K
and
(S_{0})_{\bar{K}}=S_{0}\times_{\operatorname{Spec}K}\operatorname{Spec}{\bar{K}}
. Let
\pi\colon\mathbb{P}^{2}_{\bar{K}}\to S_{0}
be the projection.

We show that there is at most one index
i\in\{1,\dotsc,r\}
such that
d_{i}=2
.
Assume that this is not the case, for instance that
d_{1}=d_{2}=2
. This means that, for
i=1,2
,

\pi^{-1}(p_{i})=\{p_{i1},p_{i2}\}\subset\mathbb{P}^{2}_{\bar{K}}
is an orbit for the action of the Galois group
\text{Gal}(\bar{K}/K)
. Then the line
\ell_{i}:=\overline{p_{i1}p_{i2}}\subset\mathbb{P}^{2}_{\bar{K}}
is fixed by the action of the Galois group, hence
\ell_{i}=\pi^{*}(\ell_{i,K})
where
\ell_{i,K}\subset S_{0}
is a curve, and
H^{0}(S_{0},\ell_{1,K}+\ell_{2,K})\cong H^{0}(\mathbb{P}^{2}_{\bar{K}},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{2}_{\bar{K}}}(2))
.
In the linear system
|\ell_{1,K}+\ell_{2,K}|
, the pencil of curves
containing
p_{1}
and
p_{2}
defines a map
\operatorname{Bl}_{p_{1},p_{2}}S_{0}\to\mathbb{P}^{1}_{K}
, against our assumptions.

Therefore there is at most one
i
such that
d_{i}=2
, and
d_{j}\geq 3
for every
j\neq i
.
Again by (
4.3
) we get
8\leq 2+3(r-1)\leq\sum_{i}d_{i}=9-K_{S}^{2}\leq 8
, and we conclude that
r=3
,
\rho_{S}=4
,
(d_{1},d_{2},d_{3})=(2,3,3)
up to order, and
K_{S}^{2}=1
.
∎

Proof of Th.
4.1
in the case
\dim Y=2
.

Let
\varphi\colon X\dasharrow X^{\prime}
be a SQM such that
f^{\prime}:=f\circ\varphi^{-1}\colon X^{\prime}\to Y
is a
K
-negative resolution of
f
, and
F\subset X^{\prime}
a general fiber of
f^{\prime}
.
Let also
\eta
be the generic point of
Y
and
X_{\eta}
the generic fiber of
f^{\prime}
; then
X_{\eta}
is a smooth del Pezzo surface over
K=\mathbb{C}(\eta)
, with
K_{X_{\eta}}^{2}=K_{F}^{2}
(by generic flatness) and
\rho_{X_{\eta}}=\dim\mathcal{N}_{1}(F,X^{\prime})=d_{f}
(by Lemma
3.5
).

If
d_{f}=\rho_{X_{\eta}}\geq 4
, then
we can apply Lemma
4.2
. If we are in case
(i)
,
there is a surjective morphism
g_{\eta}\colon X_{\eta}\to C_{\eta}
onto a curve, defined over
K
. By normalizing and taking the Stein factorization, we can assume that
C_{\eta}
is smooth and
(g_{\eta})_{*}\mathcal{O}_{X_{\eta}}=\mathcal{O}_{C_{\eta}}
.

Then by Lemma
3.6
there are a non-empty open subset
Y_{0}\subset Y
,
a quasi-projective
3
-fold
Z_{0}
with a contraction
Z_{0}\to Y_{0}
extending
C_{\eta}\to\eta
, and a contraction
g_{0}\colon(f^{\prime})^{-1}(Y_{0})\to Z_{0}
, such that
f^{\prime}_{|(f^{\prime})^{-1}(Y_{0})}
factors through
g_{0}
.
Finally using Lemma
3.14
we get
(a)
.

Suppose now that we are in case
(ii)
of Lemma
4.2
, namely
d_{f}=\rho_{X_{\eta}}=4
and
K_{X_{\eta}}^{2}=1
. Then
K_{F}^{2}=1
and
\varphi
is an isomorphism by
[
Cas22
, Lemma 5.10]
, so
f
is regular and we get
(b)
.
∎

Proposition 4.4
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold,
X\dasharrow X^{\prime}
a SQM, and
f\colon X^{\prime}\to\mathbb{P}^{1}
a
K
-negative contraction. Let
\iota\colon F\hookrightarrow X^{\prime}
be a general fiber of
f
.

If
\rho_{F}\geq 6
, or if
\rho_{F}=4,5
and
\iota_{*}\colon\mathcal{N}_{1}(F)\to\mathcal{N}_{1}(X^{\prime})
is injective,
then
f
can be factored as
X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle g}}{{\dasharrow}}Z\stackrel{{\scriptstyle h}}{{\to}}\mathbb{P}^{1}
where
\dim Z=3
,
g
is a special rational contraction, and
h
is a contraction.

Proof.

Let
Y_{0}\subseteq\mathbb{P}^{1}
be an open subset over which
f
is smooth, and set
X_{0}:=f^{-1}(Y_{0})
and
F:=f^{-1}(y)

for
y\in Y_{0}
. Note that
F
is a smooth Fano
3
-fold. We consider the monodromy action of
\pi_{1}(Y_{0},y)
on
H^{2}(F,\mathbb{R})=\mathcal{N}^{1}(F)
; the image of the restriction
\mathcal{N}^{1}(X_{0})\to\mathcal{N}^{1}(F)
is the invariant subspace
\mathcal{N}^{1}(F)^{\pi_{1}(Y_{0},y)}
, see

[
Voi02
, Th. 16.24]
.

Assume that

\rho_{F}\geq 6
. Then
F\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}\times S
where
S
is a del Pezzo surface
[
IP99
, §12.6]
; consider the projections
\pi_{1}\colon F\to\mathbb{P}^{1}
and
\pi_{2}\colon F\to S
.

Set
\tau:=\pi_{1}^{*}\operatorname{Nef}(\mathbb{P}^{1})
and
\sigma:=\pi_{2}^{*}\operatorname{Nef}(S)
. Then

\sigma
is a facet of
\operatorname{Nef}(F)
,

\tau
is a one-dimensional face of
\operatorname{Nef}(F)
, and every other one-dimensional face of
\operatorname{Nef}(F)
is contained in
\sigma
, because it corresponds to a contraction of
F
that factors through
\pi_{2}
.

Let
g\in\pi_{1}(Y_{0},y)
.
By
[
Wiś91b
,
Wiś09
]
, we have
g(\operatorname{Nef}(F))=\operatorname{Nef}(F)
, so that
g
permutes the facets of
\operatorname{Nef}(F)
; see
[
CFST16
, §2]
for an analysis of the monodromy action on the nef cone of
F
. In particular

g(\sigma)=\sigma
by
[
CFST16
, Th. 2.7]
, because
\pi_{2}
is the unique elementary contraction of fiber type of
F
, the other elementary contractions of
F
being birational. This also implies that
g(\tau)=\tau
; in particular
g
preserves the linear subspaces
\pi_{1\,}^{*}\mathcal{N}^{1}(\mathbb{P}^{1})
and
\pi_{2\,}^{*}\mathcal{N}^{1}(S)
of
\mathcal{N}^{1}(F)
.

We have
K_{F}=\pi_{1}^{*}K_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}+\pi_{2}^{*}K_{S}
, and
g(K_{F})=K_{F}
because
K_{F}=K_{X_{0}|F}
. Therefore
g
must fix also
\pi_{2}^{*}K_{S}
, so that
\pi_{2}^{*}(-K_{S})\in\mathcal{N}^{1}(F)^{\pi_{1}(Y_{0},y)}
. We conclude that there exists
H\in\operatorname{Pic}(X_{0})
such that
H_{|F}=\pi_{2}^{*}(-K_{S})
. It follows from
[
Wiś91b
, Prop. 1.3]

that
H
is relatively nef over
Y_{0}
, hence
it induces a contraction

g_{0}\colon X_{0}\to Z_{0}
such that
f_{|X_{0}}
factors through
g_{0}
and
g_{0|F}=\pi_{2}
, so that the general fiber of
g_{0}
is
\mathbb{P}^{1}
. Finally using Lemma
3.14
we get the statement.

Suppose now that

\rho_{F}=4,5
and
\iota_{*}\colon\mathcal{N}_{1}(F)\to\mathcal{N}_{1}(X^{\prime})
is injective. Then the restriction
\mathcal{N}^{1}(X^{\prime})\to\mathcal{N}^{1}(F)
is surjective, hence
\mathcal{N}^{1}(X_{0})\to\mathcal{N}^{1}(F)
is surjective too,
and the monodromy action is trivial. Thus again by
[
Wiś91b
, Prop. 1.3]

we see that every contraction of
F
extends to a relative contraction of
X_{0}
over
Y_{0}
.

Since
F
is a Fano
3
-fold with
\rho_{F}\geq 4
, by
[
IP99
, Theorem on p. 141]
it has a conic bundle onto a surface. Then
f_{|X_{0}}
factors through a contraction
g_{0}\colon X_{0}\to Z_{0}
with general fiber
\mathbb{P}^{1}
, and we conclude as above by Lemma
3.14
.
∎

Proof of Th.
4.1
in the case
Y\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}
.

Let
f^{\prime}\colon X^{\prime}\to\mathbb{P}^{1}
be a
K
-negative resolution of
f
, and
\iota\colon F\hookrightarrow X^{\prime}
a general fiber.
If
d_{f}=\dim\mathcal{N}_{1}(F,X^{\prime})\geq 5
, then either
\rho_{F}\geq 6
, or
\rho_{F}=5
and
\iota_{*}\colon\mathcal{N}_{1}(F)\to\mathcal{N}_{1}(F,X^{\prime})
is an isomorphism. Thus the statement follows from Prop.
4.4
.
∎

The following implies Th.
1.7
from the Introduction.

Theorem 4.5
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{X}\geq 7
, not isomorphic to a product of surfaces, and
f\colon X\dasharrow Y
a non-trivial rational contraction of fiber type.

Then
d_{f}\leq 4
. If moreover
d_{f}=4
and
\dim Y=2
, then
Y\cong\mathbb{P}^{2}
and
f\colon X\to\mathbb{P}^{2}
is regular and equidimensional, with general fiber a del Pezzo surface of degree one.

The bound
d_{f}\leq 4
is sharp: see
Ex.
6.2
for an example where
d_{f}=4
, with
Y=\mathbb{P}^{1}
and
\rho_{X}=7
.
Moreover the condition
\rho_{X}\geq 7
is necessary, as the following examples show.

Example 4.6
.

Let
X
be a Fano
4
-fold with
\delta_{X}=3
and
\rho_{X}=6
which is not a product of surfaces. Such
4
-folds have been classified in
[
CRS22
, §7 and Corrigendum]
, there are 9 families. Each such
X
has a quasi-elementary contraction
f\colon X\to S
where
S\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
or
S\cong\mathbb{F}_{1}
, thus
d_{f}=\rho_{X}-\rho_{S}=4
.

Example 4.7
.

Let
X
be as in Ex.
4.6
.
By composing
f\colon X\to S
with a
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-bundle
S\to\mathbb{P}^{1}
, we get a quasi-elementary contraction
f^{\prime}\colon X\to\mathbb{P}^{1}
with
d_{f^{\prime}}=\rho_{X}-1=5
.

Proof of Th.
4.5
.

We note first of all that if
\dim Y=3
, then the general fiber of
f
is
\mathbb{P}^{1}
and
d_{f}=1
, therefore we can assume that
\dim Y\in\{1,2\}
.

4.8
.

Suppose that
f
factors as
X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle g}}{{\dasharrow}}Z\stackrel{{\scriptstyle h}}{{\to}}Y
where
\dim Z=3
,
g
is a special rational contraction, and
h
is a contraction of fiber type.
Then
d_{f}\leq 3
if
\dim Y=2
and
d_{f}\leq 4
if
Y\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}
.

Proof.

We apply the results in
[
CS24
]
to
g\colon X\dasharrow Z
. First of all,
we have
\delta_{X}\leq 1
by Th.
1.9
, hence
\rho_{X}-\rho_{Z}\leq 2
by [
ibid.
, Lemma 3.12]. Moreover
Z
has the following property:
if
Z\dasharrow Z^{\prime}
is a SQM, and
Z^{\prime}\to W
is a sequence of elementary divisorial contractions, then every contraction of the sequence is of type
(2,0)
. This is shown in [
ibid.
, Lemma 5.9, 5.13, Lemma 5.15] when
\rho_{X}-\rho_{Z}=2
, and in [
ibid.
, proof of Th. 6.3] and
[
Cas13a
, §4.2]

when
g
is elementary.

Let
g^{\prime}\colon X^{\prime}\to Z
be a resolution of
g
, and note that
h\circ g^{\prime}\colon X^{\prime}\to Y
is a resolution of
f
. Let
F_{h}\subset Z
be a general fiber of
h\colon Z\to Y
, so that
F:=(g^{\prime})^{-1}(F_{h})\subset X^{\prime}
is a general fiber of
h\circ g^{\prime}
. By Def.-Rem.
3.2
and Lemma
2.6
we get
d_{f}=\dim\mathcal{N}_{1}(F,X^{\prime})\leq\rho_{X}-\rho_{Z}+\dim\mathcal{N}_{1}(F_{h},Z)\leq 2+\dim\mathcal{N}_{1}(F_{h},Z)
.

If
\dim Y=2
, then
F_{h}\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}
, hence
\dim\mathcal{N}_{1}(F_{h},Z)=1
and
d_{f}\leq 3
.

Assume that
Y\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}
. We show that
\dim\mathcal{N}_{1}(F_{h},Z)\leq 2
, which implies that
d_{f}\leq 4
.

By Lemma
3.16
we can factor
h
as
Z\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\alpha}}{{\dasharrow}}W\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\beta}}{{\to}}\mathbb{P}^{1}
where
\alpha
is an elementary rational contraction, either divisorial or of fiber type, and
\beta
is a contraction.

If
\alpha
is divisorial, we apply again Lemma
3.16
to
\beta
, and we proceed in this way until we find a factorization of
h
as:

\textstyle{{Z}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{h}
\scriptstyle{\alpha^{\prime}}
\textstyle{{W^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\beta^{\prime}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{P}^{1}}

or

\textstyle{{Z}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{h}
\scriptstyle{\alpha^{\prime}}
\textstyle{{W^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\beta^{\prime\prime}}
\textstyle{S\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\gamma}
\textstyle{\mathbb{P}^{1}}

where
\alpha^{\prime}
is a (possibly empty) sequence of elementary divisorial rational contractions,
\beta^{\prime}
and
\beta^{\prime\prime}
are elementary of fiber type,
\dim S=2
, and
\beta^{\prime}
and
\gamma
are regular.

Up to composing with SQM’s, we can assume that
\beta^{\prime\prime}
and
\alpha^{\prime}
are regular (note that
\dim\mathcal{N}_{1}(F_{h},Z)
does not vary, see Def.-Rem.
3.2
). Then
\alpha^{\prime}(F_{h})\subset W^{\prime}
is a general fiber of
\beta^{\prime}
or of
\gamma\circ\beta^{\prime\prime}
, and
\dim\mathcal{N}_{1}(\alpha^{\prime}(F_{h}),W^{\prime})\leq 2
because both contractions
\beta^{\prime}
and
\beta^{\prime\prime}
are elementary, and the general fiber of
\gamma
is
\mathbb{P}^{1}
. Moreover, since
\dim\alpha^{\prime}(\operatorname{Exc}(\alpha^{\prime}))=0
by the properties of
Z
, we have
\alpha^{\prime}(F_{h})\cap\alpha^{\prime}(\operatorname{Exc}(\alpha^{\prime}))=\emptyset
, hence
\dim\mathcal{N}_{1}(F_{h},Z)=\dim\mathcal{N}_{1}(\alpha^{\prime}(F_{h}),W^{\prime})\leq 2
(see
[
Cas20
, Lemma 2.17]
).
∎

4.9
.

We consider now the case where
f
cannot be factored as in
4.8
; then we get
d_{f}\leq 4
by
Th.
4.1
(a)
.

Suppose moreover that

d_{f}=4
and
\dim Y=2
. Then Th.
4.1
(b)
implies that

f\colon X\to Y
is regular, with general fiber a del Pezzo surface of degree one.

We show that
f
is special and
Y\cong\mathbb{P}^{2}
.
By Prop.
3.12
we can factor
f
as
X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f^{\prime}}}{{\dasharrow}}Y^{\prime}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\varphi}}{{\to}}Y
where
f^{\prime}
is a special rational contraction of fiber type, and
\varphi
is birational; then
Y^{\prime}
is a smooth rational surface (Lemma
3.19
). If
Y^{\prime}\cong\mathbb{P}^{2}
, then
\varphi
is an isomorphism,
f
is special, and we get the statement.

Assume by contradiction that
Y^{\prime}\not\cong\mathbb{P}^{2}
. Then
Y^{\prime}
is obtained as a blow-up of some Hirzebruch surface, therefore it has a contraction
\psi\colon Y^{\prime}\to\mathbb{P}^{1}
. Consider
\psi\circ f^{\prime}\colon X\dasharrow\mathbb{P}^{1}
. We show that
d_{\psi\circ f^{\prime}}>4
, contradicting the first part of the proof.

Let
\zeta\colon X\dasharrow X^{\prime}
be a SQM such that

f^{\prime\prime}:=f^{\prime}\circ\zeta^{-1}\colon X^{\prime}\to Y^{\prime}
is regular.

\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{f}
\scriptstyle{\zeta}
\textstyle{{Y^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\textstyle{Y}
\textstyle{{X^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f^{\prime\prime}}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{P}^{1}}}

Since
\varphi
is birational,
f^{\prime\prime}
and
\varphi\circ f^{\prime\prime}
have the same general fiber, and

d_{f^{\prime\prime}}=d_{\varphi\circ f^{\prime\prime}}=d_{f}=4
; moreover
d_{\psi\circ f^{\prime}}=d_{\psi\circ f^{\prime\prime}}
(see Def.-Rem.
3.2
).
If
F_{2}\subset X^{\prime}
is a general fiber of
\psi\circ f^{\prime\prime}
, and
F_{1}\subset F_{2}\subset X^{\prime}
a general fiber of
f^{\prime\prime}
, then
d_{\psi\circ f^{\prime\prime}}=\dim\mathcal{N}_{1}(F_{2},X^{\prime})>\dim\mathcal{N}_{1}(F_{1},X^{\prime})=4
. This concludes the proof. ∎

The following implies Cor.
1.8
from the Introduction.

Corollary 4.10
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{X}\geq 7
, not isomorphic to a product of surfaces.
If
\tau
is a movable face of
\operatorname{Eff}(X)
, then

\dim\tau\geq\rho_{X}-4\geq 3
.

In particular, every face of dimension
1
or
2
of
\operatorname{Eff}(X)
is fixed.

Again, the condition
\rho_{X}\geq 7
is necessary, as the following example shows.

Example 4.11
.

Let
X
be a Fano
4
-fold as in Examples
4.6
and
4.7
; we have
\rho_{X}=6
and
X
is not a product of surfaces. The cone
\operatorname{Eff}(X)
has a one-dimensional movable face, given by
(f^{\prime})^{*}\operatorname{Nef}(\mathbb{P}^{1})
where
f^{\prime}\colon X\to\mathbb{P}^{1}
is a quasi-elementary contraction as in Ex.
4.7
.

Proof of Cor.
4.10
.

By Rem.
3.11
there is a non-trivial rational contraction of fiber type

f\colon X\dasharrow Y

such that
\dim\tau\geq\rho_{X}-d_{f}
. We have

d_{f}\leq 4
by Th.
4.5
, hence
\dim\tau\geq\rho_{X}-4
.
∎

5.
Preliminaries on fixed prime divisors of Fano
4
-folds

In this section we recall the classification of fixed prime divisors in Fano
4
-folds
X
with

\rho_{X}\geq 7
,
or
\rho_{X}=6
and
\delta_{X}\leq 2
, and give many related properties that are used in the sequel.

Theorem - Definition 5.1

(
the type of a fixed prime divisor).

(See
[
Cas17
, Th. 5.1,
Cor. 5.2, Lemma 5.25]
.)
Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{X}\geq 7
,
or
\rho_{X}=6
and
\delta_{X}\leq 2
,
and
D
a fixed prime divisor in
X
.

(a)

There exists a unique diagram:

X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\xi}}{{\dasharrow}}\widetilde{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sigma}}{{\longrightarrow}}Y

where
\xi
is a SQM,
\sigma
is a divisorial elementary contraction with exceptional divisor the transform
\widetilde{D}
of
D
, and
Y
is Fano (possibly singular);

(b)

\sigma
is of type
(3,0)^{\text{\it sm}}
,
(3,0)^{Q}
,
(3,1)^{\text{\it sm}}
, or
(3,2)
, and we define
D
to be of type
(3,0)^{\text{\it sm}}
,
(3,0)^{Q}
,
(3,1)^{\text{\it sm}}
, or
(3,2)
, accordingly;

(c)

if
D
is of type
(3,2)
, then
X=\widetilde{X}
. In the other cases
\xi
factors as a sequence of at least
\rho_{X}-4

D
-negative and
K
-negative flips.

(d)

We define
C_{D}\subset D\subset X
to be the transform of a general irreducible curve
C_{\widetilde{D}}\subset\widetilde{D}\subset\widetilde{X}
contracted by
\sigma
, of minimal anticanonical degree. Then
C_{D}\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}
,
D\cdot C_{D}=-1
, and
C_{D}\subset\operatorname{dom}(\xi)
.

(e)

Given a SQM
\varphi\colon X\dasharrow X^{\prime}
and a divisorial elementary contraction
\sigma^{\prime}\colon X^{\prime}\to Y^{\prime}
with
\operatorname{Exc}(\sigma^{\prime})
