the transform of
D
, there is a commutative diagram:

\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi}
\scriptstyle{\xi}
\textstyle{{\widetilde{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi_{\scriptscriptstyle X}}
\scriptstyle{\sigma}
\textstyle{{X^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma^{\prime}}
\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi_{\scriptscriptstyle Y}}
\textstyle{{Y^{\prime}}}

where
\psi_{\scriptscriptstyle X}
and
\psi_{\scriptscriptstyle Y}
are SQM’s,
\widetilde{D}\subset\operatorname{dom}(\psi_{\scriptscriptstyle X})
,
and
\sigma(\widetilde{D})\subset\operatorname{dom}(\psi_{\scriptscriptstyle Y})
.

Among the known families of Fano
4
-folds with
\rho_{X}\geq 7
, we have examples of all four types of fixed prime divisors, see §
11.1
and §
11.2
.

Example 5.2
.

Let
X=S_{1}\times S_{2}
with
S_{1}
and
S_{2}
del Pezzo surfaces, and
\rho_{X}\geq 7
. Then every fixed prime divisor
D\subset X
is of type
(3,2)
, and
D=C\times S_{2}
or
S_{1}\times C
where
C
is a
(-1)
-curve.

Lemma 5.3

(
[
Cas17
]
, Rem. 2.17(2) and
[
Cas13a
]
, Cor. 3.14)
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{X}\geq 7
, or
\rho_{X}=6
and
\delta_{X}\leq 2
. Let
E\subset X
be a fixed prime divisor of type
(3,2)
,
X\dasharrow X^{\prime}
a SQM, and
E^{\prime}\subset X^{\prime}
the transform of
E
. Then

E
does not contain exceptional planes and

\dim\mathcal{N}_{1}(E,X)=\dim\mathcal{N}_{1}(E^{\prime},X^{\prime})
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold,
\varphi\colon X\dasharrow X^{\prime}
a SQM, and
E\subset X^{\prime}
a fixed prime divisor. We define
the type of
E
to be the type
of its transform
E_{\scriptscriptstyle X}\subset X
, and we define
C_{E}\subset E\subset X^{\prime}
to be the transform of
C_{E_{\scriptscriptstyle X}}\subset X
.

Remark 5.4
.

In the above setting, we have
C_{E_{X}}\subset\operatorname{dom}(\varphi)
and
C_{E}\subset\operatorname{dom}(\varphi^{-1})
.

Indeed
X\smallsetminus\operatorname{dom}(\varphi)
is a finite union of exceptional planes (Lemma
2.14
). If
E
is of type
(3,2)
, then
E_{X}
does contain exceptional planes by Lemma
5.3
, hence
X\smallsetminus\operatorname{dom}(\varphi)
intersects
E_{X}
at most in dimension one, and does not touch
C_{E_{X}}
.

Suppose that
E
is not of type
(3,2)
, and let
L\subset X\smallsetminus\operatorname{dom}(\varphi)
be an exceptional plane. If
L\subset E_{X}
, then
C_{E_{X}}\cap L=\emptyset
. If instead
L\not\subset E_{X}
but
L\cap E_{X}\neq\emptyset
, let us consider the SQM
\xi\colon X\dasharrow\widetilde{X}
as in Th.-Def.
5.1
, and let
\widetilde{E}\subset\widetilde{X}
be the transform of
E_{X}
. Then
L\not\subset X\smallsetminus\operatorname{dom}(\xi)
and if
\widetilde{L}\subset\widetilde{X}
is its transform, we have
\dim(\widetilde{L}\cap\widetilde{E})=1
, and the general curve
C_{\widetilde{E}}
is disjoint from
\widetilde{L}
, thus again
C_{E_{X}}\cap L=\emptyset
.

Remark 5.5

(the cone
\operatorname{Mov}(X)^{\vee}
)
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{X}\geq 7
, or
\rho_{X}=6
and
\delta_{X}\leq 2
.
Consider the cone
\operatorname{Mov}(X)^{\vee}\subset\mathcal{N}_{1}(X)
, dual of the cone of movable divisors, and note that
\operatorname{mov}(X)\subset\operatorname{Mov}(X)^{\vee}\subset\operatorname{NE}(X)
because dually
\operatorname{Nef}(X)\subset\operatorname{Mov}(X)\subset\operatorname{Eff}(X)
. By

[
Cas17
, Lemma 5.29]
we have:

\operatorname{Mov}(X)^{\vee}=\langle[C_{D}]\rangle_{D\text{ fixed}}+\operatorname{mov}(X),

and every
\langle[C_{D}]\rangle
is a one-dimensional face of
\operatorname{Mov}(X)^{\vee}
.

This means that
\operatorname{Mov}(X)^{\vee}
has two types of one-dimensional faces, and dually that
\operatorname{Mov}(X)
has two types of facets. The one-dimensional face
\langle[C_{D}]\rangle

corresponds to the facet
\operatorname{Mov}(X)\cap[C_{D}]^{\perp}
of
\operatorname{Mov}(X)
; the cones of
\operatorname{MCD}(X)
of dimension
\rho_{X}-1
contained in this facet correspond precisely to divisorial elementary rational contractions
X\dasharrow Y
with exceptional divisor
D
.

The second type of one-dimensional faces
\alpha
of
\operatorname{Mov}(X)^{\vee}
are those contained in
\operatorname{mov}(X)
, so that
\alpha
is a common one-dimensional face of the two cones. Then the corresponding facet
\alpha^{\perp}\cap\operatorname{Mov}(X)
of
\operatorname{Mov}(X)
is contained in a (movable) facet of
\operatorname{Eff}(X)
, and the cones of
\operatorname{MCD}(X)
of dimension
\rho_{X}-1
contained in this facet correspond to elementary rational contractions of fiber type.

Lemma 5.6
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{X}\geq 7
, or
\rho_{X}=6
and
\delta_{X}\leq 2
. Let

\varphi\colon X\dasharrow X^{\prime}
be a SQM and

E\subset X^{\prime}
a fixed prime divisor of type
(3,2)
. Then
[C_{E}]
generates an extremal ray of
\operatorname{NE}(X^{\prime})
if and only if
E
does not meet any exceptional line.

Proof.

If
[C_{E}]
generates an extremal ray of
\operatorname{NE}(X^{\prime})
, it must be divisorial
by Rem.
5.4
, and
the statement follows from Th.-Def.
5.1
and Lemma
2.14
.

Conversely, suppose that
E\subset X^{\prime}
does not meet exceptional lines. Then
E\subset\operatorname{dom}(\varphi^{-1})
(Lemma
2.14
(a)
) and
\varphi^{-1}(E)\subset X
cannot contain exceptional planes (Lemma
5.3
), therefore neither can
E
.

Since
E\cdot C_{E}<0
,
\operatorname{NE}(X^{\prime})
must have an
E
-negative extremal ray
R
; we have
\operatorname{Locus}(R)\subset E
, hence
R
is birational.

We claim that
R
cannot be small. Indeed if
R_{0}
is a small extremal ray of
\operatorname{NE}(X^{\prime})
, and
K_{X^{\prime}}\cdot R_{0}\geq 0
, then
\operatorname{Locus}(R_{0})
is a finite union of exceptional lines (see Lemma
2.14
), hence
\operatorname{Locus}(R_{0})\cap E=\emptyset
. If instead
-K_{X^{\prime}}\cdot R_{0}>0
, then
\operatorname{Locus}(R_{0})
is a finite union of exceptional planes (see Lemma
2.13
), therefore
\operatorname{Locus}(R_{0})\not\subset E
.

We conclude that
R
is divisorial, and by Th.-Def.
5.1

we must have
[C_{E}]\in R
.
∎

Lemma 5.7
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{X}\geq 7
, or
\rho_{X}=6
and
\delta_{X}\leq 2
. Let
\varphi\colon X\dasharrow X^{\prime}
be a SQM,
E\subset X^{\prime}
a fixed prime divisor of type
(3,2)
, and
f\colon X^{\prime}\to Y
a
K
-negative contraction such that
f(C_{E})=\{\text{\it pt}\}
. Then
[C_{E}]
generates an extremal ray of
\operatorname{NE}(X^{\prime})
.

Proof.

Since
[C_{E}]\in\operatorname{NE}(f)
and
E\cdot C_{E}<0
, there exists an extremal ray
R
of
\operatorname{NE}(f)
such that
E\cdot R<0
. Moreover
R
is
K
-negative, because
f
is. If
R
is small, then
\operatorname{Locus}(R)
is a finite union of exceptional planes (see Lemma
2.13
). In particular
\operatorname{Locus}(R)
must be contained in
\operatorname{dom}(\varphi^{-1})
(see Lemma
2.14
), and the transform of
E
in
X
contains an exceptional plane, a contradiction (see Lemma
5.3
).
Hence
R
is divisorial, and by Th.-Def.
5.1

we must have
[C_{E}]\in R
.
∎

Lemma 5.8
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{X}\geq 7
, or
\rho_{X}=6
and
\delta_{X}\leq 2
. Let
\varphi\colon X\dasharrow X^{\prime}
be a SQM and
f\colon X^{\prime}\to Y
an elementary contraction of type
(3,2)
; set
A:=f(\operatorname{Exc}(f))\subset Y
.

Then

Y
can contain finitely many pairwise disjoint exceptional lines, all disjoint from
A
. If
\Gamma\subset Y
is an irreducible curve that is not an exceptional line, then
-K_{Y}\cdot\Gamma\geq 1
. Suppose moreover that

-K_{Y}\cdot\Gamma=1
. Then
\Gamma
cannot meet any exceptional line, and if
\Gamma
meets
A
, then
\Gamma\subset A
.

Furthermore, for every SQM
\psi\colon Y\dasharrow Y^{\prime}
, we have
A\subset\operatorname{dom}(\psi)
.

Proof.

We note that
f
is
K
-negative (see Lemma
2.14
) and
Y

is locally factorial with
at most ordinary double points, contained in
A
(Lemma
2.9
).
By Th.-Def.
5.1
(e)
we have a commutative diagram:

\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma}
\scriptstyle{\varphi}
\textstyle{{X^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{{\widetilde{Y}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi_{Y}}
\textstyle{Y}

where
\varphi_{Y}
is a SQM,
\sigma
is a divisorial elementary contraction with exceptional divisor the transform of
\operatorname{Exc}(f)
,
\widetilde{Y}
is Fano,
\operatorname{Exc}(\sigma)\subset\operatorname{dom}(\varphi)
, and
\sigma(\operatorname{Exc}(\sigma))\subset\operatorname{dom}(\varphi_{Y})
.
Then the same proof as
[
CS24
, proof of Lemma 5.48]

gives the statement. Note that if
\Gamma\subset Y
is an irreducible curve with
-K_{Y}\cdot\Gamma=1
,
\Gamma\cap A\neq\emptyset
, and
\Gamma\not\subset A
, then the transform
\Gamma^{\prime}\subset X^{\prime}
has
-K_{X^{\prime}}\cdot\Gamma^{\prime}\leq 0
, thus
\Gamma^{\prime}
is an exceptional line (Lemma
2.14
) and meets
\operatorname{Exc}(f)
, contradicting Lemma
5.6
. ∎

Lemma 5.9

(
[
Cas22
]
, Lemmas 4.12 and 4.13)
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{X}\geq 7
and
D,E\subset X
two
distinct fixed prime divisors.

(a)

If
D\cdot C_{E}=0
, then
D
and
E
are adjacent.

(b)

If
E
is of type
(3,2)
and
D
and
E
are adjacent, then
D\cdot C_{E}=0
.

Lemma 5.10
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{X}\geq 7
, or
\rho_{X}=6
and
\delta_{X}\leq 2
. Let
D_{1},\dotsc,D_{m}\subset X
be
fixed prime divisors such that
D_{i}\cdot C_{D_{j}}=0
for
i\neq j
. Then
\langle[D_{1}],\dotsc,[D_{m}]\rangle
is a fixed face of
\operatorname{Eff}(X)
of dimension
m
, and there exists a birational (rational) contraction
f\colon X\dasharrow Y
with exceptional divisors
D_{1},\dotsc,D_{m}
, where
Y
is
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial with
\rho_{Y}=\rho_{X}-m
.

Proof.

It is easy to see that
[D_{1}],\dotsc,[D_{m}]\in\mathcal{N}^{1}(X)
are linearly independent. Moreover if
D
is an effective divisor with
[D]\in\langle[D_{1}],\dotsc,[D_{m}]\rangle
,
D\neq 0
, then
D\cdot C_{D_{i}}<0
for some
i\in\{1,\dotsc,m\}
, therefore
D
is not movable, and
\langle[D_{1}],\dotsc,[D_{m}]\rangle\cap\operatorname{Mov}(X)=\{0\}
.

Each
[D_{i}]
generates a one-dimensional face of
\operatorname{Eff}(X)
.
To show that
\langle[D_{1}],\dotsc,[D_{m}]\rangle
is a face, we proceed by induction on
m
, by applying Lemma
2.1
with the linear maps on
\mathcal{N}^{1}(X)
given by intersection with
C_{D_{i}}
.

Finally, for the existence of the map
f
, see
[
Cas22
, Lemma 4.2 and its proof]
.
∎

Lemma 5.11
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{X}\geq 7
and
D
and
E
two adjacent fixed prime divisors,
of type
(3,0)^{\text{\it sm}}
and
(3,2)
respectively. Then
D\cap E=\emptyset
.

Proof.

This follows from Lemma
5.3
and
[
Cas20
, Lemma 4.9]
.
∎

Lemma 5.12

(
[
Cas22
]
, proof of Lemma 7.2)
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{X}\geq 7
. Let
D
be a fixed prime divisor of type
(3,0)^{Q}
, and
E_{1}
,
E_{2}
fixed prime divisors of type
(3,2)
, both adjacent to
D
, such that
D\cap E_{i}\neq\emptyset
for
i=1,2
. Then
E_{1}\cdot C_{E_{2}}=E_{2}\cdot C_{E_{1}}=1
.

Lemma 5.13
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{X}\geq 7
,
\psi\colon X\dasharrow X^{\prime}
a SQM,
and
D,E\subset X^{\prime}
two adjacent fixed prime divisors, of type
(3,1)^{\text{\it sm}}
and
(3,2)
respectively, with
E\cdot C_{D}>0
.
Then
E\cdot C_{D}=1
and
E\cap L=\emptyset
for every exceptional plane
L\subset D
.

Proof.

Let
D_{X},E_{X}\subset X
be the transforms of
D,E
respectively.
It is shown in
[
Cas22
, Lemma 4.23]
that
E_{X}\cdot C_{D_{X}}=1
and
that
E_{X}
is disjoint from every exceptional plane contained in
D_{X}
.
This implies the statement,
indeed
E\cdot C_{D}=E_{X}\cdot C_{D_{X}}
(see Rem.
5.4
), and if
L\subset D
is an exceptional plane,
then
L\subset\operatorname{dom}(\psi^{-1})
(see Lemma
2.14
), and its transform
L_{X}\subset X
is an exceptional contained in
D_{X}
, therefore
L_{X}\cap E_{X}=\emptyset
, and hence
L\cap E=\emptyset
.
∎

Lemma 5.14
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{X}\geq 7
,

X\dasharrow\widehat{X}
a SQM, and

\sigma\colon\widehat{X}\to Y
and
\alpha\colon\widehat{X}\to Z
divisorial elementary contractions of type
(3,2)
and
(3,1)^{\text{\it sm}}
respectively. Set
E:=\operatorname{Exc}(\sigma)
and
D:=\operatorname{Exc}(\alpha)
, and assume that
D\cdot C_{E}=0
and
E\cdot C_{D}>0
.

Then

\langle[C_{D}],[C_{E}]\rangle
is a face of
\operatorname{NE}(\widehat{X})
and we have a commutative diagram:

(5.15)

\textstyle{{\widehat{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\sigma}
\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha^{\prime}}
\textstyle{Z\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma^{\prime}}
\textstyle{W}

where
\sigma^{\prime}
is an elementary contraction of type
(3,2)
with exceptional divisor
\alpha(E)
, and
\alpha^{\prime}
is the blow-up of a smooth point with exceptional divisor
\sigma(D)
(see Fig.
5.1
). Moreover
D\cong\mathbb{P}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(\mathcal{O}^{\oplus 2}\oplus\mathcal{O}(1))
and
E\cdot C_{D}=1
.

Figure 5.1.
The varieties in Lemma
5.14
.

Proof.

Since
D\cdot C_{E}=0
,
D
and
E
are adjacent by Lemma
5.9
, and

E\cdot C_{D}=1
by Lemma
5.13
.

We note that
D\cdot R\geq 0
for every extremal ray
R
of
\operatorname{NE}(\widehat{X})
different from
\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}[C_{D}]
; this follows from Th.-Def.
5.1
,
Lemma
2.14
, and
[
Cas17
, Rem. 5.6]
.

Let
H\in\operatorname{Pic}(\widehat{X})
be the pullback under
\sigma
of an ample divisor on
Y
, and set
m:=H\cdot C_{D}>0
. Then
(H+mD)\cdot C_{E}=(H+mD)\cdot C_{D}=0
, and if
R
is an extremal ray of
\operatorname{NE}(\widehat{X})

different from
\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}[C_{D}]
and
\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}[C_{E}]
,
then
H\cdot R>0
,
D\cdot R\geq 0
, and hence
(H+mD)\cdot R>0
. This shows that

H+mD
is nef and
(H+mD)^{\perp}\cap\operatorname{NE}(\widehat{X})=\langle[C_{D}],[C_{E}]\rangle
is a face of
\operatorname{NE}(\widehat{X})
, hence we have a commutative diagram as (
5.15
) where
\sigma^{\prime}
and
\alpha^{\prime}
are divisorial elementary contractions with exceptional divisors the images of
E
and
D
respectively. Then it is not difficult to see as in
[
Cas22
, proof of Lemma 4.23]
that
\sigma^{\prime}
is of type
(3,2)
,
\alpha
is the blow-up of a one-dimensional fiber of
\sigma^{\prime}
,

D\cong\mathbb{P}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(\mathcal{O}^{\oplus 2}\oplus\mathcal{O}(1))
,
\sigma(D)\cong\mathbb{P}^{3}
, and
\alpha^{\prime}
is of type
(3,0)^{\text{\it sm}}
. See also
[
CS24
, Lemma 2.18]
.
∎

Lemma 5.16
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{X}\geq 7
and
D
a fixed prime divisor of type
(3,1)^{\text{\it sm}}
. Then there is at most one fixed prime divisor
E
of type
(3,2)
which is adjacent to
D
and such that
E\cdot C_{D}>0
.

Proof.

This follows from
[
Cas22
, Lemma 4.23 and its proof]
; see also Lemma
5.14
. Let
X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\xi}}{{\dasharrow}}\widetilde{X}\to Y
be the contraction of
D
as in Th.-Def.
5.1
(a)
, and
\widetilde{D},\widetilde{E}\subset\widetilde{X}
the transforms of
D,E
respectively. Then
\widetilde{D}
is isomorphic to the blow-up of
\mathbb{P}^{3}
along a line,
E\subset\operatorname{dom}(\xi)
so that
\widetilde{E}\cong E
,
\widetilde{E}\cap\widetilde{D}
is the exceptional divisor of
\widetilde{D}\to\mathbb{P}^{3}
, and the fibers of the blow-up
\widetilde{D}\to\mathbb{P}^{3}
are numerically equivalent to
\xi(C_{E})
. Therefore the class
[C_{E}]
is uniquely determined by
D
.
∎

Lemma 5.17
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{X}\geq 7
. Let
D
be a fixed prime divisor of type
(3,1)^{\text{\it sm}}
, and
E_{1}
,
E_{2}
distinct fixed prime divisors of type
(3,2)
, both adjacent to
D
, such that
D\cap E_{i}\neq\emptyset
, for
i=1,2
. Then one of the following holds:

(i)

E_{1}\cdot C_{E_{2}}=E_{2}\cdot C_{E_{1}}=0
;

(ii)

up to exchanging
E_{1}
and
E_{2}
, we have

E_{1}\cdot C_{D}=0
and
E_{2}\cdot C_{D}=E_{2}\cdot C_{E_{1}}=1
.

Proof.

We assume that
(i)
does not hold, and show
(ii)
.
If
E_{1}\cdot C_{E_{2}}=0
, then
E_{1}
and
E_{2}
are adjacent by Lemma
5.9
, hence

E_{2}\cdot C_{E_{1}}=0
by the same lemma; similarly if
E_{2}\cdot C_{E_{1}}=0
. Therefore we can assume that

E_{1}\cdot C_{E_{2}}>0
and
E_{2}\cdot C_{E_{1}}>0
.

By Lemma
5.16
there exists
i\in\{1,2\}
such that
E_{i}\cdot C_{D}=0
; up to exchanging
E_{1}
and
E_{2}
, we can suppose that
E_{1}\cdot C_{D}=0
. Note that
D\cdot C_{E_{1}}=0
because
D
and
E_{1}
are adjacent (Lemma
5.9
).

Then by
[
Cas22
, Lemma 6.9 and Prop. 6.1]
there exists an exceptional plane
L\subset D
such that
C_{D}\equiv C_{L}+C_{E_{1}}
. By Lemma
5.3
we have
L\not\subset E_{2}
, thus
E_{2}\cdot C_{L}\geq 0
and

E_{2}\cdot C_{D}\geq E_{2}\cdot C_{E_{1}}>0
.
By Lemma
5.13

we have
E_{2}\cdot C_{D}=1
, which implies that
E_{2}\cdot C_{E_{1}}=1
.
∎

Lemma 5.18
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{X}\geq 7
,
D
a fixed prime divisor of type
(3,0)^{\text{\it sm}}
or
(3,1)^{\text{\it sm}}
, and
X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\xi}}{{\dasharrow}}\widetilde{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sigma}}{{\to}}Y
the contraction of
D
as in Th.-Def.
5.1
(a)
. Then
\delta_{Y}\leq 2
.

Proof.

We note first of all that
X
is not isomorphic to a product of surfaces because it has a fixed prime divisor of type
(3,0)^{\text{\it sm}}
or
(3,1)^{\text{\it sm}}
,
therefore

\delta_{X}\leq 1
by Th.
1.9
. Moreover
Y
is a smooth Fano
4
-fold.

If by contradiction
\delta_{Y}\geq 3
, then
\operatorname{NE}(Y)
has
an extremal ray
R
of type
(3,2)
with
\operatorname{codim}\mathcal{N}_{1}(E,Y)=\delta_{Y}
where
E=\operatorname{Locus}(R)
(see
[
Cas12
, Rem. 3.2.6]
for the case
\delta_{Y}\geq 4
, and
[
CRS22
, Th. 1.4 and proof of Lemmas 3.2 and 4.5]
for the case
\delta_{Y}=3
).

Consider the transforms
E_{X}\subset X
and
E_{\widetilde{X}}\subset\widetilde{X}
.
Let
\Gamma\subset E
be a general fiber of the contraction of
R
, and let

\Gamma_{X}\subset X
and
\Gamma_{\widetilde{X}}\subset\widetilde{X}
be its transforms. By generality we have
\Gamma\cap\sigma(\operatorname{Exc}(\sigma))=\emptyset
, hence
-K_{\widetilde{X}}\cdot\Gamma_{\widetilde{X}}=-K_{Y}\cdot\Gamma=1
. By Lemma
2.14
this implies that
\Gamma_{\widetilde{X}}\subset\operatorname{dom}(\xi^{-1})
, therefore
-K_{X}\cdot\Gamma_{X}=1
and
E_{X}\cdot\Gamma_{X}=E\cdot\Gamma=-1
. Thus
E_{X}\subset X
is a non-nef prime divisor covered by rational curves of anticanonical degree one, so that
E_{X}
is a fixed prime divisor of type
(3,2)
by
[
Cas17
, Lemma 2.18]
.
Then using Lemmas
2.6
and
5.3
we get

\dim\mathcal{N}_{1}(E,Y)\geq\dim\mathcal{N}_{1}(E_{\widetilde{X}},\widetilde{X})-1=\dim\mathcal{N}_{1}(E_{X},X)-1\geq\rho_{X}-2=\rho_{Y}-1,

a contradiction.
∎

Lemma 5.19
.

Let
X
be a Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{X}\geq 7
and
\delta_{X}\leq 1
,
X\dasharrow X^{\prime}
a SQM, and
f\colon X^{\prime}\to S
a contraction.
Assume that
\rho_{S}\geq 3
, and
let
\Gamma\subset S_{\text{\it reg}}
be a
(-1)
-curve. Then there is an exceptional line
\ell\subset X^{\prime}
, whose class is extremal in
\operatorname{NE}(X^{\prime})
, such that
f(\ell)=\Gamma
.

Proof.

Since
\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}[\Gamma]
is an extremal ray of
\operatorname{NE}(S)
, there is an extremal ray
R
of
\operatorname{NE}(X^{\prime})
such that
f_{*}(R)=\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}[\Gamma]
, so that the contraction of
R
must be birational, with fibers of dimension at most one (see
[
Cas08
, §2.5]
). If
K_{X^{\prime}}\cdot R\geq 0
, then
R
contains the class of an exceptional line
\ell\subset X^{\prime}
(see Lemma
2.14
) and we have
f(\ell)=\Gamma
.

We suppose that
K_{X^{\prime}}\cdot R<0
and show that this gives a contradiction.
Then
R
cannot be small (Lemma
2.13
), thus it must be of type
(3,2)
with locus
D:=f^{-1}(\Gamma)
, and
D
is a fixed prime divisor of type
(3,2)
(see Th.-Def.
5.1
). By Lemma
2.6
we have
\dim\mathcal{N}_{1}(D,X^{\prime})\leq 1+\rho_{X}-\rho_{S}\leq\rho_{X}-2
.
On the other hand, if
D_{X}\subset X
is the transform of
D
, Lemma
5.3
gives
\dim\mathcal{N}_{1}(D_{X},X)=\dim\mathcal{N}_{1}(D,X^{\prime})
, contradicting
\delta_{X}\leq 1
.
∎

Remark 5.20
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{X}\geq 7
and

E,D_{1},\dotsc,D_{m}
fixed prime divisors such that
E
is of type
(3,2)
, each
D_{i}
is of type
(3,1)^{\text{\it sm}}
, and
E\cdot C_{D_{i}}=D_{i}\cdot C_{D_{j}}=0
for every
i,j=1,\dotsc,m
,
i\neq j
.

By Lemma
5.9
this implies that
D_{i}\cdot C_{E}=0
for every
i=1,\dotsc,m
, and Lemma
5.10
gives that

\langle[E],[D_{1}],\dotsc,[D_{m}]\rangle
is a fixed face of
\operatorname{Eff}(X)
, and
E,D_{1},\dotsc,D_{m}
can be contracted together by a birational (rational) contraction with
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial target. However the geometry of this contraction depends on the order in which we contract the divisors; let us describe this. See also
[
CS24
, Lemma 2.19]
for the case
m=1
, and
[
Cas22
, Lemma 6.9, Prop. 6.1, Prop. 6.4]
.

Let us first describe how the divisors
E,D_{1},\dotsc,D_{m}
intersect in
X
.
For every
i,j\in\{1,\dotsc,m\}
,
i\neq j
,
D_{i}\cap D_{j}
is either empty or a disjoint union of exceptional planes
[
Cas22
, Lemma 4.13(b)]
. Let us also note that
X
is not a product of surfaces because it has fixed prime divisors of type
(3,1)^{\text{\it sm}}
, thus
\delta_{X}\leq 1
by Th.
1.9
, and
E
can be disjoint from at most one
D_{i}
(otherwise, if
E
is disjoint from
D_{i}
and
D_{j}
with
i\neq j
, then
\mathcal{N}_{1}(E,X)\subset D_{i}^{\perp}\cap D_{j}^{\perp}
by Rem.
2.7
, a contradiction).
Moreover
by
[
CS24
, Lemma 2.17
(ii)
]
, for every
i=1,\dotsc,m
such that
D_{i}\cap E\neq\emptyset
, there exists an exceptional plane
L_{i}\subset D_{i}
such that
D_{i}\cdot C_{L_{i}}=-1
,
E\cdot C_{L_{i}}=1
,
C_{D_{i}}\equiv C_{E}+C_{L_{i}}
, and
E\cap L=\emptyset
for every exceptional plane
L\subset D_{i}
such that
C_{L}\not\equiv C_{L_{i}}
; we also have that
E\cap D_{i}
is a disjoint union of surfaces isomorphic to
\mathbb{F}_{1}
. For
j\neq i
we get
D_{j}\cdot C_{L_{i}}=0
and hence
D_{j}\cap L_{i}=\emptyset
, because
D_{j}\cdot C_{L_{0}}<0
for every exceptional plane
L_{0}\subset D_{j}
.

We can first perform a sequence of
(D_{1}+\cdots+D_{m})
-negative and
E
-trivial flips, and get a SQM
\xi_{1}\colon X\dasharrow\widetilde{X}
. Then
E\subset\operatorname{dom}(\xi_{1})
, so that
[C_{E}]
is still extremal in
\operatorname{NE}(\widetilde{X})
(Lemma
5.6
), and we have a diagram:

\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\xi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\textstyle{{\widetilde{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{\alpha}}
\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\xi_{Y}}
\textstyle{{\widetilde{Y}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma_{Y}}
\textstyle{W}

where
\alpha
and
\tilde{\alpha}
are the contractions of
\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}[C_{E}]
, of type
(3,2)
, and locally isomorphic,
Y
is Fano with at most isolated, locally factorial, terminal singularities, and
\xi_{Y}
is a SQM (Th.-Def.
5.1
, Lemma
2.9
).

Figure 5.2.
The varieties in Rem.
5.20
, with
m=1
.

In
\widetilde{Y}
the divisors
D_{1},\dotsc,D_{m}
are pairwise disjoint, are contained in
\widetilde{Y}_{\text{\it reg}}
, and are the exceptional divisors of
\sigma_{Y}\colon\widetilde{Y}\to W
blow-up of
m
pairwise disjoint smooth irreducible curves
\Gamma_{1},\dotsc,\Gamma_{m}\subset W_{\text{\it reg}}
;
W
has the same singularities as
Y
and
\widetilde{Y}
(see Fig.
5.2
).

Set
f:=\sigma_{Y}\circ\tilde{\alpha}\colon\widetilde{X}\to W
. Then
f
is
a resolution of the birational map
X\dasharrow W
, with
exceptional divisors
E,D_{1},\dotsc,D_{m}
. In
\widetilde{X}
the relative cone of
f
is
\operatorname{NE}(f)=\langle[C_{E}],[C_{L_{1}}],\dotsc,[C_{L_{m}}]\rangle
, and
C_{D_{i}}\equiv C_{E}+C_{L_{i}}
for every
i
,
8
8

8

Or
\operatorname{NE}(f)=\langle[C_{E}],[C_{L_{1}}],\dotsc,[C_{L_{m-1}}],[C_{D_{m}}]\rangle
if
E\cap D_{m}=\emptyset
in
X
.
while in
\widetilde{Y}
we have
\operatorname{NE}(\sigma_{Y})=\langle[C_{D_{1}}],\dotsc,[C_{D_{m}}]\rangle
.

Moreover
W
is Fano. To see this, consider
f^{*}(-K_{W})=-K_{\widetilde{X}}+E+2(D_{1}+\cdots+D_{m})
, and let
R
be an extremal ray of

\operatorname{NE}(\widetilde{X})
. If
K_{\widetilde{X}}\cdot R\geq 0
, then there is an exceptional line

\ell\subset\widetilde{X}
with
[\ell]\in R
(see Lemma
2.14
), and we have

(D_{1}+\cdots+D_{m})\cdot\ell>0
,
-K_{\widetilde{X}}\cdot\ell=-1
,
E\cdot\ell=0
, thus
f^{*}(-K_{W})\cdot\ell>0
.
Suppose instead that
R
is
K
-negative.
The extremal rays of
\operatorname{NE}(\widetilde{X})
that are negative for some exceptional divisor of
f
are precisely those in
\operatorname{NE}(f)
, therefore if
R\not\subset\operatorname{NE}(f)
we have
-K_{\widetilde{X}}\cdot R>0
,
(E+2(D_{1}+\cdots+D_{m}))\cdot R\geq 0
, and
again
f^{*}(-K_{W})\cdot R>0
. We conclude that
f^{*}(-K_{W})
is nef and
f^{*}(-K_{W})^{\perp}\cap\operatorname{NE}(\widetilde{X})=\operatorname{NE}(f)
, which implies that
-K_{W}
is ample.

On the other hand from
\widetilde{X}
we can also consider the remaining
(D_{1}+\cdots+D_{m})
-negative flips, that will be given by the extremal rays generated by
[C_{L_{1}}],\dotsc,[C_{L_{m}}]
(which are
E
-positive), and get a SQM
\xi_{2}\colon\widetilde{X}\dasharrow\widehat{X}
. Now we get a birational map
\sigma\colon\widehat{X}\to Z
which is the blow-up of
m
pairwise disjoint smooth irreducible curves
\Gamma^{\prime}_{1},\dotsc,\Gamma^{\prime}_{m}\subset Z
, and
Z
is smooth and Fano (this can be seen with a similar argument as for
W
). In
Z
we have
E\cdot\Gamma^{\prime}_{i}>0
for every
i=1,\dotsc,m
. Moreover
[C_{E}]
is extremal in
\operatorname{NE}(Z)
, and there is an associated elementary contraction of type
(3,2)

\alpha_{0}\colon Z\to W
.

\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\xi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\textstyle{{\widetilde{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{\alpha}}
\scriptstyle{\xi_{2}}
\textstyle{{\widehat{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g}
\scriptstyle{\sigma}
\textstyle{Z\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{0}}
\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\xi_{Y}}
\textstyle{{\widetilde{Y}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma_{Y}}
\textstyle{W}

Set
g:=\alpha_{0}\circ\sigma\colon\widehat{X}\to W
. Then
g
is another resolution of the birational map
X\dasharrow W
, and now we have
\operatorname{NE}(g)=\langle[C_{D_{1}}],\dotsc,[C_{D_{m}}],[\ell_{1}],\dotsc,[\ell_{m}]\rangle
where
\ell_{i}\subset\widehat{X}
is the exceptional line corresponding to the exceptional plane
L_{i}\subset\widetilde{X}
.
9
9

9

Or
\operatorname{NE}(g)=\langle[C_{D_{1}}],\dotsc,[C_{D_{m}}],[\ell_{1}],\dotsc,[\ell_{m-1}]\rangle
if
E\cap D_{m}=\emptyset
in
X
.
Moreover
C_{E}\equiv C_{D_{i}}+\ell_{i}
for every
i
.

Lemma 5.21
.

In the setting of Rem.
5.20
, let
i\in\{1,\dotsc,m\}
be such that
E\cap D_{i}\neq\emptyset
,

A\subset W
be the image of
E
, and
\Gamma_{i}\subset W
the image of
D_{i}
, so that
A
is an irreducible surface and
\Gamma_{i}
a smooth irreducible curve contained in
W_{\text{\it reg}}
. Then
A
and
\Gamma_{i}
intersect transversally at finitely many points, contained in
A_{\text{\it reg}}
.

Proof.

Let
S
be a connected component of
E\cap D_{i}
in
X
. Then
S\cong\mathbb{F}_{1}
and if
\ell\subset\mathbb{F}_{1}
is the fiber of the
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-bundle and
e\subset\mathbb{F}_{1}
is the
(-1)
-curve, we have
\ell\equiv C_{E}
,
e=S\cap L_{i}
, and
e\equiv C_{L_{i}}
(notation as in Rem.
5.20
).

The surface
S
is contained in
\operatorname{dom}(\xi_{1})
, therefore it is mapped isomorphically to
\widetilde{X}
, where we still denote it by
S
. Set
A_{Y}:=\tilde{\alpha}(E)\subset\widetilde{Y}
and
\Lambda:=\tilde{\alpha}(S)\subset A_{Y}
;
then
\Lambda
is a connected component of
A_{Y}\cap\tilde{\alpha}(D_{i})
.

We note that
S=\tilde{\alpha}^{-1}(\Lambda)
and
\tilde{\alpha}_{|S}
is a
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-bundle, so that
\tilde{\alpha}
has one-dimensional fibers over
\Lambda
, hence both
A_{Y}
and
\widetilde{Y}
are smooth at every point of
\Lambda

(Lemma
2.9
).

Moreover
\Lambda\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}
and
\Lambda\equiv C_{D_{i}}
. If
\tilde{\alpha}(D_{i})_{|A_{Y}}=m\Lambda+B
where
B
is a divisor in
A_{Y}
supported on the other connected components of
A_{Y}\cap\tilde{\alpha}(D_{i})
(note that
A_{Y}\cap\tilde{\alpha}(D_{i})\subset(A_{Y})_{\text{\it reg}}
), we get
-1=\tilde{\alpha}(D_{i})\cdot C_{D_{i}}=\tilde{\alpha}(D_{i})\cdot\Lambda=\tilde{\alpha}(D_{i})_{|A_{Y}}\cdot_{A_{Y}}\Lambda=m\Lambda^{2}
, so that
m=1
and
\Lambda
is a
(-1)
-curve in
A_{Y}
.

This shows that, in
W
,
w_{0}:=\sigma_{Y}(\Lambda)=f(S)
is a point, and
A
is smooth at
w_{0}
. Moreover
\sigma_{Y|A_{Y}}
is the blow-up of the reduced point
w_{0}
, therefore the curve
\Gamma_{i}
and
A
intersect transversally at
w_{0}
.
∎

Lemma 5.22
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{X}\geq 7
, not isomorphic to a product of surfaces,
X\dasharrow\widetilde{X}
a SQM, and
f\colon\widetilde{X}\to Y
a special contraction with
\dim Y=3
and
\rho_{X}-\rho_{Y}=2
.
Then
\operatorname{NE}(f)=\langle[C_{E_{1}}],[C_{E_{2}}]\rangle
where
E_{1},E_{2}\subset\widetilde{X}
are fixed prime divisors of type
(3,2)
such that

\mathcal{N}_{1}(E_{i},\widetilde{X})=\mathcal{N}_{1}(\widetilde{X})
for
i=1,2
.

Proof.

The Fano
4
-folds as in the statement are classified in
[
CS24
, Th. 5.1]
.
The description of
\operatorname{NE}(f)
is in [
ibid.
, Lemma 5.6], and we keep the same notation. In particular
let
\tilde{\alpha}\colon\widetilde{X}\to\widetilde{W}
the elementary contraction of type
(3,2)
with exceptional divisor
E_{1}
, and
S:=\tilde{\alpha}(E_{1})\subset\widetilde{W}
. Then

\mathcal{N}_{1}(S,\widetilde{W})=\mathcal{N}_{1}(\widetilde{W})
by [
ibid.
, 5.67]; on the other hand

\ker\tilde{\alpha}_{*}\subset\mathcal{N}_{1}(E_{1},\widetilde{X})
and
\tilde{\alpha}_{*}(\mathcal{N}_{1}(E_{1},\widetilde{X}))=\mathcal{N}_{1}(S,\widetilde{W})
, and we conclude that
\mathcal{N}_{1}(E_{1},\widetilde{X})=\mathcal{N}_{1}(\widetilde{X})
.

Let us consider now
E_{2}
. If we are in case
(b)
of [
ibid.
, Lemma 5.39], then the situation for
E_{1}
and
E_{2}
is symmetric [
ibid.
, Lemma 5.46, 5.60], and as before we conclude that
\mathcal{N}_{1}(E_{2},\widetilde{X})=\mathcal{N}_{1}(\widetilde{X})
. If we are in case
(a)
and the surface
S
is singular, then
\tilde{\alpha}\colon\widetilde{X}\to\widetilde{W}
has a
2
-dimensional fiber
F_{0}
(Lemma
2.9
). We have
E_{2}\cdot\operatorname{NE}(\tilde{\alpha})>0
, hence
\dim(F_{0}\cap E_{2})\geq 1
and
\ker\tilde{\alpha}_{*}\subset\mathcal{N}_{1}(E_{2},\widetilde{X})
. On the other hand
T:=\tilde{\alpha}(E_{2})\supset S
, so that
\tilde{\alpha}_{*}(\mathcal{N}_{1}(E_{2},\widetilde{X}))=\mathcal{N}_{1}(T,\widetilde{W})\supset\mathcal{N}_{1}(S,\widetilde{W})=\mathcal{N}_{1}(\widetilde{W})
, and we conclude again that
\mathcal{N}_{1}(E_{2},\widetilde{X})=\mathcal{N}_{1}(\widetilde{X})
.

We are left with the case where we are in case
(a)
and
S
is smooth.
Then by [
ibid.
, Th. 5.1, Lemma 5.68, 5.70] there is a SQM
\widetilde{W}\dasharrow\widehat{W}:=\operatorname{Bl}_{q_{0},\dotsc,q_{r}}\mathbb{P}^{4}
(see §
11.1
), with
r=\rho_{X}-3
, and
S\subset\widetilde{W}
is the transform of a cubic scroll
A\subset\mathbb{P}^{4}
containing the points
q_{0},\dotsc,q_{r}
. Moreover
Y\cong\operatorname{Bl}_{p_{1},\dotsc,p_{r}}\mathbb{P}^{3}
and there is a
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-bundle
\pi\colon\widetilde{W}\to Y
, induced by the projection
\mathbb{P}^{4}\dasharrow\mathbb{P}^{3}
from
q_{0}
, such that
f=\pi\circ\tilde{\alpha}
.

The points
q_{0},\dotsc,q_{r}\in\mathbb{P}^{4}
are in general linear position [
ibid.
, 5.63], therefore up to exchanging some points we can assume that
q_{0}
does not belong to the
(-1)
-curve in
A\cong\mathbb{F}_{1}
. Then the projection of
A
from
q_{0}
is a smooth quadric surface
B_{0}\subset\mathbb{P}^{3}
, and
B:=\pi(S)=\operatorname{Bl}_{p_{1},\dotsc,p_{r}}B_{0}
is a smooth surface with
\rho_{B}=2+r=\rho_{X}-1
. Finally
E_{2}\subset\widetilde{X}
is the transform of
T=\pi^{-1}(B)\subset\widetilde{W}
, which is a
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-bundle over
B
, thus
T
is smooth with
\rho_{T}=\rho_{X}=\rho_{\widetilde{W}}+1
. We have
E_{2}\cong T
because
both
T
and
S
are smooth,
S\subset T
, and
\tilde{\alpha}
is the blow-up along
S
.

Let us consider the restriction
r\colon\mathcal{N}^{1}(\widetilde{W})\to\mathcal{N}^{1}(T)
.
Since

\mathcal{N}_{1}(T,\widetilde{W})=\mathcal{N}_{1}(S,\widetilde{W})=\mathcal{N}_{1}(\widetilde{W})
, dually
r
is injective.

We claim that the class
[S]\in\mathcal{N}^{1}(T)
is not in the image
r(\mathcal{N}^{1}(\widetilde{W}))
. Let us first show that this implies the statement. Consider the commutative diagram:

\textstyle{{\mathcal{N}^{1}(\widetilde{W})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{r}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{\alpha}^{*}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{N}^{1}(T)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi:=(\tilde{\alpha}^{*})_{|\mathcal{N}^{1}(T)}=(\tilde{\alpha}_{|E_{2}})^{*}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{N}^{1}(\widetilde{X})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{r^{\prime}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{N}^{1}(E_{2})}}

where the horizontal maps are the restrictions. Since
r
is injective and
\varphi
is an isomorphism,
\varphi(r(\mathcal{N}^{1}(\widetilde{W})))
is a hyperplane contained in
\operatorname{Im}r^{\prime}
. Moreover
\varphi([S])
is outside this hyperplane and
\varphi([S])=[E_{1|E_{2}}]\in\operatorname{Im}r^{\prime}
, and we conclude that
r^{\prime}
is surjective, hence an isomorphism as
\rho_{E_{2}}=\rho_{X}
, and finally that
\mathcal{N}_{1}(E_{2},\widetilde{X})=\mathcal{N}_{1}(\widetilde{X})
.

To prove our claim, note that the canonical class
K_{A}
is not the restriction of a class in
\mathcal{N}^{1}(\mathbb{P}^{4})
.
Let
\sigma\colon\widehat{W}\to\mathbb{P}^{4}
be the blow-up of
q_{0},\dotsc,q_{r}
, and
S^{\prime}\subset\widehat{W}
the transform of
A
. Then
\sigma_{|S^{\prime}}
is the blow-up of
A
along
q_{0},\dotsc,q_{r}
, and
K_{S^{\prime}}=(\sigma_{|S^{\prime}})^{*}(K_{A})+\sum_{i=0}^{r}e_{i}
, where
e_{i}\subset S^{\prime}
are the
(-1)
-curves, that are restrictions to
S^{\prime}
of the exceptional divisors of
\sigma
. Hence we see that
K_{S^{\prime}}
is not the restriction of a class in
\mathcal{N}^{1}(\widehat{W})
. Moreover
S\subset\widetilde{W}
is contained in the open subset where the SQM
\widetilde{W}\dasharrow\widehat{W}
is an isomorphism
[
CS24
, Lemma 5.48]
, therefore
K_{S}
is not the restriction of a class in
\mathcal{N}^{1}(\widetilde{W})
.
We have
S\subset T
and
K_{S}=(K_{T}+S)_{|S}
, hence
K_{T}+S\not\in r(\mathcal{N}^{1}(\widetilde{W}))
. On the other hand
K_{T}=(K_{\widetilde{W}}+T)_{|T}
, and we conclude that
[S]\not\in r(\mathcal{N}^{1}(\widetilde{W}))
.
∎

6.
Quasi-elementary rational contractions onto surfaces

In this section we prove the following result (Th.
1.11
from the Introduction), that will be needed in the next section; it improves Theorems
4.1
and
4.5
in the case of a quasi-elementary rational contraction onto a surface.

Theorem 6.1
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{X}\geq 7
, not isomorphic to a product of surfaces, and
f\colon X\dasharrow S
a quasi-elementary rational contraction onto a surface.
Then
\rho_{X}-\rho_{S}\leq 3
, and if
\rho_{X}-\rho_{S}>1
, then
f
factors as
X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle g}}{{\dasharrow}}Y\stackrel{{\scriptstyle h}}{{\dasharrow}}S
where
\dim Y=3
,

g
and
h
are rational contractions,
g
is special, and
h

is elementary.

The condition
\rho_{X}\geq 7
is necessary, see Ex.
4.6
; see moreover §
11.4
for an example of a Fano
4
-fold
X
with
\rho_{X}=3
and a quasi-elementary contraction
X\to\mathbb{P}^{2}
, with general fiber
\operatorname{Bl}_{2\mskip 1.0mu\text{\it pts}}\mathbb{P}^{2}
, that does not factor through a
3
-fold. We also note that the bound
\rho_{X}-\rho_{S}\leq 3
is sharp, as the following examples show.

Example 6.2
.

Let
X
be one of the two families of Fano
4
-folds with
\rho_{X}=7
described in §
11.2
. Then there are a
quasi-elementary rational contraction
f\colon X\dasharrow S

with
\dim S=2
and

d_{f}=\rho_{X}-\rho_{S}=3
, and
a rational contraction
f^{\prime}\colon X\dasharrow\mathbb{P}^{1}
with
d_{f^{\prime}}=4
.

Example 6.3
.

Let
X
be the Fano model of the blow-up of
\mathbb{P}^{4}
at
r
general points, with
r\in\{6,7,8\}
, so that
\rho_{X}\in\{7,8,9\}
(see §
11.1
). Then there is a
a quasi-elementary rational contraction
f\colon X\dasharrow S

with
\dim S=2
and
d_{f}=\rho_{X}-\rho_{S}=2
.

Theorems
6.1
and
1.5
allow to classify complete the case where
\rho_{X}-\rho_{S}=3
, as follows.

Corollary 6.4
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{X}\geq 7
, not isomorphic to a product of surfaces, and
f\colon X\dasharrow S
a quasi-elementary rational contraction onto a surface with

\rho_{X}-\rho_{S}\geq 3
. Then
\rho_{X}-\rho_{S}=3
and
X
is as in Th.
1.5
(ii)
.

6.5
Overview of the proof of Th.
6.1
.

We first show a preliminary property in Lemma
6.6
, then we prove
Th.
6.1
.
The proof is quite long and will take the whole section.

The bound
\rho_{X}-\rho_{S}\leq 3
follows easily from Th.
4.5
; the main work is to prove that
f
factors through a
3
-fold, when it is not elementary. So suppose that
\rho_{X}-\rho_{S}\in\{2,3\}
and let
\tilde{f}\colon\widetilde{X}\to S
be a
K
-negative resolution of
f
. We analyse the possible factorizations of
f
in elementary contractions, using Lemmas
3.16
and
3.18
, and show that either the statement holds, or
\tilde{f}
has two distinct factorizations
\widetilde{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sigma_{i}}}{{\to}}Y_{i}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle g_{i}}}{{\to}}S
where
\sigma_{i}
is elementary of type
(3,2)
and
g_{i}
is quasi-elementary and
K
-negative, for
i=1,2
. Set
E_{i}:=\operatorname{Exc}(\sigma_{i})\subset\widetilde{X}
.

The surface
S
is smooth del Pezzo with
\rho_{S}\geq 4
; for a
(-1)
-curve
\Gamma\subset S
,
D:=\tilde{f}^{*}\Gamma
is a fixed prime divisor in
\widetilde{X}
. Using results from Section
5
we show that
D
is of type
(3,1)^{\text{\it sm}}
, and that up to exchanging
E_{1}
and
E_{2}
we have
E_{1}\cdot C_{D}=0
,
E_{2}\cdot C_{D}=E_{2}\cdot C_{E_{1}}=1
, and
E_{1}\cdot C_{E_{2}}>1
. Then by applying Lemma
6.6
we deduce that the general fiber of
g_{2}
is
\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
.
Thanks to this, if
\rho_{X}-\rho_{S}=3
, we factor again
g_{2}
using Lemma
3.18
, and we get the statement.

We finally prove that in this setting we cannot have
\rho_{X}-\rho_{S}=2
,
proceeding by contradiction. We show that
Y_{1}
is smooth and
g_{1}\colon Y_{1}\to S
is a
\mathbb{P}^{2}
-bundle, while
the general fiber of
\tilde{f}
is
\operatorname{Bl}_{2\mskip 1.0mu\text{\it pts}}\mathbb{P}^{2}
.
Then we consider a birational map
\alpha\colon S\to\mathbb{P}^{2}
, and show that
\alpha\circ g_{1}\colon Y_{1}\to\mathbb{P}^{2}
also factors as
Y_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{SQM}}}{{\dasharrow}}\widetilde{Y}_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\beta}}{{\to}}\mathbb{P}^{2}\times\mathbb{P}^{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle h}}{{\to}}\mathbb{P}^{2}
, where
h
is the first projection and
\beta
is the blow-up of
\rho_{X}-3
lines contained in distinct fibers of
h
(compare with §
11.3
).

Let
B_{2}\subset\mathbb{P}^{2}\times\mathbb{P}^{2}
be the transform of
E_{2}\subset\widetilde{X}
; then
B_{2}
is a prime divisor of bidegree
(2,1)
, containing the lines blown-up by
\beta
. We also consider
A\subset B_{2}
the surface image of
E_{1}\subset\widetilde{X}
.
A careful analysis of the possible configurations for
B_{2}
and
A
shows that this case gives a contradiction.

Lemma 6.6
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{X}\geq 7
and

X\dasharrow\widetilde{X}
a SQM.
Let
f\colon\widetilde{X}\to S
be a
K
-negative contraction onto a surface that factors as
g\circ\sigma
, where
\sigma\colon\widetilde{X}\to Y
is a divisorial elementary contraction of type
(3,2)
.

\textstyle{{\widetilde{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\scriptstyle{\sigma}
\textstyle{{Y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g}
\textstyle{S}

Suppose also that there is a
(-1)
-curve
\Gamma\subset S_{\text{\it reg}}
such that
D:=f^{*}(\Gamma)
is a fixed prime divisor of type
(3,1)^{\text{\it sm}}
with
E\cdot C_{D}>0
, where
E:=\operatorname{Exc}(\sigma)\subset\widetilde{X}
.

Then the general fiber of
g
is
\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
.

Proof.

Since
f(C_{E})=\{\text{\it pt}\}
, we have
D\cdot C_{E}=0
, therefore
D
and
E
are adjacent by Lemma
5.9
.

Let
\tau\colon S\to S_{1}
be the contraction of the
(-1)
-curve
\Gamma
, and set
p:=\tau(\Gamma)\in S_{1}
; the composition
\tau\circ f\colon\widetilde{X}\to S_{1}
contracts
{D}
to the point
p
. We run an MMP for
{D}
, relative to
\tau\circ f
: this means that we consider
D
-negative small extremal rays in
\operatorname{NE}(\tau\circ f)
and their flips, until we find a
D
-negative divisorial extremal ray in the relative cone. In this way
we get a commutative diagram:

\textstyle{{\widetilde{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{f}}
\scriptstyle{\varphi}
\textstyle{{\widehat{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\textstyle{{Z}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{h}
\textstyle{S\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tau}
\textstyle{S_{1}}

where
\varphi
is a sequence of
D
-negative flips,
\alpha
is a divisorial elementary contraction with exceptional divisor the transform
\widehat{D}\subset\widehat{X}
of
D
, and
h
is a contraction of fiber type. Note that both
\widehat{D}
and the indeterminacy locus of
\varphi^{-1}
are contained in the fiber
(h\circ\alpha)^{-1}(p)
.

By Th.-Def.
5.1
,
Z
is smooth and is a SQM of a smooth Fano
4
-fold, and

\alpha
is the blow-up of a smooth curve
C\subset Z
.

Since
[C_{E}]
is extremal in
\operatorname{NE}(\widetilde{X})
, by Lemma
5.6

E
is disjoint from all exceptional lines in
\widetilde{X}
.
Let us factor
\varphi
as a sequence of
D
-negative flips:

\widetilde{X}=\widetilde{X}_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sigma_{1}}}{{\dasharrow}}\widetilde{X}_{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sigma_{2}}}{{\dasharrow}}\cdots\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sigma_{r-1}}}{{\dasharrow}}\widetilde{X}_{r}=\widehat{X}.

Let
\widehat{E}\subset\widehat{X}
and
E_{i}\subset\widetilde{X}_{i}
be the transforms of
E
.
We show by induction on
i=1,\dotsc,r
that
E_{i}
is disjoint from all exceptional lines in
\widetilde{X}_{i}
. For
i=1
we have already remarked this.

Suppose that the claim holds in
\widetilde{X}_{i}
, and consider the flip
\sigma_{i}\colon\widetilde{X}_{i}\dasharrow\widetilde{X}_{i+1}
.
If
\sigma_{i}
is not
K
-negative, then its indeterminacy locus is a disjoint union of exceptional lines (see Lemma
2.14
), and
X_{i+1}
will have less exceptional lines than
X_{i}
, still disjoint from
E_{i+1}
. If instead
\sigma_{i}
is
K
-negative, then its indeterminacy locus is a finite disjoint union of exceptional planes
L_{j}\subset X_{i}
(see Lemma
2.13
) such that
D\cdot C_{L_{j}}<0
, because
\sigma_{i}
is
D
-negative. Then
E_{i}\cap L_{j}=\emptyset
by Lemma
5.13
, thus in
X_{i+1}
the divisor
E_{i+1}
stays disjoint from the new exceptional lines in the indeterminacy locus of
\sigma_{i}^{-1}
.

We conclude that
\widehat{E}\subset\widehat{X}
is disjoint from all exceptional lines in
\widehat{X}
, and
by Lemma
5.6

the class
[C_{\widehat{E}}]
generates an extremal ray of
\operatorname{NE}(\widehat{X})
, which is contracted by the map
h\circ\alpha\colon\widehat{X}\to S_{1}
. Let
\hat{\sigma}\colon\widehat{X}\to\widehat{Y}
be the associated contraction, of type
(3,2)
with exceptional divisor
\widehat{E}
(see Th.-Def.
5.1
).

By Lemma
5.14
we have a commutative diagram:

\textstyle{{\widetilde{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\scriptstyle{\varphi}
\scriptstyle{\sigma}
\textstyle{{\widehat{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hat{\sigma}}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\textstyle{{Z}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{h}
\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi_{Y}}
\scriptstyle{g}
\textstyle{{\widehat{Y}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha^{\prime}}
\textstyle{W\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g_{1}}
\textstyle{S\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tau}
\textstyle{S_{1}}

where

\sigma^{\prime}
is a divisorial elementary contraction of type
(3,2)
with
\operatorname{Exc}(\sigma^{\prime})=\alpha(\widehat{E})
,

\alpha^{\prime}
is the blow-up of a smooth point
q\in W
with

\operatorname{Exc}(\alpha^{\prime})=\hat{\sigma}(\widehat{D})=:D^{\prime}\subset\widehat{Y}
, and
g_{1}
is a contraction of fiber type.

Moreover
\varphi_{Y}
is a SQM such that
\hat{\sigma}(\widehat{E})\subset\operatorname{dom}(\varphi_{Y}^{-1})
(by Th.-Def.
5.1
), therefore

\widehat{Y}\smallsetminus\operatorname{dom}(\varphi_{Y}^{-1})
is contained in the fiber
(g_{1}\circ\alpha^{\prime})^{-1}(p)
, and the same for
D^{\prime}
. We conclude that

g\colon Y\to S
,
g_{1}\circ\alpha^{\prime}\colon\widehat{Y}\to S_{1}
, and
g_{1}\colon W\to S_{1}
, all have isomorphic general fibers.

We show that
F\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
where
F\subset W
is the general fiber of
g_{1}
; this gives the statement.

By Lemma
2.9
both
\widehat{Y}
and
W
have at most isolated, locally factorial, and terminal singularities; in particular
F
is smooth.

We show that
g_{1}
is
K
-negative. Let
\ell\subset W
be an irreducible curve with
-K_{W}\cdot\ell\leq 0
, and
\ell_{\widehat{Y}}\subset\widehat{Y}
its transform.
Note that
\ell_{\widehat{Y}}\not\subset D^{\prime}
because
\alpha^{\prime}(D^{\prime})=\{q\}
, thus
D^{\prime}\cdot\ell_{\widehat{Y}}\geq 0
and

0\geq-K_{W}\cdot\ell=(-K_{\widehat{Y}}+3D^{\prime})\cdot\ell_{\widehat{Y}}\geq-K_{\widehat{Y}}\cdot\ell_{\widehat{Y}}
.
By Lemma
5.8

\ell_{\widehat{Y}}
is an exceptional line, disjoint from
\hat{\sigma}(\widehat{E})
, and
K_{\widehat{Y}}\cdot\ell_{\widehat{Y}}=1
also gives
D^{\prime}\cdot\ell_{\widehat{Y}}=0
,
thus
\ell_{\widehat{Y}}\cap D^{\prime}=\emptyset
.

Then
the transform
\ell_{\widehat{X}}\subset\widehat{X}
is again an exceptional line,
disjoint from both

\widehat{E}
and
\widehat{D}
.
It is not difficult to see that
\ell_{\widehat{X}}
must be contained in
\operatorname{dom}(\varphi^{-1})
, so that its transform
\ell_{\widetilde{X}}\subset\widetilde{X}

is an exceptional line, disjoint from
D
. Since
f
is
K
-negative,
f(\ell_{\widetilde{X}})\subset S
is a curve. Moreover it cannot be the
(-1)
-curve
\Gamma
, otherwise
\ell_{\widetilde{X}}
would be contained in
D
. We conclude that
\tau(f(\ell_{\widetilde{X}}))=g_{1}(\ell)\subset S_{1}
is a curve, and
g_{1}
is
K
-negative, therefore its general fiber

F
is a smooth del Pezzo surface.

Suppose by contradiction that
F\not\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
. Then
F
is covered by a family of curves of anticanonical degree
3
, thus
W
is covered by a family of curves of anticanonical degree
3
, contracted by
g_{1}
. If
C\subset W
is a curve of the family containing the point
q
blown-up by
\alpha^{\prime}\colon\widehat{Y}\to W
, then every irreducible component
C_{0}
of
C
satisfies
-K_{W}\cdot C_{0}>0
, because
g_{1}
is
K
-negative.
Let
C_{0}
be an irreducible component of
C
containing
q
, and

\widehat{C}_{0}\subset\widehat{Y}
its transform.
If
-K_{W}\cdot C_{0}=1
we get
-K_{\widehat{Y}}\cdot\widehat{C}_{0}\leq-2
, and if
-K_{W}\cdot C_{0}=3
we get
-K_{\widehat{Y}}\cdot\widehat{C}_{0}=0
or
-K_{\widehat{Y}}\cdot\widehat{C}_{0}\leq-3
,
in any case
contradicting Lemma
5.8
. Therefore it must be
-K_{W}\cdot C_{0}=2
,
-K_{\widehat{Y}}\cdot\widehat{C}_{0}\leq-1
, and

\widehat{C}_{0}
is an exceptional line by Lemma
5.8
. However we must also have
C=C_{0}\cup C_{1}
with
C_{1}
irreducible,
-K_{W}\cdot C_{1}=1
, and
q\not\in C_{1}
. Then the transform of
C_{1}
in
\widehat{Y}
is an irreducible curve of anticanonical degree one intersecting an exceptional line, contradicting
Lemma
5.8
.
Therefore
F\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
.
∎

Proof of Th.
6.1
.

Let us assume that
f
is not elementary, so that
\rho_{X}-\rho_{S}>1
.

Since
f
is quasi-elementary, we have
d_{f}=\rho_{X}-\rho_{S}
, and
S
is a smooth del Pezzo surface (Lemma
3.20
). By Th.
4.5
we have
\rho_{X}-\rho_{S}\leq 4
, and if

\rho_{X}-\rho_{S}=4
, then it should be
S\cong\mathbb{P}^{2}
, which is impossible because
\rho_{X}\geq 7
. Therefore we have
2\leq\rho_{X}-\rho_{S}\leq 3
, in particular
\rho_{S}\geq\rho_{X}-3\geq 4
.

We are left to show that
f
factors as
X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle g}}{{\dasharrow}}Y\stackrel{{\scriptstyle h}}{{\dasharrow}}S
where
\dim Y=3
,
g
is special, and
h
is elementary.
Let us consider a
K
-negative resolution
\tilde{f}\colon\widetilde{X}\to S
of
f
.

6.7
.

Either we get the statement, or
\widetilde{X}
contains two fixed prime divisors
E_{1},E_{2}
of type
(3,2)
such that
C_{E_{1}}
and
C_{E_{2}}
are contracted by
\tilde{f}
and
(E_{1}\cdot C_{E_{2}})(E_{2}\cdot C_{E_{1}})>1
.

Proof.

We consider first the case
\rho_{X}-\rho_{S}=2
, and apply Lemma
3.18
to
f
. If we get
(i)
, we have the statement. If we get
(ii)
,
then
f
admits two factorizations
X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\alpha_{i}}}{{\dasharrow}}W_{i}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle h_{i}}}{{\to}}S
where
\alpha_{i}
is a divisorial elementary rational contraction with exceptional locus dominating
S
for
i=1,2
, so that
\alpha_{i}
is of type
(3,2)
. Moreover
\alpha_{1}^{*}\mathcal{N}^{1}(W_{1})\neq\alpha_{2}^{*}\mathcal{N}^{1}(W_{2})
, hence
\operatorname{Exc}(\alpha_{1})\neq\operatorname{Exc}(\alpha_{2})
by Th.-Def.
5.1
.

Let
E_{i}\subset\widetilde{X}
be the transform of
\operatorname{Exc}(\alpha_{i})
; then
E_{i}
is
a fixed prime divisor of type
(3,2)
,
E_{1}\neq E_{2}
, and
C_{E_{i}}
is contracted by
\tilde{f}
. Moreover
\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}[C_{E_{i}}]
is an extremal ray of
\operatorname{NE}(\tilde{f})

by Lemma
5.7
, and finally

\operatorname{NE}(\tilde{f})=\langle[C_{E_{1}}],[C_{E_{2}}]\rangle
. Since
f
is quasi-elementary, we have
\dim(\operatorname{NE}(\tilde{f})\cap\operatorname{mov}(X))=2
(see
[
Cas13a
, Prop. 2.22]
), hence

(E_{1}\cdot C_{E_{2}})(E_{2}\cdot C_{E_{1}})>1
by
[
Cas20
, Lemma 4.6(a)]
.

Suppose now that
\rho_{X}-\rho_{S}=3
; we proceed similarly. By Lemma
3.16

f
factors as

X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle g}}{{\dasharrow}}X_{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle h}}{{\to}}S

where
g
is elementary, either divisorial or of fiber type, and
h
is of fiber type (because
f
is quasi-elementary). Moreover
h
is quasi-elementary by Lemma
3.15
and

\rho_{X_{2}}-\rho_{S}=2
, therefore
\dim X_{2}\neq 3
, and we conclude that
\dim X_{2}=4
and

g
is birational divisorial with
\operatorname{Exc}(g)
dominating
S
.

Now we apply Lemma
3.18
to
h
. If we get
(i)
, then
h
factors as

X_{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle g_{2}}}{{\dasharrow}}Y\stackrel{{\scriptstyle h_{2}}}{{\to}}S
where
\dim Y=3
and both
g_{2}
and
h_{2}
are elementary of fiber type. Since
\operatorname{Exc}(g)
dominates
S
, its image in
Y
is a divisor, hence the composition
g_{2}\circ g\colon X\dasharrow Y
is special, and we get the statement.

If instead we get
(ii)
, for
i=1,2
we have a factorization of
h
as

X_{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\alpha_{i}}}{{\dasharrow}}W_{i}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle h_{i}}}{{\to}}S

where
\alpha_{i}
is a divisorial elementary rational contraction with exceptional locus dominating
S
for
i=1,2
, so that
\alpha_{i}
is of type
(3,2)
.
Let
E_{i}\subset\widetilde{X}
be the transform of
\operatorname{Exc}(\alpha_{i})\subset X_{2}
. Similarly as in the previous case
\rho_{X}-\rho_{S}=2
we see that
E_{i}
is a fixed prime divisor of type
(3,2)
,
C_{E_{i}}
is contracted by
\tilde{f}
, and
E_{1}\neq E_{2}
. Let moreover
G\subset\widetilde{X}
be the transform of
\operatorname{Exc}(g)
, which is again a fixed prime divisor of type
(3,2)
.

We note that
G
and
E_{i}
are adjacent, because they are both contracted by the composite birational map
\widetilde{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{SQM}}}{{\dasharrow}}X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle g}}{{\dasharrow}}X_{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\alpha_{i}}}{{\dasharrow}}W_{i}
, where
W_{i}
is
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial and
\rho_{X}-\rho_{W_{i}}=2
(see e.g.
[
Cas22
, proof of Lemma 4.2]
).
By Lemma
5.9
we conclude that
G\cdot C_{E_{i}}=E_{i}\cdot C_{G}=0
for
i=1,2
.

Let us show that

({E}_{1}\cdot C_{{E}_{2}})({E}_{2}\cdot C_{{E}_{1}})>1
.
Let
\beta\colon\widehat{X}\to W_{1}
be a resolution of the above map
\widetilde{X}\dasharrow W_{1}
; with a slight abuse of notation, we still denote by
G
and
E_{i}
their transforms in
\widehat{X}
.
Then
G,E_{1}
are the exceptional divisors for
\beta
, and

\beta(E_{2})\subset W_{1}
is a divisor that dominates
S
under
h_{1}\colon W_{1}\to S
, therefore

\beta(E_{2})\cdot\operatorname{NE}(h_{1})>0
. Moreover

\beta^{*}(\beta(E_{2}))=E_{2}+(E_{2}\cdot C_{E_{1}})E_{1}+(E_{2}\cdot C_{G})G=E_{2}+(E_{2}\cdot C_{E_{1}})E_{1}
, and
[\beta_{*}(C_{E_{2}})]\in\operatorname{NE}(h_{1})
.
Hence:

0<\beta(E_{2})\cdot\beta_{*}(C_{E_{2}})=\bigl(E_{2}+(E_{2}\cdot C_{E_{1}})E_{1}\bigr)\cdot C_{E_{2}}=(E_{1}\cdot C_{E_{2}})(E_{2}\cdot C_{E_{1}})-1,

and we conclude that
(E_{1}\cdot C_{E_{2}})(E_{2}\cdot C_{E_{1}})>1
.
∎

6.8
.

Let
i\in\{1,2\}
. Since
\tilde{f}
is
K
-negative and contracts
C_{E_{i}}
, by Lemma
5.7

\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}[C_{E_{i}}]
is an extremal ray of
\operatorname{NE}(\tilde{f})
,
and we have a
factorization:

\textstyle{{\widetilde{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\sigma_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{f}}
\textstyle{{Y_{1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g_{1}}
\textstyle{S}
\textstyle{{Y_{2}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g_{2}}

where
\sigma_{i}
is the contraction
of
\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}[C_{E_{i}}]
,
Y_{i}
has at most isolated terminal and locally factorial singularities (Lemma
2.9
),
\sigma_{i}(E_{i})
dominates
S
, and
g_{i}
is quasi-elementary (Lemma
3.15
).

We also note that
g_{i}
is
K
-negative: suppose otherwise; then by Lemma
5.8

g_{i}
must contract some exceptional line of
Y_{i}
, disjoint from
\sigma_{i}(E_{i})
, and this contradicts the
K
-negativity of
\tilde{f}
.

Let
F_{i}\subset Y_{i}
be a general fiber of
g_{i}
, so that
F_{i}
is a smooth del Pezzo surface.

6.9
.

Let
\Gamma\subset S
be a
(-1)
-curve (recall that
\rho_{S}\geq 4
), and set
D:=\tilde{f}^{-1}(\Gamma)
; then
D
is a prime divisor because
\tilde{f}
is quasi-elementary (Lemma
3.9
), and it is fixed because
\Gamma
is. Moreover
\tilde{f}^{*}(\Gamma)=mD
for some
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
, thus
D\cdot\gamma=0
for every
\gamma\in\operatorname{NE}(\tilde{f})
, and
D
is the pullback of some divisor in
S
; this implies that
m=1
and
D=\tilde{f}^{*}(\Gamma)
.

We show that
D
is of type
(3,1)^{\text{\it sm}}
.
We have
\tilde{f}(D)=\Gamma
, therefore
\dim\mathcal{N}_{1}(D,\widetilde{X})\leq 1+\rho_{X}-\rho_{S}\leq 4

(Lemma
2.6
).
On the other hand we have

\delta_{X}\leq 1
by Th.
1.9
, and we deduce from Lemma
5.3
that
D
cannot be of type
(3,2)
.

Moreover for
i=1,2
we have
D\cdot C_{E_{i}}=0
, hence
D
is adjacent to
E_{i}
by Lemma
5.9
; in
\widetilde{X}
we have
{D}\cap{E}_{i}\neq\emptyset
because
{E}_{i}
dominates
S
, thus
\dim({D}\cap{E}_{i})=2
and since the indeterminacy locus of the map
\widetilde{X}\dasharrow X
is one-dimensional (Lemma
2.14
(a)
), we have
\widetilde{D}\cap\widetilde{E}_{i}\neq\emptyset
in
X
, where
\widetilde{D},\widetilde{E}_{i}\subset X
are the transforms of
D,E_{i}
respectively.

Then
D
cannot be of type
(3,0)^{\text{\it sm}}
by Lemma
5.11
. Moreover
D
cannot be of type
(3,0)^{Q}
, otherwise by Lemma
5.12
we get a contradiction with
(E_{1}\cdot C_{E_{2}})(E_{2}\cdot C_{E_{1}})>1
. We conclude that
D
is of type
(3,1)^{\text{\it sm}}
.

We apply Lemma
5.17
. Since
(E_{1}\cdot C_{E_{2}})(E_{2}\cdot C_{E_{1}})>1
, up to exchanging
E_{1}
and
E_{2}
we have:

(6.10)

E_{1}\cdot C_{D}=0,\hskip 10.00002ptE_{2}\cdot C_{D}=E_{2}\cdot C_{E_{1}}=1,\hskip 10.00002pt\text{and}\hskip 10.00002ptE_{1}\cdot C_{E_{2}}\geq 2.

Note that
E_{1}\cdot C_{E_{2}}
and
E_{2}\cdot C_{E_{1}}
do not depend on
D
,
therefore (
6.10
) must hold for
the pullback
D
of
every

(-1)
-curve
\Gamma\subset S
.

Since
E_{2}\cdot C_{D}>0
, we can apply
Lemma
6.6
to
\tilde{f}=g_{2}\circ\sigma_{2}
and
D
, and deduce that
F_{2}\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
, where
F_{2}\subset Y_{2}
is a general fiber of
g_{2}
.

6.11
.

Suppose that
\rho_{X}-\rho_{S}=3
, so that

\rho_{Y_{2}}-\rho_{S}=2
, and apply Lemma
3.18
to
g_{2}\colon Y_{2}\to S
.
We show that
(ii)
cannot happen. Otherwise,
g_{2}
factors as
Y_{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\alpha}}{{\dasharrow}}W\stackrel{{\scriptstyle h}}{{\to}}S
where
\alpha
is elementary and divisorial with exceptional locus dominating
S
. Consider
a resolution
\alpha^{\prime}\colon Y_{2}^{\prime}\to W
of
\alpha
. Then there is a sequence of flips
\psi\colon Y_{2}\dasharrow Y_{2}^{\prime}
, relative to
g_{2}
, such that

\alpha=\alpha^{\prime}\circ\psi
.

\textstyle{{Y_{2}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\textstyle{{Y_{2}^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha^{\prime}}
\textstyle{S}
\textstyle{W\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{h}

By Lemma
5.8
each flip in
\psi
has locus contained in the smooth locus of the
4
-fold, and is either
K
-negative, or
K
-positive. Therefore the locus of each flip is a finite disjoint union of exceptional planes or lines, and such locus must be contracted to finitely many points by
g_{2}
. We conclude that
h\circ\alpha^{\prime}
sends the indeterminacy locus
Y_{2}^{\prime}\smallsetminus\operatorname{dom}(\psi^{-1})
to a finite set of points, therefore
\psi
is an isomorphism on the general fiber
F_{2}
, and

h\circ\alpha^{\prime}
has general fiber
F_{2}^{\prime}\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
. However this is impossible, because
\alpha^{\prime}
must restrict to a non-trivial birational contraction on
F_{2}^{\prime}
.

Therefore we must get
(i)
from Lemma
3.18
,
and
g_{2}
factors as

Y_{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\beta}}{{\dasharrow}}Z\stackrel{{\scriptstyle k}}{{\to}}S

with
\dim Z=3
and both maps are elementary of fiber type.
Since
E_{2}
dominates
S
, its image in
Z
is a surface,
\beta\circ\sigma_{2}\colon\widetilde{X}\dasharrow Z
is special,
and we get the statement.

6.12
.

We are left with the case where
\rho_{X}-\rho_{S}=2
and
g_{1}
and

g_{2}
are elementary. We show that this case does not happen, as it leads to a contradiction.

Note that
\operatorname{NE}(\tilde{f})=\langle[C_{E_{1}}],[C_{E_{2}}]\rangle
(see
6.8
).

For
i=1,2
let
r_{i}
be the degree of
(g_{i})_{|\sigma_{i}(E_{i})}\colon\sigma_{i}(E_{i})\to S
. Then
\sigma_{i}
blows-up
F_{i}
in
r_{i}
points, so if
F\subset\widetilde{X}
is a general fiber of
\tilde{f}
, then
F
is a del Pezzo surface with

(6.13)

\rho_{F}=\rho_{F_{i}}+r_{i}.

We show that:

(6.14)

r_{1}E_{2}\cdot C_{E_{1}}=r_{2}E_{1}\cdot C_{E_{2}}.

Indeed write
(E_{i})_{|F}=\Gamma_{i1}+\cdots+\Gamma_{ir_{i}}
; then
\Gamma_{ij}\equiv C_{E_{i}}
for every
j
, and

\displaystyle r_{1}E_{2}\cdot C_{E_{1}}=E_{2}\cdot(\Gamma_{11}+\cdots+\Gamma_{1r_{1}})=(E_{2})_{|F}\cdot_{F}(\Gamma_{11}+\cdots+\Gamma_{1r_{1}})

\displaystyle=(\Gamma_{21}+\cdots+\Gamma_{2r_{2}})\cdot_{F}(E_{1})_{|F}=r_{2}E_{1}\cdot C_{E_{2}}.

Now (
6.13
), (
6.14
), (
6.10
), and
\rho_{F_{2}}=2
, give:

2+r_{2}=\rho_{F}=\rho_{F_{1}}+r_{1}=\rho_{F_{1}}+r_{2}E_{1}\cdot C_{E_{2}}\geq 1+2r_{2},

and we get
r_{2}=1
,
F_{1}\cong\mathbb{P}^{2}
,
\rho_{F}=3
, and

r_{1}=E_{1}\cdot C_{E_{2}}=2
.

6.15
.

We show that:

(6.16)

-K_{\widetilde{X}}+\tilde{f}^{*}(-K_{S})=2E_{1}+3E_{2}.

Indeed

(-K_{\widetilde{X}}-2E_{1}-3E_{2})\cdot C_{E_{i}}=0
for
i=1,2
, thus

-K_{\widetilde{X}}-2E_{1}-3E_{2}=\tilde{f}^{*}M
for some divisor
M
on
S
.

By Lemma
5.19
, for every
(-1)
-curve
\Gamma\subset S
there exists an exceptional line
\ell\subset\widetilde{X}
such that
\tilde{f}(\ell)=\Gamma
. We have
E_{i}\cdot\ell=0
for
i=1,2
by Lemma
5.6
, thus

-1=(-K_{\widetilde{X}}-2E_{1}-3E_{2})\cdot\ell=\tilde{f}^{*}M\cdot\ell=M\cdot\tilde{f}_{*}(\ell),

and this implies that

M\cdot\Gamma=-1=K_{S}\cdot\Gamma
for every
(-1)
-curve
\Gamma\subset S
. Since
S
is a del Pezzo surface with
\rho_{S}=\rho_{X}-2\geq 5
,
\mathcal{N}_{1}(S)
is generated by classes of
(-1)
-curves, and we conclude that
M=K_{S}
.

6.17
.

Set
B_{2}^{\prime}:=\sigma_{1}(E_{2})\subset Y_{1}
. Applying the pushforward
(\sigma_{1})_{*}
to (
6.16
) we get:

(6.18)

-K_{Y_{1}}+g_{1}^{*}(-K_{S})=3B_{2}^{\prime}.

Restricting to
F_{1}\cong\mathbb{P}^{2}
gives
\mathcal{O}_{Y_{1}}(B_{2}^{\prime})_{|F_{1}}\cong\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{2}}(1)
,
in particular
B_{2}^{\prime}
is
g_{1}
-ample. If
A
is an ample divisor on
S
, then for
r\gg 0
the divisor
B_{2}^{\prime}+rg_{1}^{*}(A)
is ample on
Y_{1}
, and we still have
\mathcal{O}_{Y_{1}}(B_{2}^{\prime}+rg_{1}^{*}(A))_{|F_{1}}\cong\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{2}}(1)
.
By
[
BS95
, Prop. 3.2.1]
, this implies that
g_{1}
is a
\mathbb{P}^{2}
-bundle and

Y_{1}
is smooth, so that
Y_{1}
is a SQM of a smooth Fano
4
-fold
Y^{\prime}_{1}
with
\rho_{Y_{1}^{\prime}}\geq 6
(see Th.-Def.
5.1
).

6.19
.

Let us choose a birational morphism
\alpha\colon S\to\mathbb{P}^{2}
, which blows-up the points
p_{1},\dotsc,p_{m}\in\mathbb{P}^{2}
with
m=\rho_{S}-1=\rho_{X}-3\geq 4
.
For
i\in\{1,\dotsc,m\}
let
\Gamma_{i}\subset S
be the
(-1)
-curve over
p_{i}
, and
set
D_{i}:=\tilde{f}^{*}\Gamma_{i}\subset\widetilde{X}
. Then
D_{i}
is a fixed prime divisor of type
(3,1)^{\text{\it sm}}
with
D_{i}\cdot C_{E_{1}}=D_{i}\cdot C_{E_{2}}=E_{1}\cdot C_{D_{i}}=0
and
E_{2}\cdot C_{D_{i}}=1
, for every
i=1,\dotsc,m
(see
6.9
). Moreover for
i\neq j
we have
D_{i}\cap D_{j}=\emptyset
, therefore
D_{i}\cdot C_{D_{j}}=0
. By Rem.
5.20
this implies that
\langle[E_{1}],[D_{1}],\dotsc,[D_{m}]\rangle
is a fixed face of
\operatorname{Eff}(\widetilde{X})\cong\operatorname{Eff}(X)
, of dimension
\rho_{X}-2
.

For
i\in\{1,\dotsc,m\}
set
D_{i}^{\prime}:=\sigma_{1}(D_{i})\subset Y_{1}
; the divisors
D_{1}^{\prime},\dotsc,D_{m}^{\prime}
are contracted to points by
\alpha\circ g_{1}
. We run a MMP in
Y_{1}
for
D_{1}^{\prime}+\cdots+D_{m}^{\prime}
, relative to
\alpha\circ g_{1}
, and get
a commutative diagram:

\textstyle{{\widetilde{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{f}}
\scriptstyle{\sigma_{1}}
\textstyle{{Y_{1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\xi}
\textstyle{{\widetilde{Y}_{1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\beta}
\textstyle{W\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{h}
\textstyle{S\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\textstyle{\mathbb{P}^{2}}

where
\xi
is a SQM,
\beta
is birational with exceptional divisors the transforms
D_{1}^{\prime\prime},\dotsc,D_{m}^{\prime\prime}\subset\widetilde{Y}_{1}
of
D_{1}^{\prime},\dotsc,D_{m}^{\prime}\subset Y_{1}
, and
h\colon W\to\mathbb{P}^{2}
is an elementary contraction with general fiber
F_{W}\cong\mathbb{P}^{2}
. Moreover it follows from
Rem.
5.20
that

\beta
is the blow-up of
m
pairwise disjoint smooth curves
C_{1},\dotsc,C_{m}\subset W
, and
W
is smooth and a SQM of a smooth Fano
4
-fold.

Let
\widetilde{B}_{2}^{\prime}\subset\widetilde{Y}_{1}
and
B_{2}\subset W
be the transforms of
E_{2}
, and set
H=h^{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{2}}(1)\in\operatorname{Pic}(W)
.
We have
-K_{S}=\alpha^{*}(-K_{\mathbb{P}^{2}})-\sum_{i=1}^{m}\Gamma_{i}
, and by (
6.18
)

-K_{Y_{1}}+g_{1}^{*}\alpha^{*}(-K_{\mathbb{P}^{2}})-\sum_{i=1}^{m}D_{i}^{\prime}=3B_{2}^{\prime}
, which in
\widetilde{Y}_{1}
gives

-K_{\widetilde{Y}_{1}}+3\beta^{*}(H)-\sum_{i=1}^{m}D_{i}^{\prime\prime}=3\widetilde{B}_{2}^{\prime}
.
Finally via the pushforward
\beta_{*}
we get:

(6.20)

-K_{W}=3(B_{2}-H).

This implies that

B_{2|F_{W}}\cong\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{2}}(1)
, therefore using again
[
BS95
, Prop. 3.2.1]
as in
6.17
we get

W=\mathbb{P}_{\mathbb{P}^{2}}(\mathcal{E})
, where
\mathcal{E}
is a rank
3
vector bundle on
\mathbb{P}^{2}
.
Recall that
W
is a SQM of a smooth Fano
4
-fold. Moreover, since
-K_{W}
is divisible by
3
in
\operatorname{Pic}(W)
,

W
cannot contain exceptional lines nor exceptional planes, so the second elementary contraction of
W
cannot be small (see Lemma
2.14
and Lemma
2.13
), and
W
is Fano of index

3
. This also implies that
c_{1}(\mathcal{E})\equiv 0\mod 3
, and by
[
SW90
]

we conclude that
W\cong\mathbb{P}^{2}\times\mathbb{P}^{2}
. We can assume that
h\colon\mathbb{P}^{2}\times\mathbb{P}^{2}\to\mathbb{P}^{2}
is the first projection.

Let us consider the curves
C_{1},\dotsc,C_{m}\subset\mathbb{P}^{2}\times\mathbb{P}^{2}
blown-up by
\beta
.
We have
that
h(C_{i})=p_{i}\in\mathbb{P}^{2}
with
p_{i}\neq p_{j}
for
i\neq j
, so that
C_{i}
is a smooth curve in
h^{-1}(p_{i})=\mathbb{P}^{2}
. If
L\subset h^{-1}(p_{i})
is a general line, we have
-K_{\mathbb{P}^{2}\times\mathbb{P}^{2}}\cdot L=3
and
L
must meet
C_{i}
, hence
D_{i}^{\prime\prime}\cdot\widetilde{L}>0
, where
\widetilde{L}\subset\widetilde{Y}_{1}
is
its transform. On the other hand
\widetilde{L}
cannot be an exceptional line, because
\widetilde{Y}_{1}
can contain at most finitely many of them (see Lemma
2.14
), therefore
-K_{\widetilde{Y}_{1}}\cdot\widetilde{L}>0
, which implies that
D_{i}^{\prime\prime}\cdot\widetilde{L}=1

and
C_{i}\cdot_{\mathbb{P}^{2}}L=1
, so that
C_{i}
is itself a line in
h^{-1}(p_{i})=\mathbb{P}^{2}
.

We have therefore shown that

Y_{1}
is a SQM of a blow-up of
\mathbb{P}^{2}\times\mathbb{P}^{2}
along
m
lines contained in different fibers of the first projection (see Ex.
11.3
); in particular
\widetilde{Y}_{1}\smallsetminus\operatorname{dom}(\xi^{-1})
is the disjoint union of
m
exceptional planes, given by the transforms of
h^{-1}(p_{1}),\dotsc,h^{-1}(p_{m})
, and
Y_{1}\smallsetminus\operatorname{dom}\xi
is the disjoint union of
m
exceptional lines.

6.21
.

Consider the surface
\sigma_{1}(E_{1})\subset B_{2}^{\prime}\subset Y_{1}
, and let
\widetilde{A}\subset\widetilde{B}_{2}^{\prime}\subset\widetilde{Y}_{1}
and
A\subset B_{2}\subset\mathbb{P}^{2}\times\mathbb{P}^{2}
be its transforms.

Let
i\in\{1,\dotsc,m\}
. It follows from Lemma
5.21
that, whenever
A\cap C_{i}\neq\emptyset
,
A
and
C_{i}
intersect transversally at finitely many points, where
A
is smooth.

From (
6.20
) we see that
B_{2}
has bidegree
(2,1)
in
\mathbb{P}^{2}\times\mathbb{P}^{2}
,
hence
h_{|B_{2}}\colon B_{2}\to\mathbb{P}^{2}
is generically a
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-bundle.
Moreover
C_{i}\subset B_{2}
, because
E_{2}\cdot C_{D_{i}}>0
(see
6.19
); on the other hand
B_{2}
cannot contain the whole fiber
h^{-1}(p_{i})
, otherwise
\widetilde{B}_{2}^{\prime}\subset\widetilde{Y}_{1}
would contain an exceptional plane in the indeterminacy locus of
\xi^{-1}
,
\widetilde{B}_{2}\subset{Y}_{1}
would intersect an exceptional line, and
E_{2}\subset\widetilde{X}
would intersect an exceptional line too, a contradiction (see Lemmas
5.8
and
5.6
). Therefore
C_{i}=(h_{|B_{2}})^{-1}(p_{i})
,
h_{|B_{2}}
is a
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-bundle over
p_{i}
, and

C_{i}\subset(B_{2})_{\text{\it reg}}
.

Finally we note that since
E_{1}\cdot C_{E_{2}}=2
, we have
A\cdot_{B_{2}}C_{i}=2
, and we conclude that
A
and
C_{i}
intersect at exactly two points.

We set
C_{i}=\{p_{i}\}\times L_{i}
with
L_{i}\subset\mathbb{P}^{2}
a line.

6.22
.

Let
i,j,k\in\{1,\dotsc,m\}
with
i<j<k
; we show that
L_{i}\cap L_{j}\cap L_{k}=\emptyset
(in particular this also shows that
L_{i}\neq L_{j}
). By contradiction, suppose otherwise, and let
q\in L_{i}\cap L_{j}\cap L_{k}
.

Let
h^{\prime}\colon\mathbb{P}^{2}\times\mathbb{P}^{2}\to\mathbb{P}^{2}
be the second projection, and
in
(h^{\prime})^{-1}(q)=\mathbb{P}^{2}
consider a general conic
\Gamma
through
(p_{i},q)
,
(p_{j},q)
, and
(p_{k},q)
(note that
p_{i},p_{j},p_{k}
are not collinear in
\mathbb{P}^{2}
, because
\operatorname{Bl}_{p_{i},p_{j},p_{k}}\mathbb{P}^{2}
is a del Pezzo surface, see
6.19
). Then
-K_{\mathbb{P}^{2}\times\mathbb{P}^{2}}\cdot\Gamma=6
and if
\widetilde{\Gamma}\subset\widetilde{Y}_{1}
is its transform, we have
D_{i}^{\prime\prime}\cdot\widetilde{\Gamma}>0
,
D_{j}^{\prime\prime}\cdot\widetilde{\Gamma}>0
,
D_{k}^{\prime\prime}\cdot\widetilde{\Gamma}>0
, hence

-K_{\widetilde{Y}_{1}}\cdot\widetilde{\Gamma}\leq 0
, so that
\widetilde{\Gamma}
is an exceptional line by Lemma
2.14
. However this is impossible, because
\widetilde{Y}_{1}
contains at most finitely many exceptional lines (again by Lemma
2.14
), while
\Gamma
moves in a positive dimensional family.

6.23
.

Set
\Lambda:=h^{\prime}(A)\subset\mathbb{P}^{2}
, and let
i\in\{1,\dotsc,m\}
. We have
h^{\prime}(C_{i}\cap A)\subset L_{i}\cap\Lambda
, in particular
L_{i}\cap\Lambda\neq\emptyset
because
C_{i}\cap A\neq\emptyset
. Moreover
\Lambda
cannot be a point, otherwise it should lie in
L_{1}\cap L_{2}\cap L_{3}
which is empty by
6.22
(recall that
m\geq 4
).

Let
q\in L_{i}\cap\Lambda
; we show that
(p_{i},q)\in A
. Suppose otherwise, and let
L
be a line in
(h^{\prime})^{-1}(q)
containing
(p_{i},q)
and meeting
A
.
Then
-K_{\mathbb{P}^{2}\times\mathbb{P}^{2}}\cdot L=3
, and if
\widetilde{L}\subset\widetilde{Y}_{1}
is its transform, we have
-K_{\widetilde{Y}_{1}}\cdot\widetilde{L}\leq 1
,
\widetilde{L}\cap\widetilde{A}\neq\emptyset
, and
\widetilde{L}\not\subset\widetilde{A}
, contradicting Lemma
5.8
.
Therefore
(p_{i},q)\in A
.

Since we also have
(p_{i},q)\in C_{i}
, this implies that
h^{\prime}(C_{i}\cap A)=L_{i}\cap\Lambda
, hence
L_{i}
intersects
\Lambda
set-theoretically in two points (see
6.21
).
We conclude that
\Lambda
is an irreducible curve of degree at least
2
.

6.24
.

Let
\tau\colon Z\to\mathbb{P}^{2}\times\mathbb{P}^{2}
be the blow-up of
A
, with exceptional divisor

E_{1}^{\prime}\subset Z
; we note that

Z
is a smooth Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{Z}=3
(see Rem.
5.20
), and there is a birational contraction
\varphi\colon X\dasharrow Z
.

Let
q\in\Lambda
be a general point,
\Gamma\subset\mathbb{P}^{2}\times\{q\}
a general line, and
\widetilde{\Gamma}\subset Z
its transform. Note that
A
has at most isolated singularities (Lemma
2.9
), therefore
(h^{\prime}_{|A})^{-1}(q)\subset\mathbb{P}^{2}\times\{q\}
is a smooth curve; let
d
be its degree.
Then
E_{1}^{\prime}\cdot\widetilde{\Gamma}=d
, hence
-K_{Z}\cdot\widetilde{\Gamma}=3-d\geq 1
, and we get
d\in\{1,2\}
.

Let
G_{Z}\subset Z
be the transform of
G:=\mathbb{P}^{2}\times\Lambda\subset\mathbb{P}^{2}\times\mathbb{P}^{2}
. We have

G\cdot\Gamma=0
and
\tau^{*}(G)=G_{Z}+mE_{1}^{\prime}
with
m\geq 1
, therefore
G_{Z}\cdot\widetilde{\Gamma}=-md<0
.

If
d=2
, we see that
G_{Z}
is a fixed prime divisor covered by rational curves of anticanonical degree one. Consider the birational contraction
\varphi\colon X\dasharrow Z
, and let
G_{X}\subset X
be the transform of
G_{Z}
. Then the indeterminacy locus of
\varphi^{-1}\colon Z\dasharrow X
must be disjoint from the transform of
\mathbb{P}^{2}\times\{q\}
(see
[
CS24
, Prop. 2.10]
), so that
G_{X}

is again a fixed prime divisor covered by rational curves
C
with
-K_{X}\cdot C=1
. By
[
Cas17
, Lemma 2.18]

G_{X}
must be of type
(3,2)
, but this contradicts
G_{X}\cdot C=-2m\leq-2
.

6.25
.

Therefore
d=1
, and
A
is
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-bundle over
\Lambda
; since
A
has isolated singularities, this implies that

\Lambda
is smooth.

Recall that
B_{2}
has bidegree
(2,1)
in
\mathbb{P}^{2}\times\mathbb{P}^{2}
,
hence
h^{\prime}_{|B_{2}}\colon B_{2}\to\mathbb{P}^{2}
is generically a conic bundle.

We notice that
h^{\prime}_{|B_{2}}
does not have
2
-dimensional fibers, otherwise the fibers of
h_{|B_{2}}
, as curves in
\mathbb{P}^{2}
, should all have a common point. However the curves
C_{i}
are fibers of
h_{|B_{2}}
(see
6.21
), and
by
6.22
there is no common point on the lines
L_{i}
’s. Therefore
h^{\prime}_{|B_{2}}\colon B_{2}\to\mathbb{P}^{2}
is a conic bundle; let
\Delta\subset\mathbb{P}^{2}
be its discriminant locus, parametrising singular conics.

Since the general fiber of
h^{\prime}_{|A}
is a line, we have
\Lambda\subset\Delta
, and
(h^{\prime}_{|B_{2}})^{*}(\Lambda)=A+A^{\prime}
where
A^{\prime}
is a prime divisor that dominates
\Lambda
(possibly
A^{\prime}=A
), such that the general fiber of
h^{\prime}_{|A^{\prime}}
is a line too.

This implies that
B_{2}
must be singular, otherwise
h^{\prime}_{|B_{2}}
would be an elementary contraction of a smooth Fano
3
-fold, and
A\cdot C=0
for the general fiber
C\subset B_{2}
, so that
A
should be the pullback of a divisor in
\mathbb{P}^{2}
, but it is not. Since
B_{2}
is singular,
h_{|B_{2}}\colon B_{2}\to\mathbb{P}^{2}
must have a
2
-dimensional fiber
F=\{p_{0}\}\times\mathbb{P}^{2}\subset B_{2}
, and all the fibers of the conic bundle
h^{\prime}_{B_{2}}
contain the point
p_{0}
.

6.26
.

Recall that
\deg\Lambda\geq 2
by
6.23
.
Suppose first that
\Lambda
is a conic, and recall that
C_{1}\subset(B_{2})_{\text{\it reg}}
and
A\cdot_{B_{2}}C_{1}=2
(see
6.21
). Then

2=\Lambda\cdot_{\mathbb{P}^{2}}L_{1}=(h^{\prime}_{|B_{2}})^{*}(\Lambda)\cdot_{B_{2}}C_{1}=(A+A^{\prime})\cdot_{B_{2}}C_{1}=2+A^{\prime}\cdot_{B_{2}}C_{1}
, therefore
A^{\prime}\cdot_{B_{2}}C_{1}=0
. We also have
C_{1}\not\subset A^{\prime}
, because
L_{1}\not\subset\Lambda
, and we conclude that
A^{\prime}\cap C_{1}=\emptyset
, namely
A^{\prime}\cap(h_{|B_{2}})^{-1}(p_{1})=\emptyset
, and
p_{1}\not\in h(A^{\prime})
.

Then
h(A^{\prime})\subsetneq\mathbb{P}^{2}
, so that
A^{\prime}=L_{0}\times\Lambda
where
L_{0}
is a fixed line.
Note that
B_{2}
is the total space of a net of conics; let
\Gamma\subset\mathbb{P}^{2}
be the subvariety parametrising conics of the net that have
L_{0}
as a component. It is not difficult to see that

\Gamma
must be linear, and since it contains the conic
\Lambda
, we get
\Gamma=\mathbb{P}^{2}
, but this is impossible because
B_{2}
is irreducible.
Therefore we conclude that

\deg\Lambda>2
.

6.27
.

If
\Delta\subsetneq\mathbb{P}^{2}
, then
\Delta
is a cubic curve and
\Lambda\subset\Delta
, therefore
\Lambda=\Delta
and
\Delta
is smooth (see
6.25
).
A local computation shows that this implies that
B_{2}
is smooth too (see e.g.
[
CS24
, Rem. 3.9 and its proof]
), but we have already excluded this (see
6.25
).

Therefore
\Delta=\mathbb{P}^{2}
and every fiber of
h^{\prime}_{|B_{2}}
is singular. Then it is not difficult to see that either all conics of the net have a fixed component, or they are all singular at the same point
p_{0}
(see for instance
[
Wal77
, Table 1]
), and
since
B_{2}
is irreducible, we must be in the second case.
The fibers of
h^{\prime}_{|A}
are components of fibers of
h^{\prime}_{|B_{2}}
, hence they all contain
p_{0}
, and
F\cap A
is a section of
h^{\prime}_{|A}
, where
F=\{p_{0}\}\times\mathbb{P}^{2}
. Therefore
F\cap A\cong\Lambda
, and

F\cap A
has degree
\geq 3
. Let
L\subset F
be a general line, and
\widetilde{L}\subset Z
its transform (see
6.24
). Then
E_{1}^{\prime}\cdot\widetilde{L}\geq 3
and
-K_{\mathbb{P}^{2}\times\mathbb{P}^{2}}\cdot L=3
, thus
-K_{Z}\cdot\widetilde{L}\leq 0
, a contradiction.
This concludes the proof of Th.
6.1
.
∎

7.
Fixed divisors of type
(3,2)

In this section we show the following result, which implies Th.
1.10
from the Introduction.

Theorem 7.1
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{X}\geq 7
, not isomorphic to a product of surfaces, and
E\subset X
a fixed prime divisor of type
(3,2)
such that
\mathcal{N}_{1}(E,X)\subsetneq\mathcal{N}_{1}(X)
.

Then
\rho_{X}\leq 9
,

X
has an elementary rational contraction onto a
3
-fold, and
E
is of type
(3,2)^{\text{\it sm}}
. More precisely there is a commutative diagram

(7.2)

\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\xi}
\textstyle{{\widetilde{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{f}}
\scriptstyle{\sigma}
\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g}
\textstyle{W^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{W\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{h}
\textstyle{Z}

where:

\bullet

W^{\prime}
is a smooth Fano
4
-fold and
\sigma^{\prime}
is of type
(3,2)^{\text{\it sm}}
,
with exceptional divisor
E
;

\bullet

\xi
and
W^{\prime}\dasharrow W
are SQM’s, and
E\subset\operatorname{dom}(\xi)
;

\bullet

Y
is a smooth weak Fano
10
10

10

Namely
-K_{Y}
is nef and big.

3
-fold and
\tilde{f}
is an elementary contraction and a conic bundle, finite on
\xi(E)
;

\bullet

Z
is a smooth del Pezzo surface.

The bound
\rho_{X}\leq 9
improves the bound
\rho_{X}\leq 12
shown in
[
Cas17
, Prop. 5.32]
;
we do not know if it is sharp.
Let us also note that, up to flops, there are only three possibilities for the
3
-fold
Y
, see
[
CS24
, Cor. 6.4]
.

7.3
Overview of the proof of Th.
7.1
.

We consider the cone
\operatorname{Mov}(X)^{\vee}\subset\mathcal{N}_{1}(X)
, dual of the cone of movable divisors, that has
\langle[C_{E}]\rangle
as a face of dimension one (see Rem.
5.5
). The main step is to show that there exists a two-dimensional face
\langle[C_{E}]\rangle+\alpha
of
\operatorname{Mov}(X)^{\vee}
with
\alpha\subset\operatorname{mov}(X)
(in particular this implies that
X
has an elementary rational contraction of fiber type, see again Rem.
5.5
).
To prove this, we work by contradiction, and show that otherwise we can produce a special rational contraction of fiber type
\psi\colon X\dasharrow T
, with
\rho_{X}-\rho_{T}=2
, and contracting
C_{E}
. Then we reach a contradiction by applying Lemma
5.22
when
\dim T=3
, and Th.
6.1
when
\dim T=2
.

Once we have the one-dimensional face
\alpha
of
\operatorname{Mov}(X)^{\vee}
as above, this gives an elementary rational contraction of fiber type
X\dasharrow Y
. If
X\to W^{\prime}
is the elementary contraction of type
(3,2)
with exceptional divisor
E
, since
\langle[C_{E}]\rangle+\alpha
is a face of
\operatorname{Mov}(X)^{\vee}
, we see that there are elementary rational contractions
Y\dasharrow Z
and
W^{\prime}\dasharrow Z
onto the same target. Then by analysing the maps, and using that
\mathcal{N}_{1}(E,X)\subsetneq\mathcal{N}_{1}(X)
, we get the statement.
