Proof of Th.
7.1
.

Since
X
is not a product of surfaces and
\rho_{X}\geq 7
, we have
\delta_{X}\leq 1
by Th.
1.9
. Therefore

\delta_{X}=1
and
\mathcal{N}_{1}(E,X)
is a hyperplane in
\mathcal{N}_{1}(X)
.

Consider the cone
\operatorname{Mov}(X)^{\vee}\subset\mathcal{N}_{1}(X)
, dual of the cone of movable divisors; we have

\operatorname{Mov}(X)^{\vee}=\langle[C_{D}]\rangle_{D\text{ fixed}}+\operatorname{mov}(X)
,
and
\langle[C_{E}]\rangle
is a one-dimensional face of
\operatorname{Mov}(X)^{\vee}
, see Rem.
5.5
.

7.4
.

There exists a one-dimensional face
\alpha
of
\operatorname{Mov}(X)^{\vee}
, contained in
\operatorname{mov}(X)
, such that

\langle[C_{E}]\rangle+\alpha
is a face of
\operatorname{Mov}(X)^{\vee}
.

Proof.

We proceed by contradiction, and assume that every
2
-dimensional face of

\operatorname{Mov}(X)^{\vee}
containing
\langle[C_{E}]\rangle
has the form

\langle[C_{E}],[C_{D}]\rangle
for some fixed prime divisor
D\subset X
.

Then
as in
[
Cas17
, proof of Prop. 5.32]
we show that there exists a

2
-dimensional face
\langle[C_{E}],[C_{E^{\prime}}]\rangle
of

\operatorname{Mov}(X)^{\vee}
where
E^{\prime}
is a
fixed prime divisor of type
(3,2)
such that
[C_{E^{\prime}}]\not\in\mathcal{N}_{1}(E,X)
,
E\cap E^{\prime}\neq\emptyset
,
E\cdot C_{E^{\prime}}>0
, and
E^{\prime}\cdot C_{E}>0
. In particular
\langle[C_{E}],[C_{E^{\prime}}]\rangle\cap\operatorname{mov}(X)\neq\{0\}
by
[
Cas20
, Lemma 4.6(a)]
.

The
2
-dimensional face
\langle[C_{E}],[C_{E^{\prime}}]\rangle
of

\operatorname{Mov}(X)^{\vee}
corresponds by duality to the codimension
2
face

\eta:=\operatorname{Mov}(X)\cap C_{E}^{\perp}\cap C_{E^{\prime}}^{\perp}

of
\operatorname{Mov}(X)
. Note that
\eta\subset\partial\operatorname{Eff}(X)
, because
\langle[C_{E}],[C_{E^{\prime}}]\rangle\cap\operatorname{mov}(X)\neq\{0\}
. Let
\eta_{0}\in\operatorname{MCD}(X)
be a cone of dimension
\rho_{X}-2
with
\eta_{0}\subset\eta
, and

\psi\colon X\dasharrow T
the rational contraction such that
\eta_{0}=\psi^{*}(\operatorname{Nef}(T))
; then
\rho_{T}=\rho_{X}-2\geq 5
,
\psi
is of fiber type, and
\dim T\in\{2,3\}
.

We show that
\psi
is special.

Let
\tau_{\psi}
be the minimal face of
\operatorname{Eff}(X)
containing
\eta
(see Def.-Rem.
3.2
). By Lemma
3.10
, we need to show that

\tau_{\psi}\cap\operatorname{Mov}(X))=\eta
.

Note that
\tau_{\psi}\cap\operatorname{Mov}(X)
is a proper face of
\operatorname{Mov}(X)
and contains
\eta
, therefore if
\tau_{\psi}\cap\operatorname{Mov}(X))\supsetneq\eta
, then
\tau_{\psi}\cap\operatorname{Mov}(X)
must be a facet of
\operatorname{Mov}(X)
containing
\eta
, and contained in

\partial\operatorname{Eff}(X)
.

On the other hand
\eta
is contained in exactly two facets of
\operatorname{Mov}(X)
, that are

\operatorname{Mov}(X)\cap C_{E}^{\perp}
and
\operatorname{Mov}(X)\cap C_{E^{\prime}}^{\perp}
, and both of them are not contained in
\partial\operatorname{Eff}(X)
. Therefore
\tau_{\psi}\cap\operatorname{Mov}(X)
cannot be a facet, it must be

\tau_{\psi}\cap\operatorname{Mov}(X)=\eta
, and
\psi
is special.

Let

\tilde{\psi}\colon\widetilde{X}\longrightarrow T

be a
K
-negative resolution of
\psi
, and let
\widetilde{E},\widetilde{E}^{\prime}\subset\widetilde{X}
be the transforms of
E,E^{\prime}
. Since
\eta_{0}\subset C_{E}^{\perp}\cap C_{E^{\prime}}^{\perp}
,
\tilde{\psi}
contracts both
C_{\widetilde{E}}
and
C_{\widetilde{E}^{\prime}}
, and by Lemma
5.7
we have
\operatorname{NE}(\tilde{\psi})=\langle[C_{\widetilde{E}}],[C_{\widetilde{E}^{\prime}}]\rangle
. In particular, since
\operatorname{NE}(\tilde{\psi})
does not contain small extremal rays, there are no flips relative to
\tilde{\psi}
, and
\tilde{\psi}\colon\widetilde{X}\to T
is the unique resolution of
\psi
.

We have
\dim\mathcal{N}_{1}(\widetilde{E},\widetilde{X})=\dim\mathcal{N}_{1}(E,X)=\rho_{X}-1
by Lemma
5.3
.
If
\dim T=3
, then
\mathcal{N}_{1}(\widetilde{E},\widetilde{X})\subsetneq\mathcal{N}_{1}(\widetilde{X})
contradicts Lemma
5.22
, therefore

\dim T=2
.

Since
\psi
is special, the images of
\widetilde{E},\widetilde{E}^{\prime}
in
T
are either a curve, or
T
itself.
If
\tilde{\psi}(\widetilde{E})
is a curve in
T
, then
\dim\mathcal{N}_{1}(\widetilde{E},\widetilde{X})\leq 1+\rho_{X}-\rho_{T}=3
(see Lemma
2.6
), a contradiction, and
similarly for
\widetilde{E}^{\prime}
. Therefore
\tilde{\psi}(\widetilde{E})=\tilde{\psi}(\widetilde{E}^{\prime})=T
and

\tilde{\psi}
is quasi-elementary, because the general fiber will contain curves
C_{\widetilde{E}}
,
C_{\widetilde{E}^{\prime}}
.

Then Th.
6.1
implies that

\psi
factors as the composition of two rational elementary contractions
X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f}}{{\dasharrow}}Y\stackrel{{\scriptstyle g}}{{\dasharrow}}T

where
\dim Y=3
. Up to composing with a SQM of
Y
, we can assume that
g
is regular.
By taking a resolution
\hat{f}\colon\widehat{X}\to Y
of
f
, we get also a resolution
g\circ\hat{f}\colon\widehat{X}\to T
of
\psi
, hence it must be
\widehat{X}=\widetilde{X}
and
\tilde{\psi}=g\circ\hat{f}
. This implies that
\operatorname{NE}(\hat{f})
is an extremal ray of
\operatorname{NE}(\tilde{\psi})
, which
gives a contradiction, because
\operatorname{NE}(\tilde{\psi})=\langle[C_{\widetilde{E}}],[C_{\widetilde{E}^{\prime}}]\rangle
.
∎

7.5
.

Let
\langle[C_{E}]\rangle+\alpha
be a
2
-dimensional face of
\operatorname{Mov}(X)^{\vee}
, with
\alpha
one-dimensional face of
\operatorname{mov}(X)
(see
7.4
).
Dually this means that both
C_{E}^{\perp}\cap\operatorname{Mov}(X)
and
\alpha^{\perp}\cap\operatorname{Mov}(X)
are facets of
\operatorname{Mov}(X)
, and they intersect in a face
\eta
of
\operatorname{Mov}(X)
of dimension
\rho_{X}-2
.

Let us choose two cones
\eta_{0},\tau\in\operatorname{MCD}(X)
as follows:
\eta_{0}\subset\eta
of dimension
\rho_{X}-2
, and
\tau\subset\alpha^{\perp}\cap\operatorname{Mov}(X)
of dimension
\rho_{X}-1
and containing
\eta_{0}
.

Let
f\colon X\dasharrow Y
be the elementary rational contraction with
\tau=f^{*}(\operatorname{Nef}(Y))
;
f
is of fiber type, because
\alpha\subset\partial\operatorname{mov}(X)
, hence

\tau\subset\partial\operatorname{Eff}(X)
.
Since
\eta_{0}\subset\tau
, there is a contraction
g\colon Y\to Z
such that
(g\circ f)^{*}(\operatorname{Nef}(Z))=\eta_{0}
(see Rem.
2.2
), and
g
is elementary because
\dim\eta_{0}=\rho_{X}-2
. Note that since
\rho_{Z}=\rho_{X}-2\geq 5
, we have
\dim Z\in\{2,3\}
.

Let
X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\xi}}{{\dasharrow}}\widetilde{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\tilde{f}}}{{\to}}Y
be a
K
-negative resolution of
f

and

\varphi\colon\widehat{X}\to Z
a
K
-negative resolution of
g\circ f
.
We have
\eta_{0}\subset C_{E}^{\perp}
, thus
\varphi
contracts
C_{E}
, moreover

\varphi
is
K
-negative: by Lemma
5.7
it factors through an elementary contraction
\sigma\colon\widehat{X}\to W
, of type
(3,2)
, with exceptional divisor the transform
\widehat{E}\subset\widehat{X}
of
E
.
We have a diagram:

\textstyle{{\widetilde{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{f}}
\textstyle{{\widehat{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi}
\scriptstyle{\sigma}
\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g}
\textstyle{Z}
\textstyle{W\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{h}

We also note that
\dim\mathcal{N}_{1}(\widehat{E},\widehat{X})=\dim\mathcal{N}_{1}(E,X)=\rho_{X}-1
by Lemma
5.3
.

7.6
.

Since
\sigma
is of type
(3,2)
and
h
is elementary of fiber type, both
W
and
Z
are
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial. Moreover
\tilde{f}
is elementary of fiber type,
Y
is
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial too, and
g
cannot be small.

We show that
g
is of fiber type. By contradiction, if
g
is divisorial, then there is a prime divisor
D\subset\widetilde{X}
such that
\tilde{f}(D)=\operatorname{Exc}(g)
and hence
\operatorname{codim}g(\tilde{f}(D))>1
. If
\widehat{D}\subset\widehat{X}
is the transform of
D
, then
h(\sigma(\widehat{D}))=g(\tilde{f}(D))
, thus it must be
\operatorname{codim}\sigma(\widehat{D})>1
and
\widehat{D}=\widehat{E}
. On the other hand
\dim\varphi(\widehat{D})\leq 1
, therefore
\dim\mathcal{N}_{1}(\widehat{D},\widehat{X})\leq 3
(see Lemma
2.6
), a contradiction.

We conclude that
g
is of fiber type,
\dim Y=3
,
\dim Z=2
, and the composition
g\circ\tilde{f}
is quasi-elementary. This implies that
\sigma(\widehat{E})
dominates
Z
; moreover
Z
is a smooth del Pezzo surface (Lemma
3.20
).

In particular
f\colon X\dasharrow Y
is a rational contraction onto a
3
-fold, and we get
\rho_{X}\leq 9
by Th.
1.5
.

7.7
.

Let us consider the
2
-dimensional cone
\operatorname{NE}(\varphi)=\operatorname{NE}(\sigma)+R
, where
R
is an extremal ray of
\operatorname{NE}(\widehat{X})
.
We have
\mathcal{N}_{1}(\widehat{E},\widehat{X})\subsetneq\mathcal{N}_{1}(\widehat{X})
while
\varphi_{*}(\mathcal{N}_{1}(\widehat{E},\widehat{X}))=\mathcal{N}_{1}(\varphi(\widehat{E}),Z)=\mathcal{N}_{1}(Z)
, therefore
\ker\varphi_{*}\not\subset\mathcal{N}_{1}(\widehat{E},\widehat{X})
. On the other hand
\operatorname{NE}(\sigma)\subset\mathcal{N}_{1}(\widehat{E},\widehat{X})
, therefore we must have

R\not\subset\mathcal{N}_{1}(\widehat{E},\widehat{X})
. We also have
\widehat{E}\cdot\operatorname{NE}(\sigma)<0
, and there are curves
C\subset\widehat{X}
contracted by
\varphi
such that
\widehat{E}\cdot C>0
, therefore
\widehat{E}\cdot R>0
.

Let
F\subset\widehat{X}
be a non-trivial fiber of the contraction of
R
, so that
\mathcal{N}_{1}(F,\widehat{X})=\mathbb{R}R
. Then
\widehat{E}\cap F\neq\emptyset
(because
\widehat{E}\cdot R>0
) and
\dim(\widehat{E}\cap F)=0
(because
R\not\subset\mathcal{N}_{1}(\widehat{E},\widehat{X})
), hence
\dim F=1
.
Since
\varphi
is
K
-negative, this implies that
R
cannot be small (Lemma
2.13
). Then there are no small rays in
\operatorname{NE}(\varphi)
, therefore
\widetilde{X}=\widehat{X}
,
\varphi=g\circ\tilde{f}
,
R=\operatorname{NE}(\tilde{f})
, and we get a diagram as (
7.2
).
Moreover

\tilde{f}
is finite on
\widetilde{E}
because
\operatorname{NE}(\tilde{f})\not\subset\mathcal{N}_{1}(\widetilde{E},\widetilde{X})
, hence
every fiber of
\tilde{f}
has dimension one, so that
\tilde{f}
is a conic bundle and
Y
is smooth (see
[
Wiś91a
, Th. (1.2)]
), and
Y
is weak Fano by
[
CS24
, Cor. 6.4]
.

We show that
\sigma
is of type
(3,2)^{\text{\it sm}}
. By Lemma
2.9
, we have to show that every fiber of
\sigma
has dimension
\leq 1
. If
\sigma
had some
2
-dimensional fiber
F_{0}
, then
\tilde{f}(F_{0})\subset Y
would be a
2
-dimensional fiber of
g
, which is impossible. This also implies that
W
is smooth.

By Th.-Def.
5.1
, there is an elementary contraction
\sigma^{\prime}\colon X\to W^{\prime}
of type
(3,2)
with exceptional divisor
E
, and
W^{\prime}
is Fano; moreover
\sigma^{\prime}
and
\sigma
are locally isomorphic, therefore
E\subset\operatorname{dom}\xi
where
\xi\colon X\dasharrow\widetilde{X}
is the SQM,
\sigma^{\prime}
is of type
(3,2)^{\text{\it sm}}
too, and
W^{\prime}
is smooth. This concludes the proof of Th.
7.1
.
∎

We conclude this section with some technical lemmas that follow from our previous results, and are needed in the sequel.

Lemma 7.8

(refined version of
[
Cas22
]
, Lemma 7.2)
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{X}\geq 7
, and
D\subset X
a fixed prime divisor of type
(3,0)^{Q}
. Suppose that there are two fixed prime divisors
E_{1},E_{2}
, of type
(3,2)
, both adjacent to
D
.
Then
X
has a rational contraction onto a
3
-fold.

Proof.

Note that
X
is not isomorphic to a product of surfaces, because it has a fixed prime divisor of type
(3,0)^{Q}
, hence
\delta_{X}\leq 1
by Th.
1.9
. Then

[
Cas22
, proof of Lemma 7.2]
shows that either

\mathcal{N}_{1}(E_{i},X)\subsetneq\mathcal{N}_{1}(X)
for some
i\in\{1,2\}
, or
X
has
a rational contraction onto a
3
-fold, thus the statement follows from Th.
7.1
.
∎

Lemma 7.9

(refined version of
[
Cas22
]
, Lemma 5.13)
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{X}\geq 7
, not isomorphic to a product of surfaces, and
f\colon X\dasharrow S
a rational contraction onto a surface with
\rho_{S}=1
. Suppose that there is a unique prime divisor in
X
contracted to a point by
f
. Then there is a fixed prime divisor
E\subset X
of type
(3,2)
such that
\mathcal{N}_{1}(E,X)\subsetneq\mathcal{N}_{1}(X)
.

Proof.

As in
[
Cas22
, proof of Lemma 5.13]
we see that there is a special rational contraction
g\colon X\dasharrow T
with
\dim T=\rho_{T}=2
. We have
\rho_{X}-\rho_{T}\geq 5
while
d_{g}\leq 4
by Th.
4.5
(see Def.-Rem.
3.2
), hence
g
is not quasi-elementary,
and the statement follows from [
ibid.
, Lemma 5.12].
∎

Proposition 7.10

(refined version of
[
Cas22
]
, Prop. 7.1)
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{X}\geq 7
,
D
a fixed prime divisor of type
(3,1)^{\text{\it sm}}
or
(3,0)^{Q}
, and
X\dasharrow\widetilde{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sigma}}{{\to}}Y
the associated contraction as in Th.-Def.
5.1
. Suppose that
Y
contains a nef prime divisor
H
covered by a family of rational curves of anticanonical degree one, and such that
H\cap\sigma(\operatorname{Exc}\sigma)=\emptyset
.
Then one of the following holds:

(i)

X
has a (regular) contraction onto a
3
-fold, that sends
D
to a point;

(ii)

there is a fixed prime divisor
E\subset X
of type
(3,2)
such that
\mathcal{N}_{1}(E,X)\subsetneq\mathcal{N}_{1}(X)
.

Proof.

We note that since
X
contains a fixed prime divisor of type
(3,1)^{\text{\it sm}}
or
(3,0)^{Q}
,
X
is not a product of surfaces, and
\delta_{X}\leq 1
by Th.
1.9
.

We follow
[
Cas22
, proof of Prop. 7.1]
, which shows the existence of a commutative diagram:

\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi}
\scriptstyle{h}
\textstyle{{\widetilde{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma}
\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g}
\textstyle{Z}

where
g
and
h
are contractions, and
h(D)=g(\sigma(\operatorname{Exc}(\sigma)))=\{\text{\it pt}\}
.

We show that
g
and
h
are of fiber type.
If
D
is
(3,1)^{\text{\it sm}}
, then
Y
is a smooth Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{Y}\geq 6
, and
\delta_{Y}\leq 2
by Lemma
5.18
. Now if
[C]\in\operatorname{mov}(Y)
, then
g
is of fiber type. Otherwise as in [
ibid.
, proof of Prop. 7.1] we see that
[C]
generates an extremal ray of type
(3,2)
of
\operatorname{NE}(Y)
, with locus
E_{1}
, and there is another
fixed prime divisor
E_{2}\subset Y
, of type
(3,2)
, such that
E_{2}\cdot C>0
and
[C_{E_{2}}]\in\operatorname{NE}(g)
. Then
E_{1}\cdot C_{E_{2}}>0

[
Cas13b
, Lemma 2.2(b)]
, hence
[C]+[C_{E_{2}}]\in\operatorname{mov}(Y)

[
Cas20
, Lemma 4.6 and its proof]
, and again
g

is of fiber type.
The case where

D
is
(3,0)^{Q}
is treated in
[
Cas22
, proof of Prop. 7.1]
.

Therefore
g
and
h
are of fiber type, and
[
ibid.
, proof of Prop. 7.1] shows that
\dim Z>1
.
If
\dim Z=3
we have
(i)
, while
if
\dim Z=2
, then by [
ibid.
, Lemma 5.14] we have

\rho_{Z}=1
and
D
is the unique prime divisor contracted to a point by
h
. Hence Lemma
7.9
gives
(ii)
.
∎

8.
Fano
4
-folds with no small contractions and blow-ups of cubic
4
-folds

In this section we consider Fano
4
-folds with no small contraction, and show the following result (Th.
1.2
from the Introduction).

Theorem 8.1
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{X}\geq 7
, not isomorphic to a product of surfaces, and without small elementary contractions.

Then
\rho_{X}\leq 9
and there are a cubic
4
-fold
Z\subset\mathbb{P}^{5}
with at most isolated ordinary double points, and
s=\rho_{X}-1
distinct planes
A_{1},\dotsc,A_{s}\subset Z
intersecting pairwise in a point, such that
X
is obtained from
Z
by blowing-up one plane
A_{i}
and successively the transforms of the other ones.
11
11

11

The resulting
4
-fold does not depend on the order of the blow-ups, see Lemma
2.10
.

We have
\operatorname{Sing}(Z)\subset\cup_{i}A_{i}
;
12
12

12

The blow-up of the surfaces
A_{i}
resolves the ordinary double points, see Rem
2.8
.
for each
i<j
the point
A_{i}\cap A_{j}
is in
Z_{\text{\it reg}}
, and
A_{i}\cap A_{j}\cap A_{k}=\emptyset
for
i<j<k
.

Let us note that products of del Pezzo surfaces do not have small elementary contractions.
Moreover the condition
\rho_{X}\geq 7
is necessary in Th.
8.1
, as the following example shows.

Example 8.2
.

Let
X
be a Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{X}=6
and
\delta_{X}=3
which is not a product of surfaces; there are 9 such families, see Ex.
4.6
. Then
X
has no small elementary contraction. Indeed there is a quasi-elementary contraction
f\colon X\to S
where
S
is a surface with
\rho_{S}=2
; the cone
\operatorname{NE}(f)
is described in
[
CRS22
, §6]
, and it does not contain small extremal rays. On the other hand, if
\operatorname{NE}(X)
had a small extremal ray
R\not\subset\operatorname{NE}(f)
, then
f
should be finite on
\operatorname{Locus}(R)
which is a union of exceptional planes (see Lemma
2.13
), but this is impossible because
\rho_{S}=2
.

As remarked in the Introduction, we do not know whether a Fano
4
-fold as in Th.
8.1
exists, see §
11.7
and Question
11.6
. See also §
11.7
for the geometry of the blow-up of a smooth cubic
4
-fold along two planes intersecting in a point; in particular we show that it is Fano.

Remark 8.3
.

Let
X
be as in Th.
8.1
, and consider the blow-up
f\colon X\to Y
of the last surface
S\subset Y
. The proof of Th.
8.1
(in particular Cor.
8.21
) will show that
Y
is Fano with at most isolated ordinary double points lying in
S
, and
S
is a smooth del Pezzo surface with
\rho_{S}=\rho_{Y}=\rho_{X}-1\in\{6,7,8\}
. Moreover
\operatorname{NE}(S)\cong\operatorname{NE}(Y)
under the natural map given by the inclusion
S\hookrightarrow Y
, and
\operatorname{Eff}(Y)\cong\operatorname{NE}(S)
under the restriction. Every conic bundle
S\to\mathbb{P}^{1}
extends to a contraction
Y\to\mathbb{P}^{2}
sending
S
to a line, and every birational map
S\to\mathbb{P}^{2}
extends to a blow-up map
Y\to Z^{\prime}
, where
Z^{\prime}
is a cubic
4
-fold, sending
S
to a plane.

8.4
Overview of the proof of Th.
8.1
.

We show first of all that if
X\to T
is a non-trivial contraction of fiber type, then
T\cong\mathbb{P}^{2}
(Lemma
8.5
). This relies on both Th.
4.5
, Th.
1.9
, and
[
CS24
]
.

Then we consider birational contractions of
X
.
For the case of an elementary contraction
f\colon X\to Y
,
it is shown in
[
Cas24
]
that
f
is the blow-up of a smooth del Pezzo surface
S\subset Y
with
K_{S}=K_{Y|S}
, and
Y
is Fano with at most isolated ordinary double points (Prop.
8.7
).

We first generalize this description to every birational contraction
\sigma\colon X\to Z
(Prop.
8.8
):
\sigma
is the blow-up of smooth del Pezzo surfaces
A_{1},\dotsc,A_{\rho_{X}-\rho_{Z}}\subset Z
that intersect pairwise transversally
in (at most) finitely many points, and
Z
is Fano with at most isolated ordinary double points. Moreover for every
i=1,\dotsc,\rho_{X}-\rho_{Z}
we have

K_{A_{i}}=K_{Z|A_{i}}
,
and if
E_{i}\subset X
is the exceptional divisor over
A_{i}
,
it follows from Th.
7.1
that
\mathcal{N}_{1}(E_{i},X)=\mathcal{N}_{1}(X)
and hence
\mathcal{N}_{1}(A_{i},Z)=\mathcal{N}_{1}(Z)
, so that
\rho_{A_{i}}\geq\rho_{Z}
. This also implies that
E_{i}\cap E_{j}\neq\emptyset
and hence
A_{i}\cap A_{j}\neq\emptyset
for every
i<j
.

Let us fix
i
and factor
\sigma
as
X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sigma_{i}}}{{\to}}Y_{i}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle h_{i}}}{{\to}}Z
, where
h_{i}
blows-up all the surfaces
A_{j}
for
j\neq i
,
\rho_{Y_{i}}=\rho_{X}-1
, and
\sigma_{i}
blows-up the transform
S_{i}\subset Y_{i}
of
A_{i}
. Then
h_{i|S_{i}}\colon S_{i}\to A_{i}
blows-up all the points where
A_{i}
intersects the other surfaces
A_{j}
,
j\neq i
. On the other hand,
S_{i}
is del Pezzo, thus
\rho_{S_{i}}\leq 9
. This allows to bound both
\rho_{A_{i}}
and the cardinality of
A_{i}\cap A_{j}
(Rem.
8.19
), and is used repeatedly.

Then we show that, if for some
i\in\{1,\dotsc,\rho_{X}-\rho_{Z}\}
we have
\rho_{A_{i}}=\rho_{Z}\geq 3
, then
\operatorname{NE}(A_{i})\cong\operatorname{NE}(Z)
, and one can extend contractions from
A_{i}
to
Z
(Cor.
8.21
).

We use all these preliminary results to prove that
there exists a birational contraction
X\to Z
with
\rho_{Z}=1
and such that one of the blown-up surfaces, say
A_{1}\subset Z
, also has
\rho_{A_{1}}=1

(Lemma
8.23
).
With this we can finally show Th.
8.1
, let us give an outline.

We have
A_{1}\cong\mathbb{P}^{2}
,
K_{Z|A_{1}}=K_{A_{1}}
, and we show that the restriction
\operatorname{Pic}(Z)\to\operatorname{Pic}(A_{1})
is an isomorphism. This implies that
Z
is Fano with index
3
, namely a del Pezzo
4
-fold, and these
4
-folds are classified. Let
H\in\operatorname{Pic}(Z)
be such that
-K_{Z}=3H
, and set
d:=H^{4}
; then
h^{0}(Z,-K_{Z})=15d+10
(see
8.30
).

Next we show that there are two other blown-up surfaces, say
A_{2}
and
A_{3}
, that intersect
A_{1}
only in one point, trasversally. If
Z^{\prime}\to Z
is the blow-up of
A_{2}
and
A_{3}
, and
A_{1}^{\prime}\subset Z^{\prime}
is the transform of
A_{1}
, we have
A_{1}^{\prime}\cong\operatorname{Bl}_{2\mskip 1.0mu\text{\it pts}}\mathbb{P}^{2}
and
\rho_{A_{1}^{\prime}}=\rho_{Z^{\prime}}=3
(see
8.28
), and hence
\operatorname{NE}(A_{1}^{\prime})\cong\operatorname{NE}(Z^{\prime})
by the previous part of the proof; we also compute that
h^{0}(Z^{\prime},-K_{Z^{\prime}})\in\{15d-10,\dotsc,15d-6\}
. Then we extend the contraction
A_{1}^{\prime}\to\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
to an elementary birational contraction
Z^{\prime}\to W
which is again the blow-up of a surface;
W
is a Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{W}=2
and index
2
. By studying the contractions of
W
, we show that
W
is a double cover of
\mathbb{P}^{2}\times\mathbb{P}^{2}
with branch divisor of degree
(2,2)
(see
8.34
). This gives
h^{0}(W,-K_{W})=45
, and allows to compute that
h^{0}(Z^{\prime},-K_{Z^{\prime}})=36
and that
d=3
, so that
Z
is a cubic
4
-fold, each
A_{i}
is a plane, and
A_{i}\cap A_{j}
is just one point for every
i<j
.
We also get
h^{0}(Z,-K_{Z})=55
and we can compute
h^{0}(X,-K_{X})
in terms of the number of blown-up planes, that are
\rho_{X}-1
. Finally, using that
h^{0}(X,-K_{X})\geq 0
, we get
\rho_{X}\leq 9
(see
8.35
).

Lemma 8.5
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{X}\geq 7
, not isomorphic to a product of surfaces, and without small elementary contractions.

Let
f\colon X\dasharrow Y
be a non-trivial rational contraction of fiber type. Then
f
is regular and equidimensional, and
Y\cong\mathbb{P}^{2}
.

Proof.

Since
X
has no small elementary contraction,
f
is regular. Let
F\subset X
be a general fiber; we have
\dim\mathcal{N}_{1}(F,X)=d_{f}\leq 4
by Th.
4.5
(see Def.-Rem.
3.2
). In particular
F
cannot be a divisor because
\delta_{X}\leq 1
(Th.
1.9
) and
\rho_{X}\geq 7
, hence
\dim Y>1
.

Suppose by contradiction that
\dim Y=3
. Then by Prop.
3.12

f
factors through a special rational contraction
f^{\prime}\colon X\dasharrow Y^{\prime}
with
\dim Y^{\prime}=3
. As above
f^{\prime}
is regular, and
\rho_{Y^{\prime}}\leq 3
by Lemma
2.16
. On the other hand
\rho_{X}-\rho_{Y^{\prime}}\leq 2
by
[
CS24
, Lemma 3.12]
, and we have a contradiction.

We conclude that
\dim Y=2
. As above we can factor
f
as
X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f^{\prime}}}{{\to}}Y^{\prime}\to Y
where
f^{\prime}
is equidimensional and
\dim Y^{\prime}=2
; in particular
Y^{\prime}
is a smooth rational surface (Lemma
3.19
). If
\rho_{Y^{\prime}}>1
, then there is a morphism
Y^{\prime}\to\mathbb{P}^{1}
, and the composition
X\to\mathbb{P}^{1}
contradicts the previous part of the proof. Therefore
\rho_{Y^{\prime}}=1
and
Y\cong Y^{\prime}\cong\mathbb{P}^{2}
.
∎

Corollary 8.6
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{X}\geq 7
, not isomorphic to a product of surfaces, and without small elementary contractions. Let
E\subset X
be a fixed prime divisor. Then
E
is of type
(3,2)
and

\mathcal{N}_{1}(E,X)=\mathcal{N}_{1}(X)
.

Proof.

Since
X
has no small elementary contraction,
E
must be of type
(3,2)
by Th. -Def.
5.1
(c)
. Then
\mathcal{N}_{1}(E,X)=\mathcal{N}_{1}(X)

by Th.
7.1
and Lemma
8.5
.
∎

Proposition 8.7

(refinement of
[
Cas24
]
, Th. 4.1)
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{X}\geq 7
, not isomorphic to a product of surfaces, and without small elementary contractions.

Let
f\colon X\to Y
be an elementary contraction.
Then

f
is of type
(3,2)
,

Y
is Fano with at most isolated ordinary double points, and
S:=f(\operatorname{Exc}(f))\subset Y
is a smooth del Pezzo surface with
K_{S}=K_{Y|S}
.

Proof.

The statement follows from
[
Cas24
, Th. 4.1]
. Note that this reference
has the assumption
\rho_{X}\geq 8
, but this is used only to show that a contraction
g\colon X\to Z
with
\rho_{X}-\rho_{Z}\leq 3
cannot be of fiber type. In our setting this follows from Lemma
8.5
, and the same proof applies under the assumption
\rho_{X}\geq 7
.
Finally
Y
is Fano by Th.-Def.
5.1
, and
has at most isolated ordinary double points by Lemma
2.9
.
∎

Proposition 8.8
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{X}\geq 7
, not isomorphic to a product of surfaces, and without small elementary contractions.

Let
\sigma\colon X\to Z
be a birational contraction; then
\operatorname{NE}(\sigma)=R_{1}+\cdots+R_{s}
with
R_{i}
extremal ray of type
(3,2)
of
\operatorname{NE}(X)
for every
i=1,\dotsc,s
, and
s=\rho_{X}-\rho_{Z}
. Set
E_{i}:=\operatorname{Locus}(R_{i})
.
The following holds:

(a)

Z
is Fano with at most isolated ordinary double points, and
A_{i}:=\sigma(E_{i})
is a smooth del Pezzo surface with
K_{A_{i}}=K_{Z|A_{i}}
and
\mathcal{N}_{1}(A_{i},Z)=\mathcal{N}_{1}(Z)
for every
i=1,\dotsc,s
;

(b)

for every
i\neq j
the surfaces
A_{i}
and
A_{j}
intersect transversally in finitely many points where
Z
is regular, while
A_{i}\cap A_{j}\cap A_{k}=\emptyset
if
i<j<k
. Each singular point of
Z
is contained in some
A_{i}
. Moreover
\operatorname{Exc}(\sigma)=E_{1}\cup\cdots\cup E_{s}
and
\sigma
is obtained
by blowing-up one surface
A_{i}
and successively the transforms of the other ones, in any order;

(c)

every elementary contraction of
Z
is either of type
(3,2)
, or
Z\to\mathbb{P}^{2}
.

Proof.

8.9
.

By Prop.
8.7
each
R_{i}
is of type
(3,2)
, and
if
\sigma_{i}\colon X\to Y_{i}
is the contraction of
R_{i}
, then
Y_{i}
has at most isolated ordinary double points, and
S_{i}:=\sigma_{i}(E_{i})
is a smooth del Pezzo surface with
K_{S_{i}}=K_{Y_{i}|S_{i}}
.

By
[
Cas24
, Lemma 2.5]
we also have
E_{i}\cdot R_{j}=0
for every
i\neq j
. In particular
[C_{E_{1}}],\dotsc,[C_{E_{s}}]\in\mathcal{N}_{1}(X)
are linearly independent, and

\operatorname{NE}(\sigma)
is a simplicial cone of dimension
s=\rho_{X}-\rho_{Z}
.

8.10
.

Clearly
E_{i}\subset\operatorname{Exc}(\sigma)
for every
i=1,\dotsc,s
. Conversely if
C\subset X
is an irreducible curve with
[C]\in\operatorname{NE}(\sigma)
,
we have
C\equiv\sum_{j=1}^{s}\lambda_{j}C_{E_{j}}
with
\lambda_{j}\in\mathbb{Q}_{\geq 0}
.
Then for every
i=1,\dotsc,s
we have
E_{i}\cdot C=-\lambda_{i}\leq 0
, and either
C\subset E_{i}
, or
\lambda_{i}=0
. Therefore
either
[C]\in R_{i}
for some
i\in\{1,\dotsc,s\}
, or
C\subset E_{i}\cap E_{j}
for some
i\neq j
. In particular
\operatorname{Exc}(\sigma)=E_{1}\cup\cdots\cup E_{s}
.

Moreover, on the open subset
X\smallsetminus\cup_{j\neq i}E_{j}
,
\sigma
coincides with
\sigma_{i}
, therefore
\dim A_{i}=2
,
A_{i}\smallsetminus\cup_{j\neq i}A_{j}
is smooth, and
Z
has at most isolated ordinary double points at
A_{i}\smallsetminus\cup_{j\neq i}A_{j}
. We also note that
\mathcal{N}_{1}(E_{i},X)=\mathcal{N}_{1}(X)
by Cor.
8.6
, hence
\mathcal{N}_{1}(A_{i},Z)=\sigma_{*}(\mathcal{N}_{1}(E_{i},X))=\mathcal{N}_{1}(Z)
, for every
i=1,\dotsc,s
.

8.11
.

We show that
\sigma
factors as the composition of
s
elementary divisorial contractions of type
(3,2)
with exceptional divisors
E_{1},\dotsc,E_{s}
, in arbitrary order, and
Z
is
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial. We proceed by induction on
s
, the statement being clear for
s=1
. Consider a facet of
\operatorname{NE}(\sigma)
, up to renumbering we can assume that it is
R_{1}+\cdots+R_{s-1}
, and factor
\sigma
as:

\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma}
\scriptstyle{\sigma^{\prime}}
\textstyle{{Z^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{Z}

where
\operatorname{NE}(\sigma^{\prime})=R_{1}+\cdots+R_{s-1}
. Then the statement holds for
\sigma^{\prime}
by induction,
Z^{\prime}
is
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial, and
f
is an elementary birational contraction with exceptional locus
\sigma^{\prime}(E_{s})
, therefore
f
is divisorial and
Z
is
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial. Moreover
f
is of type
(3,2)
because
\dim A_{s}=2
. This gives the statement.

8.12
.

If
g\colon Z\to W
is an elementary contraction, we can consider the composition
g\circ\sigma\colon X\to W
. If
g
is of fiber type, then
W\cong\mathbb{P}^{2}
by Lemma
8.5
. If instead
g
is birational, then it is of type
(3,2)
by
8.11
. This gives
(c)
.

8.13
.

Let
i,j\in\{1,\dotsc,s\}
with
i<j
. We have
E_{i}\cap E_{j}\neq\emptyset
by Cor.
8.6
and Rem.
2.7
. Moreover every connected component
of
E_{i}\cap E_{j}
is isomorphic to
\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
with the rulings being curves in
R_{i}
and
R_{j}
(see
[
Cas22
, Lemma 4.15]
and
[
Cas24
, Prop. 3.4]
). Therefore if
C\subset X
is an irreducible curve contained in
E_{i}\cap E_{j}
, we have
[C]\in R_{i}+R_{j}
. Together with
8.10
, this implies that for every irreducible curve
C\subset X
contracted by
\sigma
we have
[C]\in R_{i}+R_{j}
for some
i<j
.

Let
\sigma_{ij}\colon X\to Y_{ij}
be the contraction of
R_{i}+R_{j}
, and
set
B_{i}:=\sigma_{ij}(E_{i})
,
B_{j}:=\sigma_{ij}(E_{j})
.
The behaviour of
\sigma_{ij}
is analyzed in
[
Cas24
, Prop. 3.4 and its proof]
, where it is shown that:

(1)

\dim(B_{i}\cap B_{j})=0
and
E_{i}\cap E_{j}=\sigma_{ij}^{-1}(B_{i}\cap B_{j})
;

(2)

if
B_{i}\cap B_{j}=\{y_{1},\dotsc,y_{m}\}
, then
B_{i}
,
B_{j}
, and
Y_{ij}
are smooth at each
y_{a}
, and the fiber
T_{a}:=\sigma_{ij}^{-1}(y_{a})
is an isolated
2
-dimensional fiber of
\sigma_{ij}
and a connected component of
E_{i}\cap E_{j}
;

(3)

if
we factor
\sigma_{ij}
as

X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sigma_{i}}}{{\longrightarrow}}Y_{i}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle g}}{{\longrightarrow}}Y_{ij}

where
g
is an elementary contraction of type
(3,2)
with exceptional divisor
E_{j}^{\prime}:=\sigma_{i}(E_{j})
, then
(E_{j}^{\prime})_{|S_{i}}=C_{1}+\cdots+C_{m}
(recall that

S_{i}=\sigma_{i}(E_{i})\subset Y_{i}
, see
8.9
)
where
C_{1},\dotsc,C_{m}
are pairwise disjoint
(-1)
-curves in
S_{i}
, and fibers of
g
; moreover
C_{a}=\sigma_{i}(T_{a})
and
C_{a}\subset(Y_{i})_{\text{\it reg}}
for every
a=1,\dotsc,m
.

In particular, locally around
y_{1},\dotsc,y_{m}
,
g
is just the blow-up of the smooth surface
B_{j}
in the smooth
4
-fold
Y_{ij}
, thus the restriction
g_{|S_{i}}\colon S_{i}\to B_{i}
is the blow-up of
B_{i}
at the scheme intersection
B_{i}\cap B_{j}
. Since
S_{i}
is smooth (see
8.9
), this intersection must be reduced.

8.14
.

Let
k\in\{1,\dotsc,s\}
,
k\neq i,j
, and let
T
be a connected component of
E_{i}\cap E_{j}
; note that
\mathcal{N}_{1}(T,X)=\mathbb{R}R_{i}\oplus\mathbb{R}R_{j}
is
2
-dimensional. If
T\cap E_{k}\neq\emptyset
, then
T\subset E_{k}
, because
E_{k}\cdot R_{i}=E_{k}\cdot R_{j}=0
. Then the contraction
\sigma_{k}
of
R_{k}
must be finite on
T
, and
\sigma_{k}(T)=S_{k}\subset Y_{k}
.
This gives

\mathcal{N}_{1}(S_{k},Y_{k})=(\sigma_{k})_{*}(\mathcal{N}_{1}(T,X))
,
\dim\mathcal{N}_{1}(S_{k},Y_{k})\leq 2
,
and
\dim\mathcal{N}_{1}(E_{k},X)\leq 1+\dim\mathcal{N}_{1}(S_{k},Y_{k})\leq 3

(see Lemma
2.6
), a contradiction. Hence
E_{i}\cap E_{j}\cap E_{k}=\emptyset
and
A_{i}\cap A_{j}\cap A_{k}=\emptyset
.

On the open subset
X\smallsetminus\cup_{k\neq i,j}E_{k}
,
\sigma
coincides with
\sigma_{ij}
, and
A_{i}\cap A_{j}\subset\sigma(X\smallsetminus\cup_{k\neq i,j}E_{k})
. We conclude from
8.13

that every

A_{i}
is smooth,
A_{i}
and
A_{j}
intersect transversally at finitely many points where
Z
is smooth, and
Z
has at most isolated ordinary double points contained in some
A_{i}
; in particular
Z
has locally factorial, terminal singularities (see Rem.
2.8
). We have shown
(b)
.

8.15
.

We have
-K_{X}+E_{1}+\cdots+E_{s}=\sigma^{*}(-K_{Z})
. If
R
is an extremal ray of
\operatorname{NE}(X)
different from
R_{1},\dotsc,R_{s}
, then
E_{i}\cdot R\geq 0
for every
i=1,\dotsc,s
by
[
Cas24
, Lemma 2.3]
, thus
-K_{X}+E_{1}+\cdots+E_{s}
is nef and
(-K_{X}+E_{1}+\cdots+E_{s})^{\perp}\cap\operatorname{NE}(X)=\operatorname{NE}(\sigma)
. This implies that
-K_{Z}
is ample, namely
Z
is Fano.

8.16
.

Fix
i\in\{1,\dotsc,s\}
. We have a factorization:

(8.17)

\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma}
\scriptstyle{\sigma_{i}}
\textstyle{{Y_{i}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{h_{i}}
\textstyle{Z;}

set
E^{\prime}_{j}:=\sigma_{i}(E_{j})\subset Y_{i}
for every
j\neq i
. Then
h_{i|S_{i}}\colon S_{i}\to A_{i}
is a blow-up of smooth points, with exceptional locus
\cup_{j\neq i}E^{\prime}_{j|S_{i}}
, which is a union of pairwise disjoint
(-1)
-curves
\Gamma_{a}
(see
8.13
(3)). We have
K_{S_{i}}=(h_{i|S_{i}})^{*}K_{A_{i}}+\sum_{a}\Gamma_{a}
and
K_{Y_{i}}=h_{i}^{*}K_{Z}+\sum_{j\neq i}E_{j}^{\prime}
; then
K_{S_{i}}=K_{Y_{i}|S_{i}}
(see
8.9
) implies that
(h_{i|S_{i}})^{*}K_{A_{i}}=(h_{i|S_{i}})^{*}K_{Z|A_{i}}
and finally that
K_{A_{i}}=K_{Z|A_{i}}
, so we have
(a)
.∎

Remark 8.18
.

In the setting of Prop.
8.8
, let
g\colon Z\to W
be an elementary birational contraction.
Then
B:=g(\operatorname{Exc}(g))\subset W
is a smooth del Pezzo surface and

h^{0}(Z,-K_{Z})=h^{0}(W,-K_{W})-11+\rho_{B}.

Indeed by Prop.
8.8
both
Z
and
W
are Fano with locally factorial and terminal singularities, so that
h^{i}(Z,-K_{Z})=0
for
i>0
and
h^{0}(Z,-K_{Z})=\chi(Z,-K_{Z})
, and similarly for
W
. Moreover, still by Prop.
8.8
applied to
g\circ\sigma\colon X\to W
, we have that
B
is a smooth del Pezzo surface with
K_{B}=K_{W|B}
, hence the formula follows from Lemma
2.11
and
\chi(B,-K_{B})=h^{0}(B,-K_{B})=11-\rho_{B}
.

Remark 8.19
.

In the setting of Prop.
8.8
, set
m_{ij}:=\#(A_{i}\cap A_{j})
for every
i,j=1,\dotsc,\rho_{X}-\rho_{Z}
,
i\neq j
; note that
m_{ij}
is also the number of connected components of
E_{i}\cap E_{j}
in
X
.

Let
i\in\{1,\dotsc,\rho_{X}-\rho_{Z}\}
. The blow-up of
A_{i}
at the points of intersection with
\cup_{j\neq i}A_{j}
is the del Pezzo surface
S_{i}\subset Y_{i}
as in (
8.17
), and
\rho_{S_{i}}\leq 9
, therefore:

(8.20)

9\geq\rho_{A_{i}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j=1\\
j\neq i\end{subarray}}^{\rho_{X}-\rho_{Z}}m_{ij}\geq\rho_{A_{i}}+\rho_{X}-\rho_{Z}-1.

Corollary 8.21
.

In the setting of Prop.
8.8
, suppose that
\rho_{Z}\geq 3
and
\rho_{A_{i}}=\rho_{Z}
for some
i\in\{1,\dotsc,\rho_{X}-\rho_{Z}\}
. Let
\iota\colon A_{i}\hookrightarrow Z
be the inclusion.
Then
\iota_{*}\colon\mathcal{N}_{1}(A_{i})\to\mathcal{N}_{1}(Z)
is an isomorphism such that
\iota_{*}(\operatorname{NE}(A_{i}))=\operatorname{NE}(Z)
. For every
(-1)
-curve
C\subset A_{i}
there exists a (unique) extremal ray
R
of
\operatorname{NE}(Z)
such that
[C]\in R
; moreover
R
is of type
(3,2)
and
E_{R|A_{i}}=C
, where
E_{R}:=\operatorname{Locus}(R)
.

Proof.

We have
K_{A_{i}}=K_{Z|A_{i}}
and
\mathcal{N}_{1}(A_{i},Z)=\mathcal{N}_{1}(Z)
by Prop.
8.8
(a)
. In particular
\iota_{*}\colon\mathcal{N}_{1}(A_{i})\to\mathcal{N}_{1}(Z)
is surjective, thus it is an isomorphism because
\rho_{A_{i}}=\rho_{Z}
.

We show that
\iota_{*}(\operatorname{NE}(A_{i}))=\operatorname{NE}(Z)
. The inclusion

\subseteq

is clear. Conversely, let
R
be an extremal ray of
\operatorname{NE}(Z)
. Then
R
is of type
(3,2)
by Prop.
8.8
(c)
and because
\rho_{Z}\geq 3
. By the proof of Prop.
8.8
(more precisely
8.13
and
8.14
) we have
E_{R}\cap A_{i}\neq\emptyset
and
E_{R|A_{i}}=C_{1}+\cdots+C_{m}
with
C_{1},\dotsc,C_{m}
pairwise disjoint
(-1)
-curves with
[C_{j}]\in R
, so that
R\in\iota_{*}(\operatorname{NE}(A_{i}))
, and we conclude that
\iota_{*}(\operatorname{NE}(A_{i}))=\operatorname{NE}(Z)
. Moreover
-K_{Z}\cdot C_{j}=-K_{A_{i}}\cdot C_{j}=1
for every
j
, which implies that
C_{j}\equiv C_{1}
in
Z
. Since
\iota_{*}
is injective, we get that
C_{j}\equiv C_{1}
in
A_{i}
, therefore
C_{j}=C_{1}
and
m=1
, because these are
(-1)
-curves.
∎

Lemma 8.22
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{X}\geq 7
, not isomorphic to a product of surfaces, and without small elementary contractions.
If there is a contraction

f\colon X\to\mathbb{P}^{2}
, then
d_{f}\leq 3
.

We recall that
d_{f}=\dim\mathcal{N}_{1}(F,X)
for a general fiber
F\subset X
of
f
, see Def.-Rem.
3.2
.

Proof.

Let us factor
f
as
X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sigma}}{{\to}}Z\stackrel{{\scriptstyle g}}{{\to}}\mathbb{P}^{2}
, with
\rho_{Z}=2
and
g
elementary; let
F\subset X
be a general fiber of
f
and
F_{Z}:=\sigma(F)\subset Z
a general fiber of
g
.

Then
\sigma
is birational by Lemma
8.5
,

g
is elementary of fiber type, and
\dim\mathcal{N}_{1}(F_{Z},Z)=1
.
We apply Prop.
8.8
to
\sigma
;
consider the surfaces
A_{1},\dotsc,A_{\rho_{X}-2}\subset Z
blown-up by
\sigma
.
Up to renumbering
let
A_{1},\dotsc,A_{r}
be those dominating
\mathbb{P}^{2}
under
g
, with
r\in\{0,\dotsc,\rho_{X}-2\}
.
Then

d_{f}=\dim\mathcal{N}_{1}(F,X)=r+\dim\mathcal{N}_{1}(F_{Z},Z)=r+1.

Suppose by contradiction that
d_{f}\geq 4
. Then by Th.
4.5
we have
d_{f}=4
,
r=3
, and
F
is a del Pezzo surface of degree one.
Let us further factor
f
as

X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sigma^{\prime\prime}}}{{\longrightarrow}}Z^{\prime}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sigma^{\prime}}}{{\longrightarrow}}Z\stackrel{{\scriptstyle g}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathbb{P}^{2}

where
\sigma^{\prime}
is the blow-up of
A_{1},A_{2},A_{3}\subset Z
,
\rho_{Z^{\prime}}=5
, and set
F^{\prime}:=\sigma^{\prime\prime}(F)\subset Z^{\prime}
. Then the surfaces blown-up by
\sigma^{\prime\prime}
do not dominate
\mathbb{P}^{2}
, hence
F^{\prime}
is contained in the open subset where
\sigma^{\prime\prime}
is an isomorphism, so that
F^{\prime}\cong F
and
F^{\prime}
is a del Pezzo surface of degree one.
By considering the anticanonical pencil in
F^{\prime}
, we see that
Z^{\prime}
is covered by a proper family
V
of irreducible curves of anticanonical degree one (recall that
Z^{\prime}
is Gorenstein Fano by Prop.
8.8
).

We have
\rho_{Z^{\prime}}<\rho_{X}
, hence
\sigma^{\prime\prime}
is not an isomorphism;
let
B\subset Z^{\prime}
be a surface blown-up by
\sigma^{\prime\prime}
. By
Prop.
8.8
we know that
B
is a smooth del Pezzo surface with
K_{Z^{\prime}|B}=K_{B}
.
Let
p\in B
be a general point. There is an irreducible curve
\Gamma
of the family
V
such that
p\in\Gamma
. Since
-K_{Z^{\prime}}\cdot\Gamma=1
, we must have
\Gamma\subset B
(see
[
CS24
, Prop. 2.10]
); moreover

-K_{B}\cdot\Gamma=-K_{Z^{\prime}}\cdot\Gamma=1
.

We conclude that
B
is covered by curves of anticanonical degree one, hence
\rho_{B}=9
. On the other hand (
8.20
) applied to
\sigma^{\prime\prime}
and
A_{i}=B
gives

9\geq\rho_{B}+\rho_{X}-\rho_{Z^{\prime}}-1=\rho_{X}+3
, a contradiction.
∎

Lemma 8.23
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{X}\geq 7
, not isomorphic to a product of surfaces, and without small elementary contractions.

Then there exists a birational contraction
\sigma\colon X\to Z
with

\rho_{Z}=1
and such that
\rho_{A_{i}}=1
for some
i\in\{1,\dotsc,\rho_{X}-1\}

(notation as in Prop.
8.8
).

Proof.

8.24
.

Let
\tau\colon X\to W
be a contraction with
\rho_{W}=2
; it is birational by Lemma
8.5
, and Prop.
8.8
applies; in particolar
W
is a Fano
4
-fold with at most ordinary double points as singularities, and
\tau
blows-up
\rho_{X}-2\geq 5
surfaces
B_{1},\dotsc,B_{\rho_{X}-2}\subset W
. For every
i=1,\dotsc,\rho_{X}-2
, (
8.20
) gives
9\geq\rho_{B_{i}}+\rho_{X}-3\geq\rho_{B_{i}}+4
, therefore
\rho_{B_{i}}\leq 5
.

Let us consider
the two elementary contractions
g_{j}\colon W\to Z_{j}
of
W
, with

j\in\{1,2\}
, and set
f_{j}:=g_{j}\circ\tau\colon X\to Z_{j}
.

If
g_{j}
is of fiber type, then
by Lemmas
8.5
and
8.22
we have
Z_{j}\cong\mathbb{P}^{2}
,
f_{j}
is equidimensional, and
d_{f_{j}}\leq 3
; moreover as in the proof of Lemma
8.22
we see that at most two surfaces
B_{i}
can dominate
\mathbb{P}^{2}
under
g_{j}
.

If
g_{j}
is birational, then it must be of type
(3,2)
by Prop.
8.8
.
Set
E_{j}:=\operatorname{Exc}(g_{j})\subset W
and
A_{j}:=g_{j}(E_{j})\subset Z_{j}
.
Suppose that
\rho_{A_{j}}>1
; we show that there are at most two indices
i\in\{1,\dotsc,\rho_{X}-2\}
such that
E_{j|B_{i}}
is reducible.

We apply Rem.
8.19
to
f_{j}=g_{j}\circ\tau\colon X\to Z_{j}
and the surface
A_{j}
; the other surfaces blown-up by
f_{j}
are
g_{j}(B_{1}),\dotsc,g_{j}(B_{\rho_{X}-2})
. Set
m_{i}:=\#(g_{j}(B_{i})\cap A_{j})
, and note that this is also the number of components of
E_{j|B_{i}}
in
W
. Then (
8.20
) gives
9\geq\rho_{A_{j}}+\sum_{i=1}^{\rho_{X}-2}m_{i}\geq 2+\sum_{i}m_{i}
, therefore
\sum_{i=1}^{\rho_{X}-2}m_{i}\leq 7
and the sum has
\rho_{X}-2\geq 5
terms, so
we see that there are at most two indices
i\in\{1,\dotsc,\rho_{X}-2\}
such that
m_{i}>1
, namely such that
E_{j|B_{i}}
is reducible.

8.25
.

Suppose that both
g_{1}
and
g_{2}
are of fiber type.

Since
\rho_{X}-2\geq 5
, by
8.24
there is at least one surface, say
B_{1}\subset W
, such that
g_{j}(B_{1})\subsetneq\mathbb{P}^{2}
for
j=1,2
.
On the other hand
g_{j}(B_{1})=f_{j}(E_{1})
cannot be a point because
f_{j}
is equidimensional, therefore
g_{j}(B_{1})
is a curve in
\mathbb{P}^{2}
. Since
B_{1}
is a smooth del Pezzo surface,
g_{j|B_{1}}
factors through a conic bundle
h_{j}\colon B_{1}\to\mathbb{P}^{1}
, such that
h_{1}
and
h_{2}
define a finite map
h\colon B_{1}\to\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
. Since
\rho_{B_{1}}\leq 5
,
[
Dru16
, Prop. 7.2]
implies that
B_{1}\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
.

Similarly as in
8.24
, using (
8.20
) and
\rho_{B_{1}}=2
,
we see that there is at least one index
i\in\{2,\dotsc,\rho_{X}-2\}
such that
\#(B_{1}\cap B_{i})=1
; up to renumbering we can assume
i=2
.
Consider the factorization of
\tau
given by:

X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\tau^{\prime\prime}}}{{\longrightarrow}}W^{\prime}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\tau^{\prime}}}{{\longrightarrow}}W

where

\tau^{\prime}
is the blow-up of
B_{2}
, and let
B_{1}^{\prime}\subset W^{\prime}
be the transform of
B_{1}
. Then
B_{1}^{\prime}\cong\operatorname{Bl}_{2\mskip 1.0mu\text{\it pts}}\mathbb{P}^{2}
, so that
\rho_{B_{1}^{\prime}}=\rho_{W^{\prime}}=3
, and we can apply Cor.
8.21
.
In particular, let
C_{1},C_{2}\subset B_{1}^{\prime}
be the
(-1)
-curves contracted by
B_{1}^{\prime}\to\mathbb{P}^{2}
; then for
i=1,2
the class
[C_{i}]\in\operatorname{NE}(W^{\prime})
generates an extremal ray
R_{i}
of type
(3,2)
, with locus
G_{i}\subset W^{\prime}
such that
G_{i|B_{1}^{\prime}}=C_{i}
.
Moreover

R_{1}+R_{2}
is a face of
\operatorname{NE}(W^{\prime})
, because
\operatorname{NE}(W^{\prime})\cong\operatorname{NE}(B_{1}^{\prime})
.

Let
h\colon W^{\prime}\to T
be the contraction with
\operatorname{NE}(h)=R_{1}+R_{2}
, so that
\rho_{T}=1
.
We have
G_{1}\cdot C_{2}=G_{1|B_{1}^{\prime}}\cdot_{B_{1}^{\prime}}C_{2}=C_{1}\cdot_{B_{1}^{\prime}}C_{2}=0
, and similarly
G_{2}\cdot C_{1}=0
. Then it is easy to see that
\operatorname{Exc}(h)\subset G_{1}\cup G_{2}
, and
h
is birational. Moreover
A_{T}:=h(B_{1}^{\prime})\subset T
is one of the surfaces blown-up by the birational contraction
h\circ\tau^{\prime\prime}\colon X\to T
, and
\rho_{A_{T}}=1
by construction, so we get the statement.

8.26
.

Suppose now that
g_{1}
and
g_{2}
are both birational; recall that
E_{j}=\operatorname{Exc}(g_{j})\subset W
and
A_{j}=g_{j}(E_{j})\subset Z_{j}
for
j=1,2
. We show that
\rho_{A_{j}}=1
for some
j\in\{1,2\}
, which gives the statement.

By contradiction, suppose that
\rho_{A_{j}}>1
for
j=1,2
.
Then, by
8.24
, there are at
most
4
indices
i\in\{1,\dotsc,\rho_{X}-2\}
such that
E_{j|B_{i}}
is reducible for some
j\in\{1,2\}
, and since
\rho_{X}-2\geq 5
,
there exists an index
i
, say
i=1
, such that
E_{j|B_{1}}
is irreducible
for
j=1,2
. Then

E_{j|B_{1}}=C_{j}
is a
(-1)
-curve in
B_{1}
, with
[C_{j}]\in\operatorname{NE}(g_{j})
(see
8.13
(3)).

We have
\operatorname{NE}(W)=\operatorname{NE}(g_{1})+\operatorname{NE}(g_{2})
and
E_{1}\cdot\operatorname{NE}(g_{1})<0
, hence
E_{1}\cdot\operatorname{NE}(g_{2})>0
. Then
C_{1}\cdot_{B_{1}}C_{2}=E_{1|B_{1}}\cdot_{B_{1}}C_{2}=E_{1}\cdot C_{2}>0
, and since

B_{1}
is a smooth del Pezzo surface with
\rho_{B_{1}}\leq 5
(see
8.24
), it is easy to see that it must be
C_{1}\cdot_{B_{1}}C_{2}=1
.
Conversely this implies that
E_{1}\cdot C_{2}=1
, and similarly

E_{2}\cdot C_{1}=1
. This gives
(E_{1}+E_{2})\cdot C_{1}=(E_{1}+E_{2})\cdot C_{2}=0
, which is impossible because
\rho_{W}=2
,
[C_{1}]
and
[C_{2}]
generate
\mathcal{N}_{1}(W)
, and we would get
E_{1}+E_{2}\equiv 0
.

8.27
.

Finally suppose that
g_{1}\colon W\to Z_{1}
is birational and
g_{2}\colon W\to\mathbb{P}^{2}
is of fiber type. Set
E:=\operatorname{Exc}(g_{1})\subset W
and
A:=g_{1}(E)\subset Z_{1}
. We have
\operatorname{NE}(W)=\operatorname{NE}(g_{1})+\operatorname{NE}(g_{2})
, and
E\cdot\operatorname{NE}(g_{1})<0
, hence
E\cdot\operatorname{NE}(g_{2})>0
.

If
\rho_{A}=1
we get the statement, thus let us assume that
\rho_{A}>1
. Then by
8.24
there at most two indices
i\in\{1,\dotsc,\rho_{X}-2\}
such that
E_{|B_{i}}
is reducible, and at most two indices
i\in\{1,\dotsc,\rho_{X}-2\}
such that
B_{i}
dominates
\mathbb{P}^{2}
under
g_{2}
. Since
\rho_{X}-2\geq 5
, there is at least one index
i
, say
i=1
, such that
g_{2}(B_{1})\subsetneq\mathbb{P}^{2}
and
E_{|B_{1}}=C
a
(-1)
-curve in
B_{1}
.

We show that
\rho_{B_{1}}=2
. Then the surface
g_{1}(B_{1})\subset Z_{1}
has Picard number
1
, and this gives the statement.

Assume by contradiction that
\rho_{B_{1}}>2
.
As in
8.25
we see that
g_{2}(B_{1})\subset\mathbb{P}^{2}
is a curve and

g_{2|B_{1}}
factors through a conic bundle
h\colon B_{1}\to\mathbb{P}^{1}
; since

\rho_{B_{1}}>2
,
h

has at least one reducible fiber
\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}
, where
\Gamma_{i}
are
(-1)
-curves with
\Gamma_{1}\cdot_{B_{1}}\Gamma_{2}=1
.
Let
i\in\{1,2\}
. Note that in
W
we have
[\Gamma_{i}]\in\operatorname{NE}(g_{2})
and hence
E\cdot\Gamma_{i}>0
, therefore
C\cdot_{B_{1}}\Gamma_{i}=E_{|B_{1}}\cdot_{B_{1}}\Gamma_{i}=E\cdot\Gamma_{i}>0
.

However it is impossible to have such a configuration
C,\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}
of
(-1)
-curves in
B_{1}
, because
\rho_{B_{1}}\leq 5
(see
8.24
). Indeed, consider a birational map
\varphi\colon B_{1}\to\mathbb{P}^{2}
that contracts
C
to a point
p\in\mathbb{P}^{2}
. The
(-1)
-curves of
B_{1}
are given by the exceptional curves for
\varphi
, and the transforms of the lines through two blown-up points.
Since
C\cdot\Gamma_{i}>0
,
\varphi(\Gamma_{i})
must be a line
\overline{pq_{i}}

where
q_{i}
is another blown-up point, but then the two lines meet only at
p
and
\Gamma_{1}
and
\Gamma_{2}
should be disjoint in
B_{1}
.
This concludes the proof of Lemma
8.23
.∎

Proof of Th.
8.1
.

By Lemma
8.23
there exists a birational contraction
\sigma\colon X\to Z
with
\rho_{Z}=1
and such that
\rho_{A_{i}}=1
for some
i\in\{1,\dotsc,\rho_{X}-1\}
(notation as in Prop.
8.8
).
Up to renumbering we can assume that
\rho_{A_{1}}=1
, so that
A_{1}\cong\mathbb{P}^{2}
.
Similarly as before (see the proof of Lemma
8.23
), using (
8.20
) we see
that there are at least two indexes
j\in\{2,\dotsc,\rho_{X}-1\}
with
\#(A_{1}\cap A_{j})=1
; up to renumbering we can assume that
m_{12}=m_{13}=1
.

8.28
.

Consider the factorization of
\sigma
given by:

X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sigma^{\prime\prime}}}{{\longrightarrow}}Z^{\prime}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sigma^{\prime}}}{{\longrightarrow}}Z

where
\rho_{Z^{\prime}}=3
and
\sigma^{\prime}
is the blow-up of
A_{2}
and
A_{3}
. Let
A_{1}^{\prime}\subset Z^{\prime}
be the transform of
A_{1}\subset Z
. Then
\sigma^{\prime}_{|A_{1}^{\prime}}\colon A_{1}^{\prime}\to A_{1}
blows-up two points, so that
A_{1}^{\prime}\cong\operatorname{Bl}_{2\mskip 1.0mu\text{\it pts}}\mathbb{P}^{2}
and
\rho_{A_{1}^{\prime}}=\rho_{Z^{\prime}}=3
. For
i=2,3
we denote by
C_{i}\subset A_{1}^{\prime}
the
(-1)
-curve over the point
A_{1}\cap A_{i}
, and
C_{0}\subset A_{1}^{\prime}
the
(-1)
-curve not contracted by
\sigma^{\prime}
; recall that
\operatorname{NE}(A_{1}^{\prime})=\langle[C_{0}],[C_{2}],[C_{3}]\rangle
.

Let
\iota\colon A^{\prime}_{1}\hookrightarrow Z^{\prime}
be the inclusion.
By Cor.
8.21

\iota_{*}\colon\mathcal{N}_{1}(A^{\prime}_{1})\to\mathcal{N}_{1}(Z^{\prime})
is an isomorphism that induces an isomorphism between
\operatorname{NE}(A_{1}^{\prime})
and
\operatorname{NE}(Z^{\prime})
, hence

\operatorname{NE}(Z^{\prime})
is a simplicial
3
-dimensional cone generated by
[C_{0}],[C_{2}],[C_{3}]
.

For
i=0,2,3
let
R_{i}
be the extremal ray of
\operatorname{NE}(Z^{\prime})
generated by
[C_{i}]
(so that
\operatorname{NE}(\sigma^{\prime})=R_{2}+R_{3}
); by Cor.
8.21
each
R_{i}
is of type
(3,2)
, with locus
E_{i}\subset Z^{\prime}
such that
E_{i|A_{1}^{\prime}}=C_{i}
.

This implies that restriction

r^{\prime}\colon\operatorname{Pic}(Z^{\prime})\to\operatorname{Pic}(A^{\prime}_{1})
is an isomorphism. Indeed
r^{\prime}
is injective because
\iota_{*}
is surjective, and it is surjective
because
\operatorname{Pic}(A^{\prime}_{1})
is generated by the classes of
C_{0},C_{2},C_{3}
, which are restrictions of divisors in
Z^{\prime}
.

8.29
.

The restriction
\operatorname{Pic}(Z)\to\operatorname{Pic}(A_{1})
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

We have a commutative diagram:

\textstyle{{\operatorname{Pic}(Z^{\prime})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{r^{\prime}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Pic}(A^{\prime}_{1})}
\textstyle{{\operatorname{Pic}(Z)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\sigma^{\prime})^{*}}
\scriptstyle{r}
\textstyle{{\operatorname{Pic}(A_{1})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\sigma^{\prime}_{|A_{1}^{\prime}})^{*}}

where
r^{\prime}
is an isomorphism and
(\sigma^{\prime})^{*}
and
(\sigma^{\prime}_{|A_{1}^{\prime}})^{*}
are injective, so that
r
is injective too.

If
L\in\operatorname{Pic}(A_{1})
, consider
(\sigma^{\prime}_{|A_{1}^{\prime}})^{*}(L)\in\operatorname{Pic}(A_{1}^{\prime})
. Since
r^{\prime}
is surjective, there exists
M\in\operatorname{Pic}(Z^{\prime})
such that
M_{|A_{1}^{\prime}}=(\sigma^{\prime}_{|A_{1}^{\prime}})^{*}(L)
. We have
M\cdot C_{i}=M_{|A_{1}^{\prime}}\cdot C_{i}=0
for
i=2,3
, so that
M=(\sigma^{\prime})^{*}(M_{2})
for some
M_{2}\in\operatorname{Pic}(Z)
, and

(\sigma^{\prime}_{|A_{1}^{\prime}})^{*}(M_{2|A_{1}})=(\sigma^{\prime})^{*}(M_{2})_{|A_{1}^{\prime}}=M_{|A_{1}^{\prime}}=(\sigma^{\prime}_{|A_{1}^{\prime}})^{*}(L)
, hence

M_{2|A_{1}}=L
and
r
is surjective.
∎

8.30
.

By Prop.
8.8

Z
is a Fano
4
-fold with at most locally factorial, isolated ordinary double points,
\rho_{Z}=1
, and
K_{A_{1}}=K_{Z|A_{1}}
. Since
A_{1}\cong\mathbb{P}^{2}
,
8.29
implies that

Z
has index
3
, namely

Z
is a del Pezzo variety, see
[
IP99
, §3.2]
. Let
d\in\mathbb{N}
be its degree,
i.e.

d=H^{4}
where
H
is the ample generator of
\operatorname{Pic}(Z)
and
-K_{Z}=3H
. We have
\chi(Z,H)=h^{0}(Z,H)=d+3
[
ibid.
, Rem. 3.2.2(ii)],
\chi(Z,-H)=\chi(Z,-2H)=0
, and
\chi(Z,\mathcal{O}_{Z})=1
, which yields
\chi(Z,tH)=\frac{1}{24}(t+1)(t+2)(dt^{2}+3dt+12)
, and finally
h^{0}(Z,-K_{Z})=15d+10
.

8.31
.

The birational map
\sigma^{\prime}\colon Z^{\prime}\to Z
factors as two blow-ups
Z^{\prime}\to Z^{\prime\prime}\to Z
with center first
A_{2}\subset Z
and then the transform
A_{3}^{\prime\prime}\subset Z^{\prime\prime}
of
A_{3}\subset Z
. By Prop.
8.8
,
A_{2}
is a smooth del Pezzo surface with
-K_{A_{2}}=-K_{Z|A_{2}}=3H_{|A_{2}}
, so that
A_{2}
has again index
3
and
A_{2}\cong\mathbb{P}^{2}
. Rem.
8.18
gives

h^{0}(Z^{\prime\prime},-K_{Z^{\prime\prime}})=h^{0}(Z,-K_{Z})-10=15d
.

By applying (
8.20
) to
X\to Z^{\prime\prime}
and
A_{3}^{\prime\prime}\subset Z^{\prime\prime}
we get
\rho_{A_{3}^{\prime\prime}}\leq 10-(\rho_{X}-\rho_{Z^{\prime\prime}})=12-\rho_{X}\leq 5
, thus
again by Rem.
8.18
we get

(8.32)

h^{0}(Z^{\prime},-K_{Z^{\prime}})=15d-11+\rho_{A_{3}^{\prime\prime}}\in\{15d-10,\dotsc,15d-6\}.

8.33
.

Fix
j\in\{2,3\}
and consider the contraction
g_{j}\colon Z^{\prime}\to Z_{j}
with
\operatorname{NE}(g_{j})=R_{0}+R_{j}
(see
8.28
), so that
\rho_{Z_{j}}=1
. In
Z^{\prime}
we have
E_{j}\cdot C_{0}=E_{j|A_{1}^{\prime}}\cdot_{A_{1}^{\prime}}C_{0}=C_{j}\cdot C_{0}=1
, and similarly
E_{0}\cdot C_{j}=1
. This implies that
g_{j}
must be of fiber type. Indeed if
g_{j}
is birational, we can apply Prop.
8.8
to
g_{j}\circ\sigma^{\prime\prime}\colon X\to Z_{j}
, and we get extremal rays
\widetilde{R}_{0}
,
\widetilde{R}_{j}
in
\operatorname{NE}(g_{j}\circ\sigma^{\prime\prime})
, with
(\sigma^{\prime\prime})_{*}(\widetilde{R}_{0})=R_{0}
and
(\sigma^{\prime\prime})_{*}(\widetilde{R}_{j})=R_{j}
, and loci
\widetilde{E}_{0}=(\sigma^{\prime\prime})^{*}(E_{0})
,
\widetilde{E}_{j}=(\sigma^{\prime\prime})^{*}(E_{j})
in
X
. Then we get
\widetilde{E}_{j}\cdot\widetilde{R}_{0}>0
by the projection formula, but it is shown in the proof of Prop.
8.8
that
\widetilde{E}_{j}\cdot\widetilde{R}_{0}=0
.

Therefore
g_{j}
is of fiber type, and again
by considering the composition
g_{j}\circ\sigma^{\prime\prime}\colon X\to Z_{j}
, we get
Z_{j}\cong\mathbb{P}^{2}
by Lemma
8.5
.

Let us also note that
(E_{0}+E_{j})\cdot R_{0}=(E_{0}+E_{j})\cdot R_{j}=0
, so that
\mathcal{O}_{Z^{\prime}}(E_{0}+E_{j})=g_{j}^{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{2}}(a_{j})
for some
a_{j}\in\mathbb{N}
.

We have
(E_{0}+E_{j})_{|A_{1}^{\prime}}=C_{0}+C_{j}
fiber of a conic bundle
h_{j}\colon A_{1}^{\prime}\to\mathbb{P}^{1}
, therefore

g_{j}(A_{1}^{\prime})\subset\mathbb{P}^{2}
is a curve, and we have a factorization

\textstyle{{A_{1}^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g_{j|A_{1}^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{h_{j}}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{P}^{1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\nu_{j}}
\textstyle{{g_{j}(A_{1}^{\prime})}}

where
\nu_{j}
is a finite map. Then
h_{j}^{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(1)=C_{0}+C_{j}=(E_{0}+E_{j})_{|A_{1}^{\prime}}=h_{j}^{*}\nu_{j}^{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{2}}(a_{j})_{|g_{j}(A_{1}^{\prime})}
, which gives

\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(1)=\nu_{j}^{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{2}}(a_{j})_{|g_{j}(A_{1}^{\prime})}
.
We conclude that
a_{j}=1
,
g_{j}(A_{1}^{\prime})
is a line in
\mathbb{P}^{2}
,

\nu_{j}
is an isomorphism, and
g_{j|A_{1}^{\prime}}=h_{j}
.

8.34
.

Let now

f\colon Z^{\prime}\to W
be the contraction of
R_{0}
; we have
\rho_{W}=2
, and
f
is of type
(3,2)
by Prop.
8.8
(c)
. Then
Prop.
8.8
applies to
the composition

f\circ\sigma^{\prime\prime}\colon X\to W
; in particular
W
is a Fano
4
-fold with at most locally factorial, isolated ordinary double points.
Set
A_{W}:=f(A_{1}^{\prime})\subset W
; then
A_{W}
is one of the surfaces blown-up by

f\circ\sigma^{\prime\prime}
, hence it is a smooth del Pezzo surface with
K_{A_{W}}=K_{W|A_{W}}
.

Moreover
g_{j}\colon Z^{\prime}\to\mathbb{P}^{2}
factors through
f
, so that

W
has two elementary contractions

\pi_{j}\colon W\to\mathbb{P}^{2}
for
j=1,2
, that induce a finite map
\pi\colon W\to\mathbb{P}^{2}\times\mathbb{P}^{2}
.

The restriction
f_{|A_{1}^{\prime}}
contracts the “middle”
(-1)
-curve
C_{0}
of
A_{1}^{\prime}\cong\operatorname{Bl}_{2\mskip 1.0mu\text{\it pts}}\mathbb{P}^{2}
, and
A_{W}\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
. Moreover by
8.33
for
j=2,3
the image
\pi_{j}(A_{W})\subset\mathbb{P}^{2}
is a line, and
\pi_{j|A_{W}}\colon A_{W}\to\mathbb{P}^{1}
are the two projections.

As in
8.28
we see that
the restriction
\operatorname{Pic}(W)\to\operatorname{Pic}(A_{W})
is an isomorphism, and
K_{W|A_{W}}=K_{A_{W}}
, therefore
W
has index two. Moreover
K_{A_{W}}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}}(-2,-2)
implies that
K_{W}=\pi_{1}^{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{2}}(-2)+\pi_{2}^{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{2}}(-2)
.

Consider the general fiber
F_{1}\subset W
of
\pi_{1}\colon W\to\mathbb{P}^{2}
. It is a smooth del Pezzo surface with
K_{F_{1}}=K_{W|F_{1}}
, thus
F_{1}
has index two and
F_{1}\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
. Notice that
\deg\pi=\deg(\pi_{2|F_{1}}\colon F_{1}\to\mathbb{P}^{2})
. We have

\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}}(-2,-2)=K_{F_{1}}=K_{W|F_{1}}=\bigl(\pi_{1}^{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{2}}(-2)+\pi_{2}^{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{2}}(-2)\bigr)_{|F_{1}}=(\pi_{2|F_{1}})^{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{2}}(-2),

hence
(\pi_{2|F_{1}})^{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{2}}(1)=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}}(1,1)
, and we conclude that
\deg\pi=2
. Finally
K_{W}=\pi^{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{2}\times\mathbb{P}^{2}}(-2,-2)=\pi^{*}K_{\mathbb{P}^{2}\times\mathbb{P}^{2}}+R
with
R\sim\pi^{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{2}\times\mathbb{P}^{2}}(1,1)
, and

W
is a double cover of
\mathbb{P}^{2}\times\mathbb{P}^{2}
with branch locus a divisor of degree
(2,2)
.
We have
\pi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{W}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{2}\times\mathbb{P}^{2}}\oplus\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{2}\times\mathbb{P}^{2}}(-1,-1)
, and

\pi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{W}(-K_{W})=\pi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{W}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{2}\times\mathbb{P}^{2}}(2,2)=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{2}\times\mathbb{P}^{2}}(2,2)\oplus\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{2}\times\mathbb{P}^{2}}(1,1),

therefore
h^{0}(W,-K_{W})=45
.

8.35
.

The blow-up map
f\colon Z^{\prime}\to W
has center a smooth del Pezzo surface of index two (by Prop.
8.8
and because
W
has index two), therefore
h^{0}(Z^{\prime},-K_{Z^{\prime}})=h^{0}(W,-K_{W})-9=36
by Rem.
8.18
.
Together with (
8.32
), this implies that
d=3
and
Z\subset\mathbb{P}^{5}
is a cubic
4
-fold, see
[
IP99
, Th. 3.3.1]
; moreover
A_{i}\subset Z
is a plane for every
i=1,\dotsc,\rho_{X}-1
.

The intersection
A_{i}\cap A_{j}
is finite, therefore
A_{i}
and
A_{j}
intersect only in one point, namely
m_{ij}=1
for every
i\neq j
(see Rem.
8.19
).
Moreover
A_{i}\cap A_{j}\cap A_{k}=\emptyset
if
i<j<k
by Prop.
8.8
.

Let us write
\rho:=\rho_{X}
for simplicity.
The map
X\to Z
factors a sequence of
\rho-1
blow-ups:

X=X_{\rho}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sigma_{\rho-1}}}{{\longrightarrow}}X_{\rho-1}{\longrightarrow}\cdots\cdots{\longrightarrow}X_{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sigma_{1}}}{{\longrightarrow}}X_{1}=Z,

where, for
i=1,\dotsc,\rho-1
,
\sigma_{i}\colon X_{i+1}\to X_{i}
blows-up a smooth del Pezzo surface
S_{i}\subset X_{i}
with
K_{X_{i|S_{i}}}=K_{S_{i}}
, given by the transform of
A_{i}\subset Z
. The induced map
S_{i}\to A_{i}
blows-up
\sum_{j=1}^{i-1}m_{ij}=i-1
points, therefore
\rho_{S_{i}}=i
, and
Rem.
8.18
gives

h^{0}(X_{i+1},-K_{X_{i+1}})=h^{0}(X_{i},-K_{X_{i}})-11+i,

and
h^{0}(X_{1},-K_{X_{1}})=55
.
This gives
h^{0}(X,-K_{X})=\frac{1}{2}(\rho^{2}-23\rho+132)
; on the other hand
h^{0}(X,-K_{X})\geq 2
(see Th.
2.12
), which implies that
\rho_{X}\leq 9
. This concludes the proof of Th.
8.1
.∎

9.
Fano
4
-folds with a divisor of type
(3,0)^{\text{\it sm}}

Let
X
be a Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{X}\geq 7
, that is not a product of surfaces; we consider now the case where
X
has some small elementary contraction. The following remark shows that such
X
must contain a fixed prime divisor of type
(3,0)^{\text{\it sm}}
,
(3,1)^{\text{\it sm}}
, or
(3,0)^{Q}
.

Remark 9.1
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{X}\geq 7
, having a small elementary contraction
f\colon X\to Y
. We have
\operatorname{NE}(f)\not\subset\operatorname{mov}(X)=\operatorname{Eff}(X)^{\vee}
, therefore there exists a one-dimensional face
\tau
of
\operatorname{Eff}(X)
such that
\tau\cdot\operatorname{NE}(f)<0
. By Cor.
4.10

\tau
is a fixed face, hence
\tau=\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}[D]
for some fixed prime divisor
D\subset X
, and
D\cdot\operatorname{NE}(f)<0
. In particular
D
contains
\operatorname{Locus}(f)
which is a union of exceptional planes (Lemma
2.13
), and
D
cannot be of type
(3,2)
(Lemma
5.3
). We conclude that
D
must be of type
(3,0)^{\text{\it sm}}
,
(3,1)^{\text{\it sm}}
, or
(3,0)^{Q}
(see Th.-Def.
5.1
).

In this section we consider the case where
X
has a fixed prime divisor of type
(3,0)^{\text{\it sm}}
, and show the following result (Th.
1.3
from the Introduction).

Theorem 9.2
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{X}\geq 7
having a fixed prime divisor of type
(3,0)^{\text{\it sm}}
. Then one of the following holds:

(i)

X
has an elementary rational contraction onto a
3
-fold and
\rho_{X}\leq 9
;

(ii)

\rho_{X}=7
,

h^{0}(X,-K_{X})\in\{2,\dotsc,15\}
,
and there is a SQM
X\dasharrow X^{\prime}
such that
X^{\prime}=\operatorname{Bl}_{6\mskip 1.0mu\text{\it pts}}Y
,
Y
a smooth Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{Y}=1
and
h^{0}(Y,-K_{Y})=h^{0}(X,-K_{X})+90\in\{92,\dotsc,105\}
. Moreover every fixed prime divisor of
X
is of type
(3,0)^{\text{\it sm}}
.

9.3
Overview of the proof of Th.
9.2
.

The proof follows the same strategy as in
[
Cas17
]
, where the bound
\rho_{X}\leq 12
is shown. Let us note that, in case
(i)
where
X
has an elementary rational contraction onto a
3
-fold, the bound
\rho_{X}\leq 9
follows from Th.
1.5
.

We treat separately the two cases where every fixed prime divisor is of type
(3,0)^{\text{\it sm}}
(Prop.
9.4
), and where there are also fixed prime divisors of other types (Prop.
9.20
).

In the first case, there are a smooth Fano
4
-fold
Y
and a SQM
X\dasharrow X^{\prime}
such that
X^{\prime}=\operatorname{Bl}_{\text{\it pts}}Y
, and
Y
does not have divisorial elementary rational contractions. We compute that
h^{0}(Y,-K_{Y})=h^{0}(X,-K_{X})+15(\rho_{X}-\rho_{Y})
.

If
Y
has an elementary rational contraction onto a
3
-fold, then we lift this contraction to
X
, and get
(i)
.
If
\rho_{Y}=1
, then either
Y\cong\mathbb{P}^{4}
and we get again
(i)
(see §
11.1
), or we get
h^{0}(Y,-K_{Y})\leq 105
from
[
Cas17
]
, which implies

(ii)
(see
9.6
).

Otherwise, we show that we can reduce to the situation where

\rho_{Y}=2
and
Y
has two distinct elementary rational contractions of fiber type
Y\dasharrow B_{i}
where
B_{i}
is
\mathbb{P}^{1}
or
\mathbb{P}^{2}
, for
i=1,2
; moreover
\rho_{X}-\rho_{Y}\geq 5
gives

h^{0}(Y,-K_{Y})\geq 75
.
We show that this case does not happen, by studying explicitly the geometry of
Y
.
This is the longest part of the proof (see
9.7
-
9.17
).

Consider now the case where
X
has a fixed prime divisor of type
(3,0)^{\text{\it sm}}
and also some fixed prime divisor of another type. We know that
\operatorname{Eff}(X)
is generated by classes of fixed prime divisors, and that every
2
-dimensional face is fixed (Cor.
4.10
). Then there must a fixed prime divisor
D
of type
(3,0)^{\text{\it sm}}
and a fixed prime divisor
B
, not of type
(3,0)^{\text{\it sm}}
, that are adjacent. We consider the contraction
X\dasharrow Y
of
D
, so that
Y
is a smooth Fano
4
-fold, and the transform
B_{Y}\subset Y
is still a fixed prime divisor. By analysing
the possibilities for
B_{Y}
, we show that in each case there is a fixed prime divisor
E\subset X
, of type
(3,2)
, such that
D\cap E=\emptyset
. Then we get
(i)
by Th.
7.1
.

Proposition 9.4

(refinement of
[
Cas17
]
, Th. 4.3)
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{X}\geq 7
, and suppose that every fixed prime divisor of
X
is of type
(3,0)^{\text{\it sm}}
. Then the statement of Th.
9.2
holds.

Proof.

Let us first note that, if
X
has an elementary rational contraction onto a
3
-fold, then
\rho_{X}\leq 9
by Th.
1.5
, and we get
(i)
.

We follow
[
Cas17
, proof of Lemma 4.22]
, where it is shown that there is a diagram

X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\varphi}}{{\dasharrow}}Y\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f}}{{\longrightarrow}}Z

where
\varphi
is a sequence of flips and divisorial elementary contractions, and
f
is an elementary contraction of fiber type.

Up to increasing the number of flips and divisorial contractions in
\varphi
, we can also assume that
Y
has no divisorial elementary rational contractions, hence
Y
contains no fixed prime divisors (see Rem.
2.3
).

By [
ibid.
, Lemma 4.19], up to SQM we can assume that
Y
is a smooth Fano
4
-fold, and there is a resolution

\varphi^{\prime}\colon X^{\prime}\to Y

of
\varphi
such that
\varphi^{\prime}

is the blow-up of
Y
in
r:=\rho_{X}-\rho_{Y}
distinct points. Then we have:

(9.5)

2\leq h^{0}(X,-K_{X})=h^{0}(Y,-K_{Y})-15r,

by Th.
2.12
and [
ibid.
, Prop. 3.3].

If
\dim Z=3
, then by [
ibid.
, Lemma 4.21] we have
(i)
.

9.6
.

Suppose that
\rho_{Y}=1
and
Z=\{\text{\it pt}\}
.
If
Y\cong\mathbb{P}^{4}
,
then
X
is the Fano model of
\operatorname{Bl}_{\text{\it pts}}\mathbb{P}^{4}
and has an elementary rational contraction onto
\operatorname{Bl}_{\text{\it pts}}\mathbb{P}^{3}
(see §
11.1
), thus we have
(i)
.
If instead
Y\not\cong\mathbb{P}^{4}
,
as in [
ibid.
, proof of Th. 3.2, p. 380] we see that
h^{0}(Y,-K_{Y})\leq 105
, which together with (
9.5
) implies
(ii)
.

9.7
.

Suppose that
\dim Z=2
, and note that
Z
is smooth (Lemma
3.19
).
We notice that

Z
cannot have divisorial elementary contractions, otherwise the pullback under
f
of the exceptional divisor would be a fixed prime divisor in
Y
, against our reductions.
Therefore
Z
is a minimal smooth rational surface with no
divisorial elementary contraction, and we conclude that either
Z\cong\mathbb{P}^{2}
and
\rho_{Y}=2
, or
Z\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
and
\rho_{Y}=3
. We also note that if
\dim Z=1
, then
Z\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}
and
\rho_{Y}=2
.

9.8
.

Suppose that
Z\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
, and consider the blow-up
of the first point
W:=\operatorname{Bl}_{p_{1}}Y\to Y
in
\varphi^{\prime}\colon X^{\prime}\to Y
. The composition
W\to\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
is not equidimensional, and by Prop.
3.12
it factors as

\textstyle{{W}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{S\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}}}

where
W\dasharrow S
is an elementary rational contraction,
S
is a smooth surface (see Lemma
3.19
), and
S\to\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
is the blow-up of a smooth point, thus
S\cong\operatorname{Bl}_{2\mskip 1.0mu\text{\it pts}}\mathbb{P}^{2}
.

Consider a
(-1)
-curve of
S
contracted by
S\to\mathbb{P}^{2}
, and its pullback
G
in
W
. This is a fixed
prime divisor, and up to to replacing
W
with a SQM, we can assume that
G=\operatorname{Exc}(g)
for some
divisorial elementary contraction
g\colon W\to Y^{\prime}
(see Rem.
2.3
). Then
g
must be the blow-up of a smooth point (by
[
Cas17
, Lemma 4.19(2)]
applied to the composition
X\dasharrow Y^{\prime}
), and there is an elementary rational contraction
Y^{\prime}\dasharrow\mathbb{F}_{1}
.

\textstyle{{W}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g}
\textstyle{{Y^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{S\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{F}_{1}}}

Now we consider the blow-up
\mathbb{F}_{1}\to\mathbb{P}^{2}
and the composition
Y^{\prime}\dasharrow\mathbb{P}^{2}
; it factors again as an elementary divisorial rational contraction
Y^{\prime}\dasharrow Y^{\prime\prime}
and an elementary contraction
Y^{\prime\prime}\to\mathbb{P}^{2}
. Finally, by replacing
Y
with
Y^{\prime\prime}
,
and we can assume that
\rho_{Y}=2
and
Z\cong\mathbb{P}^{2}
.

9.9
.

We are left with the possibility that
\rho_{Y}=2
and
Y
has two distinct elementary rational contractions of fiber type
Y\dasharrow B_{i}
where
B_{i}
is
\mathbb{P}^{1}
or
\mathbb{P}^{2}
, for
i=1,2
, so that
\operatorname{Mov}(Y)=\operatorname{Eff}(Y)
and the two one-dimensional faces of this cone are the pullbacks of
\operatorname{Nef}(B_{i})
,
i=1,2
.
We are going to show that
this contradicts
\rho_{X}\geq 7
.

9.10
.

Suppose that
Y
has index
>1
. Then
Y
cannot have small elementary contractions (see Lemma
2.13
), therefore
both maps
Y\to B_{i}
must be regular. Since each map must be finite on the fibers of the other one, such fibers are at most
2
-dimensional, and we conclude that
Y
has two elementary contractions onto
\mathbb{P}^{2}
.

Fano
4
-folds with index
>1
and Picard number
>1
are classified, see
[
IP99
, Cor. 3.1.15, Th. 3.3.1, Th. 7.2.15]
. An inspection of the classification shows that the only cases where
\rho_{Y}=2
and
Y
has two elementary contractions onto
\mathbb{P}^{2}
are
\mathbb{P}^{2}\times\mathbb{P}^{2}
and
a double cover of
\mathbb{P}^{2}\times\mathbb{P}^{2}
ramified over a divisor of degree
(2,2)
.

On the other hand
Y
cannot be covered by a family of curves of anticanonical degree
3
by
[
Cas17
, Lemma 3.12]
, and this excludes
\mathbb{P}^{2}\times\mathbb{P}^{2}
. In the other case
we have
h^{0}(Y,-K_{Y})=45
(see
8.34
), and by (
9.5
) this implies that
r\leq 2
and
\rho_{X}\leq 4
, a contradiction.

9.11
.

We suppose from now on that
Y
has index one.

For
i=1,2
let us consider a
K
-negative resolution

f_{i}\colon Y_{i}\to B_{i}
of
Y\dasharrow B_{i}
;
we have a diagram:

\textstyle{{Y_{1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{1}}
\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\psi_{2}}
\textstyle{{Y_{2}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{2}}
\textstyle{B_{1}}
\textstyle{{B_{2}}}

where
\psi_{i}
is a SQM.
Fix
i\in\{1,2\}
and let
D_{i}\subset Y_{i}
be a general fiber of
f_{i}
if
B_{i}=\mathbb{P}^{1}
, the pullback of a general line if
B_{i}=\mathbb{P}^{2}
.
Let
\widetilde{D}_{i}\subset Y
be the transform of
D_{i}
.

9.12
.

We notice that
the general fiber
F_{i}
of
f_{i}
cannot have a covering family of rational curves of anticanonical degree
3
.
Otherwise,
Y_{i}
would have a covering family
V
of rational curves, of anticanonical degree
3
, contracted to points by
f_{i}
. However this is excluded as in
[
Cas17
, 4.24]
.

This implies that
D_{i}
contains an irreducible curve
\Gamma_{i}
, contracted by
f_{i}
, with
c_{i}:=-K_{D_{i}}\cdot\Gamma_{i}\in\{1,2,4\}

[
Kol96
, Cor. IV.1.15]
, and
c_{i}\in\{1,2\}
if
B_{i}=\mathbb{P}^{2}
. Notice that
D_{i}\cdot\Gamma_{i}=0
and
-K_{Y_{i}}\cdot\Gamma_{i}=c_{i}
.

Since
F_{i}
is Fano, it is rationally connected.
Consider
f_{i}\colon Y_{i}\to B_{i}
if
B_{i}=\mathbb{P}^{1}
, or

f_{i|D_{i}}\colon D_{i}\to\mathbb{P}^{1}

if
B_{i}=\mathbb{P}^{2}
. These are families of rationally connected varieties over a curve, and
by
[
GHS03
]
they have a section, namely
there exists
C_{i}\subset Y_{i}
such that
D_{i}\cdot C_{i}=1
.

9.13
.

We have
\operatorname{Eff}(Y)=\langle[\widetilde{D}_{1}],[\widetilde{D}_{2}]\rangle
, so there exist positive integers
n,a_{1},a_{2}
such that

n(-K_{Y})=a_{1}\widetilde{D}_{1}+a_{2}\widetilde{D}_{2},

and we can assume that
\gcd(n,a_{1},a_{2})=1
.
Now let
\widehat{D}_{2}\subset Y_{1}
be the transform of
D_{2}
. In
Y_{1}
we have

(9.14)

n(-K_{Y_{1}})=a_{1}D_{1}+a_{2}\widehat{D}_{2},

and intersecting with
C_{1}
we get

n(-K_{Y_{1}}\cdot C_{1})=a_{1}+a_{2}\widehat{D}_{2}\cdot C_{1}.

This implies that
\gcd(a_{2},n)
divides
a_{1}
, and hence that
\gcd(a_{2},n)=1
. Similarly we get that
\gcd(a_{1},n)=1
.

Set
d:=\gcd(a_{1},a_{2})
and
a_{i}:=da_{i}^{\prime}
for
i=1,2
.
We have

n(-K_{Y})=d(a^{\prime}_{1}\widetilde{D}_{1}+a^{\prime}_{2}\widetilde{D}_{2})

and
\gcd(n,d)=1
. This implies that
d=1
, because
Y
has index
1
.
Now intersecting with
\Gamma_{1}
in
Y_{1}
we get from (
9.14
):

nc_{1}=a_{2}\widehat{D}_{2}\cdot\Gamma_{1},

which implies that
a_{2}
divides
c_{1}
and hence that
a_{2}\in\{1,2,4\}
, and
a_{2}\in\{1,2\}
if
B_{1}=\mathbb{P}^{2}
.
Similarly we deduce that
a_{1}\in\{1,2,4\}
.

Since
\gcd(a_{1},a_{2})=1
,
up to exchanging
Y_{1}
and
Y_{2}
we can assume that
a_{1}=1
, so that (
9.14
) becomes:

(9.15)

n(-K_{Y_{1}})=D_{1}+a_{2}\widehat{D}_{2}.

9.16
.

Suppose that
B_{1}\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}
. Then
D_{1}
is a smooth Fano
3
-fold and
h^{0}(D_{1},-K_{Y_{1}|D_{1}})=h^{0}(D_{1},-K_{D_{1}})=\frac{1}{2}(-K_{D_{1}})^{3}+3\leq 35

[
IP99
, Cor. 7.1.2]
. Moreover by (
9.15
):

-K_{Y_{1}}-D_{1}=a_{2}\widehat{D}_{2}-(n-1)(-K_{Y_{1}}),

and we know that
h^{0}(Y_{1},-K_{Y_{1}})=h^{0}(Y,-K_{Y})>0
by Th.
2.12
;
thus we get

\displaystyle h^{0}(Y_{1},-K_{Y_{1}}-D_{1})=h^{0}\bigl(Y_{1},a_{2}\widehat{D}_{2}-(n-1)(-K_{Y_{1}})\bigr)\leq h^{0}(Y_{1},a_{2}\widehat{D}_{2})=h^{0}(Y_{2},a_{2}D_{2})

\displaystyle=h^{0}\bigl(Y_{2},f_{2}^{*}\mathcal{O}_{B_{2}}(a_{2})\bigr)=h^{0}\bigl(B_{2},\mathcal{O}_{B_{2}}(a_{2})\bigr)\leq h^{0}\bigl(\mathbb{P}^{2},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{2}}(4)\bigr)=15.

Now the exact sequence

0\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{Y_{1}}(-K_{Y_{1}}-D_{1})\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{Y_{1}}(-K_{Y_{1}})\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{D_{1}}(-K_{Y_{1}|D_{1}})\longrightarrow 0

yields

h^{0}(Y,-K_{Y})=h^{0}(Y_{1},-K_{Y_{1}})\leq h^{0}(Y_{1},-K_{Y_{1}}-D_{1})+h^{0}(D_{1},-K_{Y_{1}|D_{1}})\leq 15+35=50,

that together with (
9.5
) implies that
r\leq 3
and
\rho_{X}\leq 5
, a contradiction.

9.17
.

Suppose now that
B_{1}\cong\mathbb{P}^{2}
, and
consider the blow-up
of the first point

\operatorname{Bl}_{p_{1}}Y\to Y
in
\varphi^{\prime}\colon X^{\prime}\to Y
. By
[
Cas17
, Lemma 4.19(1)]
, applied to the map
X\dasharrow\operatorname{Bl}_{p_{1}}Y
, we see that there is a smooth Fano
4
-fold
W
, with
\rho_{W}=3
, and a SQM
\operatorname{Bl}_{p_{1}}Y\dasharrow W
. Moreover there is a SQM of
X
which is obtained by blowing-up
W
at
\rho_{X}-3
distinct points, so that using again [
ibid.
, Prop. 3.3]
we have:

(9.18)

h^{0}(W,-K_{W})=h^{0}(X,-K_{X})+15(\rho_{X}-3).

Since
p_{1}
cannot belong to an exceptional plane [
ibid.
, Lemma 4.4(3)], we see that
p_{1}\in\operatorname{dom}(\psi_{i})
for
i=1,2
(see
9.11
); we still denote by
p_{1}
its image in
Y_{i}
. Let

\sigma_{i}\colon W_{i}\longrightarrow Y_{i}

be the blow-up of
p_{1}
, and
E_{i}\subset W_{i}
the exceptional divisor.
Then
W_{i}
is another SQM of
\operatorname{Bl}_{p_{1}}Y
and
W
.

Consider the composition
f_{1}\circ\sigma_{1}\colon W_{1}\to\mathbb{P}^{2}
. As in
9.8
, we see that this map must factor through an elementary rational contraction
W_{1}\dasharrow\mathbb{F}_{1}
, where
h\colon\mathbb{F}_{1}\to\mathbb{P}^{2}
is the blow-up of the point
f_{1}(p_{1})
.

\textstyle{{W_{1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma_{1}}
\textstyle{{Y_{1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{1}}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{F}_{1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{h}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{P}^{2}}}

Recall that
D_{1}=f_{1}^{*}(l)
where
l\subset\mathbb{P}^{2}
is a general line (see
9.11
). Using (
9.15
) we get:

(9.19)

\begin{split}-nK_{W_{1}}=&\ \sigma_{1}^{*}(-nK_{Y_{1}})-3nE_{1}=\sigma_{1}^{*}\bigl(D_{1}+a_{2}\widehat{D}_{2}\bigr)-3nE_{1}\\
=&\ \sigma_{1}^{*}\bigl(f_{1}^{*}(l)\bigr)+a_{2}\sigma_{1}^{*}(\widehat{D}_{2})-3nE_{1}.\end{split}

Let
\alpha\colon W_{1}^{\prime}\to\mathbb{F}_{1}
be a
K
-negative resolution of
W_{1}\dasharrow\mathbb{F}_{1}
,
\widehat{D}_{2}^{\prime}\subset W_{1}^{\prime}
the transform of
\sigma_{1}^{*}(\widehat{D}_{2})
, and

E_{1}^{\prime}\subset W_{1}^{\prime}
the transform of
E_{1}=\operatorname{Exc}(\sigma_{1})
.
We have

E_{1}^{\prime}=\alpha^{*}(e)
where
e\subset\mathbb{F}_{1}

is the
(-1)
-curve. Let also

\pi\colon\mathbb{F}_{1}\to\mathbb{P}^{1}
be the
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-bundle; we have
h^{*}(l)=f+e
where
f\subset\mathbb{F}_{1}
is a general fiber of
\pi
. Finally let us consider
F=\alpha^{*}(f)
a general fiber of
\pi\circ\alpha\colon W_{1}^{\prime}\to\mathbb{P}^{1}
.

\textstyle{{W_{1}^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\textstyle{{W_{1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma_{1}}
\textstyle{{Y_{1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{1}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{P}^{1}}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{F}_{1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\scriptstyle{h}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{P}^{2}}}

We note that
F
is a smooth Fano
3
-fold. Indeed there is a
K
-negative resolution
W_{1}^{\prime\prime}\to\mathbb{P}^{1}
of
\pi\circ\alpha
, and the indeterminacy locus of the SQM
W_{1}^{\prime}\dasharrow W_{1}^{\prime\prime}
is contracted to points by
\alpha
, therefore it does not meet
F
. Hence
F
is isomorphic to a general fiber of
W_{1}^{\prime\prime}\to\mathbb{P}^{1}
, which is Fano. We also note that
\alpha(F)=f
, so that
\rho_{F}\geq 2
.

In
W_{1}^{\prime}
we get, from (
9.19
):

\displaystyle-nK_{W_{1}^{\prime}}=

\displaystyle\ \alpha^{*}\bigl(h^{*}(l)\bigr)+a_{2}\widehat{D}_{2}^{\prime}-3nE_{1}^{\prime}=\alpha^{*}(f+e)+a_{2}\widehat{D}_{2}^{\prime}-3nE_{1}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ F+a_{2}\widehat{D}_{2}^{\prime}-(3n-1)E_{1}^{\prime}.

Recall that
\mathcal{O}_{Y_{2}}(D_{2})=f_{2}^{*}\mathcal{O}_{B_{2}}(1)
(see
9.11
), and
that
a_{2}\in\{1,2\}
because
B_{1}\cong\mathbb{P}^{2}
(see
9.13
).
Therefore we have:

\displaystyle h^{0}(W_{1}^{\prime},-K_{W_{1}^{\prime}}-F)=h^{0}\bigl(W_{1}^{\prime},a_{2}\widehat{D}_{2}^{\prime}-(3n-1)E_{1}^{\prime}-(n-1)(-K_{W_{1}^{\prime}})\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq h^{0}(W_{1}^{\prime},a_{2}\widehat{D}_{2}^{\prime})=h^{0}\bigl(W_{2},\sigma_{2}^{*}(a_{2}D_{2})\bigr)=h^{0}\bigl(W_{2},\sigma_{2}^{*}\bigl(f_{2}^{*}\mathcal{O}_{B_{2}}(a_{2})\bigr)\bigr)

\displaystyle=h^{0}(B_{2},\mathcal{O}_{B_{2}}(a_{2}))\leq h^{0}(\mathbb{P}^{2},\mathcal{O}(2))=6.

Moreover
-K_{W_{1}^{\prime}|F}=-K_{F}
and
h^{0}(F,-K_{F})=\frac{1}{2}(-K_{F})^{3}+3\leq 34
by classification
[
IP99
, Ch. 12]
, and finally we get
h^{0}(W,-K_{W})=h^{0}(W_{1}^{\prime},-K_{W_{1}^{\prime}})\leq h^{0}(W_{1}^{\prime},-K_{W_{1}^{\prime}}-F)+h^{0}(F,-K_{F})\leq 40
. Together with (
9.18
)
this implies that
\rho_{X}\leq 5
, again a contradiction. This concludes the proof of Prop.
9.4
.∎

Proposition 9.20

(refinement of
[
Cas17
]
, Th. 5.40)
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{X}\geq 7
having both a fixed prime divisor
of type
(3,0)^{\text{\it sm}}
and a fixed prime divisor not of type
(3,0)^{\text{\it sm}}
.

Then
X
contains a fixed prime divisor
D

of type
(3,0)^{\text{\it sm}}
and
a fixed prime divisor
E
of type
(3,2)
such that
D\cap E=\emptyset
; in particular
\mathcal{N}_{1}(E,X)\subsetneq\mathcal{N}_{1}(X)
.

Proof.

We proceed as in
[
Cas17
, proof of Th. 5.40]
.
Since
X
has a fixed prime divisor of type
(3,0)^{\text{\it sm}}
,
X
is not isomorphic to a product of surfaces, hence
\delta_{X}\leq 1
(Th.
1.9
) and every face of
\operatorname{Eff}(X)
of dimension
\leq 2
is fixed (Cor.
4.10
).

Since every one-dimensional face of
\operatorname{Eff}(X)
is generated by a fixed prime divisor, there must a
2
-dimensional face
\langle[D],[B]\rangle
such that

D
is
of type
(3,0)^{\text{\it sm}}
and

B
is not. This face is fixed, hence

D
and
B
are adjacent.

Let
X\dasharrow\widetilde{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sigma}}{{\to}}Y
be the contraction of
D
as in Th.-Def.
5.1
(a)
, so that
X\dasharrow\widetilde{X}
is a SQM,
Y
is a smooth Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{Y}\geq 6
, and
\sigma
is the blow-up of a point
p\in Y
with exceptional divisor the transform of
D
; we also have
\delta_{Y}\leq 2
by Lemma
5.18
, so that Th.-Def.
5.1
applies to
Y
too.

We note that since every
2
-dimensional face of
\operatorname{Eff}(X)
is fixed, the same holds for every one-dimensional face of
\operatorname{Eff}(Y)
, and the transform
B_{Y}\subset Y
of
B
is a fixed prime divisor of
Y
(see
[
Cas17
, Lemma 2.21]
). Moreover
p\not\in B_{Y}
by [
ibid.
, Lemma 5.41], and
B_{Y}
is not of type
(3,0)^{\text{\it sm}}
by [
ibid.
