, Lemma 4.14].

Let
C_{1},\dotsc,C_{s}\subset Y
be the images of the exceptional lines of
\widetilde{X}
; then
C_{i}
cannot meet any irreducible curve of anticanonical degree one in
Y
, for every
i=1,\dotsc,s
[
ibid.
, Lemma 3.11(1)].

If
B_{Y}
is of type
(3,2)
, then
B_{Y}
is covered by irreducible curves of anticanonical degree one, therefore
B_{Y}\cap C_{i}=\emptyset
for every
i=1,\dotsc,s
. Then

B_{Y}

is contained in the domain of the birational map
Y\dasharrow X
, therefore
B\cong B_{Y}
and
B
is of type
(3,2)
too. Moreover
B\cap D=\emptyset
, and we have the statement.

Suppose that
B_{Y}
is of type
(3,0)^{Q}
. Note that
Y
cannot have a covering family of curves of anticanonical degree
3
(see [
ibid.
, Lemma 3.12]), hence by [
ibid.
, Prop. 5.18]
Y
must contain a prime divisor
E
covered by curves of anticanonical degree one. As before, we have
C_{i}\cap E=\emptyset
for every
i=1,\dotsc,s
. Moreover, as in [
ibid.
, 5.44] we see that
[C_{i}]\in\operatorname{mov}(Y)
for every
i=1,\dotsc,s
. Thus
\tau:=E^{\perp}\cap\operatorname{mov}(Y)
is a face of
\operatorname{mov}(Y)
containing
[C_{1}],\dotsc,[C_{s}]
.

By [
ibid.
, Lemma 5.7] we have

\dim\tau\geq\dim\mathbb{R}([C_{1}],\dotsc,[C_{s}])=\dim\mathcal{N}_{1}(D,X)-1\geq\rho_{X}-2=\rho_{Y}-1.

Therefore
\tau
is a facet of
\operatorname{mov}(Y)
, and dually
\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}[E]
is a one-dimensional face of
\operatorname{Eff}(Y)=\operatorname{mov}(Y)^{\vee}
. Hence
E
is a fixed divisor, it must be of type
(3,2)
[
ibid.
, Lemma 2.18], and as in the previous part of the proof we see that the transform of
E
in
X
is a fixed prime divisor of type
(3,2)
disjoint from
D
.

Finally, if
B_{Y}
is of type
(3,1)^{\text{\it sm}}
, there is a SQM
\varphi\colon Y\dasharrow\widetilde{Y}
and a smooth Fano
4
-fold
Z
such that
\widetilde{Y}
is the blow-up of
Z
along a smooth irreducible curve
C\subset Z
, with exceptional divisor the transform
B_{\widetilde{Y}}\subset\widetilde{Y}
of
B_{Y}
. Let
\alpha
be a one-dimensional face of
\operatorname{Eff}(Z)
with
\alpha\cdot C>0
. Then
\alpha\not\subset\operatorname{Mov}(Z)
by [
ibid.
, Lemma 5.17], so that
\alpha=\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}[G]
where
G\subset Z
is a fixed prime divisor; let
G_{\widetilde{Y}}\subset\widetilde{Y}
,
G_{Y}\subset Y
, and
G_{X}\subset X
be its transforms.
By
[
Cas22
, Lemmas 4.2 and 4.4]
,
\langle[D],[B],[G_{X}]\rangle
is a fixed face of
\operatorname{Eff}(X)
,
such that
G_{X}
is adjacent to both
D
and
B
.
Since
G\cdot C>0
, we have
G_{\widetilde{Y}}\cap B_{\widetilde{Y}}\neq\emptyset
, thus
G_{Y}\cap B_{Y}
is non-empty and is not a union of exceptional planes (recall that
\dim(\widetilde{Y}\smallsetminus\operatorname{dom}\varphi)=1
by Lemma
2.14
). This implies that also

G_{X}\cap B
is non-empty and is not a union of exceptional planes. Then
G_{X}
must be of type
(3,2)
by
[
Cas22
, Lemma 4.13(b)]
, hence
G_{X}\cap D=\emptyset
by Lemma
5.11
, and we get the statement.
∎

Proof of Th.
9.2
.

If every fixed prime divisor of
X
is of type
(3,0)^{\text{\it sm}}
, we apply Prop.
9.4
. Otherwise by Prop.
9.20
there are a fixed prime divisor
E
of type
(3,2)
, and another fixed prime divisor
D
, such that

D\cap E=\emptyset
. Then we have
\mathcal{N}_{1}(E,X)\subsetneq\mathcal{N}_{1}(X)
(see Rem.
2.7
), and we get
(i)

by Th.
7.1
.
∎

10.
Fano
4
-folds with a divisor of type
(3,1)^{\text{\it sm}}
or
(3,0)^{Q}

In this section we conclude our study of Fano
4
-folds with
\rho\geq 7
having a small elementary contraction, by considering the case where
X
has a fixed prime divisor of type
(3,1)^{\text{\it sm}}
or
(3,0)^{Q}
(Th.
10.1
). Then we prove Cor.
10.5
characterizing Fano
4
-folds with
\rho\geq 9
that are not products of surfaces; this result implies
Theorems
1.1
and
1.4
from the Introduction. Finally we also give an application to Fano
4
-folds with
\rho=8
(Cor.
10.6
).

Theorem 10.1
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{X}\geq 9
having a fixed prime divisor of type
(3,1)^{\text{\it sm}}
or
(3,0)^{Q}
. Then

X
has a rational contraction onto a
3
-fold and
\rho_{X}=9
.

Note that in the setting of the theorem, it is enough to show that
X
has a rational contraction onto a
3
-fold, because then
\rho_{X}=9
follows from Th.
1.5
.

To prove Th.
10.1
, we follow
[
Cas22
, proofs of Th. 8.1 and Th. 9.1]
, where in the same setting the bound
\rho_{X}\leq 12
is shown. Thanks to the results in the previous sections, in particular Cor.
4.10
, Th.
6.1
, and Th.
7.1
, and also to Th.
1.9
, we can improve the bound to
9
and simplify the proofs.
We first treat the case where
X
has a fixed prime divisor of type
(3,1)^{\text{\it sm}}
, and then the case where
X
has a fixed prime divisor of type
(3,0)^{Q}
(and no fixed prime divisors of type
(3,1)^{\text{\it sm}}
).
Since the proofs from
[
Cas22
]
are quite technical, instead of reporting them in full we will
point out which modifications are needed to get our statement; for the case of a divisor of type
(3,1)^{\text{\it sm}}
we will also give a highlight of the main points of the proof.

Proof of Th.
10.1
, case
(3,1)^{\text{\it sm}}
.

We follow
[
Cas22
, proof of Th. 8.1]
.

Let us first assume that:
X
is a smooth Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{X}\geq 8
, having a fixed prime divisor
D
of type
(3,1)^{\text{\it sm}}
, and
with no rational contraction onto a
3
-fold.

Since
X
has a fixed prime divisor of type
(3,1)^{\text{\it sm}}
,
X
is not isomorphic to a product of surfaces.
Then we have the following properties:

\bullet

\delta_{X}\leq 1
(Th.
1.9
);

\bullet

\mathcal{N}_{1}(E,X)=\mathcal{N}_{1}(X)
for every fixed prime divisor
E\subset X
of type
(3,2)

(Th.
7.1
);

\bullet

every face of
\operatorname{Eff}(X)
of dimension
\leq 3
is fixed (Cor.
4.10
);

\bullet

X
has no fixed prime divisor of type
(3,0)^{\text{\it sm}}
(Prop.
9.20
).

We consider the contraction
X\dasharrow\widetilde{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sigma}}{{\to}}Y
of
D
as in Th.-Def.
5.1
, so that
Y
is a smooth Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{Y}\geq 7
and
\sigma
is the blow-up of a smooth irreducible curve
C\subset Y
. Then we have:

(a)

Y
has no rational contraction onto a
3
-fold;

(b)

\mathcal{N}_{1}(E,Y)=\mathcal{N}_{1}(Y)
for every fixed prime divisor
E\subset Y
of type
(3,2)
;

(c)

Y
has no fixed prime divisor of type
(3,0)^{\text{\it sm}}
;

(d)

Y
is not isomorphic to a product of surfaces;

(e)

\delta_{Y}\leq 1
.

Here
(a)
,
(b)
, and
(c)
follow from the same properties of
X
as shown in

[
Cas22
, 8.2]
, and
(d)
and
(e)
are proved in [
ibid.
, 8.3].
We also have:

(f)

every face of
\operatorname{Eff}(Y)
of dimension
\leq 2
is fixed (Cor.
4.10
).

As in [
ibid.
, 8.5], we introduce the following notation: if
C
belongs to a family of rational curves of anticanonical degree one with locus a divisor, we denote this divisor by
E_{0}
. Then we have:

(g)

if
Y
contains a nef prime divisor
H
covered by a family
V
of rational curves of anticanonical degree one, then
H=E_{0}
and
[C]\equiv[V]
.

Indeed we must have
H\cap C\neq\emptyset
by Prop.
7.10
,
(a)
, and
(b)
. Then
C
is a member of the family
V
by [
ibid.
, Lemma 4.21
(a)
], so that
H=E_{0}
.

As in [
ibid.
, 8.6 - 8.7], we denote by
E_{1},\dotsc,E_{r}
the set of fixed prime divisors of
Y
of type
(3,2)
and different from
E_{0}
.
It is shown in [
ibid.
, 8.12 - 8.16] that
r\geq\rho_{X}-5
, and that for every
i=1,\dotsc,r
the transform
\widetilde{E}_{i}\subset X
of
E_{i}
is a fixed prime divisor of type
(3,2)
having intersection zero with
C_{D}
.
Note that [
ibid.
, 8.9 and 8.10] are not needed because of
(f)
, and [
ibid.
, 8.11] is replaced by
(g)
. Moreover [
ibid.
, 8.18] shows that every fixed prime divisor of
Y
is of type
(3,1)^{\text{\it sm}}
or
(3,2)
(and both types do occur), hence by [
ibid.
, Lemma 4.22] every fixed prime divisor of
X
, adjacent to
D
, is still of type
(3,1)^{\text{\it sm}}
or
(3,2)
. We get the following intermediate result.

Proposition 10.2

(refinement of
[
Cas22
]
, Prop. 8.17)
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{X}\geq 8
and
D\subset X
a fixed prime divisor of type
(3,1)^{\text{\it sm}}
. Then one of the following holds:

(i)

X
has a rational contraction onto a
3
-fold;

(ii)

there are at least
\rho_{X}-5
fixed prime divisors of type
(3,2)
adjacent to
D
and having intersection zero with
C_{D}
; moreover every fixed prime divisor adjacent to
D
is of type
(3,1)^{\text{\it sm}}
or
(3,2)
.

We assume from now on that
\rho_{X}\geq 9
, namely that
X
is a smooth Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{X}\geq 9
, having a fixed prime divisor
D
of type
(3,1)^{\text{\it sm}}
, and
with no rational contraction onto a
3
-fold.
We show that this gives a contradiction.

Notice that
\rho_{Y}\geq 8
, in particular
every face of
\operatorname{Eff}(Y)
of dimension
\leq 3
is fixed, by
Cor.
4.10
and
(d)
; this replaces [
ibid.
, 8.24].

Let
B\subset Y
be a
fixed prime divisor
of type
(3,1)^{\text{\it sm}}
; by
(a)
and Prop.
10.2
applied to
Y
and
B
, we see that

B
is adjacent to some divisor among
E_{1},\dotsc,E_{r}
.

Using this, [
ibid.
, 8.26 - 8.27]
shows that there exist a fixed prime divisor
B_{1}\subset Y
of type
(3,1)^{\text{\it sm}}
, and
i\in\{1,\dotsc,r\}
, say
i=1
, such that
B_{1}+E_{1}
is movable and not big, so that the linear system
m|B_{1}+E_{1}|
for
m\gg 0
defines a rational contraction of fiber type
f\colon Y\dasharrow Z
. By [
ibid.
, Lemma 5.11],
(a)
, and
(b)
, we have
Z\cong\mathbb{P}^{2}
and
f
is special.

We note that
C
cannot be contracted to a point by
f
, otherwise the composition
X\dasharrow\mathbb{P}^{2}
has a unique prime divisor (precisely
D
) contracted to a point, and this contradicts Lemma
7.9
. Thus
C
is not contracted to a point,
and as in [
ibid.
, proof of 8.9], this implies that the general fiber of
f
is
\mathbb{P}^{2}
, hence the minimal face
\tau_{f}
of
\operatorname{Eff}(Y)
containing
f^{*}\operatorname{Nef}(\mathbb{P}^{2})
is a facet (see Def.-Rem.
3.2
).

Then [
ibid.
, 8.29] shows that there are irreducible curves
\Gamma_{1},\dotsc,\Gamma_{\rho_{Y}-2}\subset\mathbb{P}^{2}
, and fixed prime divisors
B_{2},\dotsc,B_{\rho_{Y}-2}\subset Y
of type
(3,1)^{\text{\it sm}}
, such that (up to renumbering)
f^{*}(\Gamma_{i})=B_{i}+E_{i}
for
i=1,\dotsc,\rho_{Y}-2
, and

\tau_{f}=\langle[B_{1}],\dotsc,[B_{\rho_{Y}-2}],[E_{1}],\dotsc,[E_{\rho_{Y}-2}]\rangle.

Moreover
\langle[B_{1}],\dotsc,[B_{\rho_{Y}-4}],[E_{\rho_{Y}-3}],[E_{\rho_{Y}-2}]\rangle
is a face of
\tau_{f}
of dimension
\rho_{Y}-2
, hence there exists a facet
\eta
of
\operatorname{Eff}(Y)
such that

\langle[B_{1}],\dotsc,[B_{\rho_{Y}-4}],[E_{\rho_{Y}-3}],[E_{\rho_{Y}-2}]\rangle=\tau_{f}\cap\eta

(see [
ibid.
, 8.32]).

If
P\subset Y
is a fixed prime divisor such that
[P]\in\eta\smallsetminus\tau_{f}
, [
ibid.
, 8.33 - 8.35] shows that, up to renumbering,
P+B_{i}
is movable for
i=1,\dotsc,\rho_{Y}-6
. Let
\eta_{1}
be the minimal face of
\eta
containing
[(\rho_{Y}-6)P+B_{1}+\cdots+B_{\rho_{Y}-6}]
, so that
\eta_{1}
contains
[P],[B_{1}],\dotsc,[B_{\rho_{Y}-6}]
, and
[P+B_{i}]\in\eta_{1}\cap\operatorname{Mov}(Y)
for every
i=1,\dotsc,\rho_{Y}-6
; in particular
\eta_{1}\cap\operatorname{Mov}(Y)
is a face of
\operatorname{Mov}(Y)
of dimension
\geq\rho_{Y}-6
. As in Rem.
3.11
, we take a cone
\alpha\in\operatorname{MCD}(Y)
such that
\alpha\subset\eta_{1}\cap\operatorname{Mov}(Y)
and
\dim\alpha=\dim(\eta_{1}\cap\operatorname{Mov}(Y))
, and consider the rational contraction
h\colon Y\dasharrow S
such that
\alpha=h^{*}(\operatorname{Nef}(S))
(see [
ibid.
, 8.36]).

Then
h
is of fiber type and special, by Lemma
3.10
; moreover
\rho_{S}=\dim(\eta_{1}\cap\operatorname{Mov}(Y))\geq\rho_{Y}-6\geq 2
, hence
S\not\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}
, and by
(a)
we have
\dim S=2
.
By [
ibid.
, Lemma 5.12] and
(b)
,
h
must be quasi-elementary.

Finally Th.
6.1
,
(a)
, and
(d)
imply that
h
is elementary, therefore

\rho_{S}=\rho_{Y}-1
,
\eta_{1}=\eta
, and
h^{*}(\mathcal{N}^{1}(S))=\mathbb{R}\eta
. On the other hand
[E_{\rho_{Y}-2}]\in\eta
, and by [
ibid.
, Lemma 5.11] and
(b)
this gives a contradiction.
This concludes the first part of the proof of Th.
10.1
.
∎

Lemma 10.3

(refinement of
[
Cas22
]
, Lemma 9.2)
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{X}\geq 7
and let
D,E
be two adjacent fixed prime divisors of type
(3,0)^{Q}
in
X
. Let
L\subset E
be an exceptional plane such that
L\cap D=\emptyset
.
Then one of the following holds:

(i)

X
has a rational contraction onto a
3
-fold;

(ii)

there exists an exceptional plane
M\subset D
such that
C_{M}+C_{E}\equiv C_{D}+C_{L}
and
D\cdot C_{M}=-1
.

Proof.

Note that
X
is not a product of surfaces, because it contains fixed prime divisors of type
(3,0)^{Q}
, hence
\delta_{X}\leq 1
by Th.
1.9
.
The same proof as the one of
[
Cas22
, Lemma 9.2]
applies, just using Th.
7.1
instead of [
ibid.
, Th. 4.8], and Prop.
7.10
and Th.
7.1
instead of [
ibid.
, Prop. 7.1].
∎

Lemma 10.4

(refinement of
[
Cas22
]
, Lemma 9.3)
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{X}\geq 7
and let
D,E
be two adjacent fixed prime divisors of type
(3,0)^{Q}
in
X
. Suppose that one of the following holds:

(a)

every fixed prime divisor is of type
(3,0)^{Q}
;

(b)

there is a fixed prime divisor
F
, of type
(3,2)
, such that
\langle[D],[E],[F]\rangle
is a fixed face of
\operatorname{Eff}(X)
.

Then one of the following holds:

(i)

X
has a rational contraction onto a
3
-fold;

(ii)

there exist

L_{1},\dotsc,L_{\rho_{X}-2}\subset E
exceptional planes, disjoint from
D
, such that
[C_{E}],[C_{L_{1}}],\dotsc,[C_{L_{\rho_{X}-2}}]
is a basis of
D^{\perp}
. In case
(b)
, we can moreover assume that
C_{E}\equiv C_{F}+C_{L_{1}}
and
[C_{E}],[C_{F}],[C_{L_{2}}],\dotsc,[C_{L_{\rho_{X}-2}}]
is a basis of
D^{\perp}
.

Proof.

Note that
X
is not a product of surfaces, because it contains fixed prime divisors of type
(3,0)^{Q}
, hence
\delta_{X}\leq 1
by Th.
1.9
.
The same proof as the one of
[
Cas22
, Lemma 9.3]
applies, just using Lemma
7.8
instead of [
ibid.
, Lemma 7.2], and Th.
7.1
instead of [
ibid.
, Th. 4.8].
∎

Proof of Th.
10.1
, case
(3,0)^{Q}
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{X}\geq 9
having a fixed prime divisor of type
(3,0)^{Q}
.
Note that
X
is not a product of surfaces, therefore
\delta_{X}\leq 1
by Th.
1.9
.

If
X
has a rational contraction onto a
3
-fold, then
\rho_{X}=9
by Th.
1.5
.
We assume that
X
has no rational contraction onto a
3
-fold, and reach a contradiction.
By Th.
9.2
and by the first part of the proof, every fixed prime divisor of
X
is of type
(3,0)^{Q}
or
(3,2)
.

The same proof as
[
Cas22
, proof of Th. 9.1]
works, with the following modifications.

\bullet

By Cor.
4.10
, every face of
\operatorname{Eff}(X)
of dimension
\leq 4
is fixed; this simplifies [
ibid.
, 9.4 and 9.6].

\bullet

In [
ibid.
, 9.4 and 9.5] we apply Lemma
7.8
instead of [
ibid.
, Lemma 7.2].

\bullet

In [
ibid.
, 9.7] we apply Th.
7.1
instead of [
ibid.
, Th. 4.8].

\bullet

In [
ibid.
, 9.12 and 9.13] we apply Lemma
10.4
instead of [
ibid.
, Lemma 9.3].

\bullet

In [
ibid.
, 9.14 and 9.24] we apply Lemma
10.3
instead of [
ibid.
, Lemma 9.2].

\bullet

In [
ibid.
, 9.20] we get a contradiction, because we are assuming
\rho_{X}\geq 9
.
∎

Corollary 10.5
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{X}\geq 9
, not isomorphic to a product of surfaces.
Then
\rho_{X}=9
and one of the following holds:

(i)

X
has an elementary rational contraction onto a
3
-fold;

(ii)

X
is the blow-up of
W
along a normal surface
S
,
where
W
is the Fano model of
\operatorname{Bl}_{7\mskip 1.0mu\text{\it pts}}\mathbb{P}^{4}
, and
S\subset W
is the transform of a cubic scroll or
a cone over a twisted cubic in

\mathbb{P}^{4}
, containing the blown-up points;

(iii)

X
is a blow-up of a cubic
4
-fold as in Th.
1.2
.

Note that this result implies
Theorems
1.1
and
1.4
.

Proof.

If
X
has no small elementary contractions, then by Th.
1.2
we have
\rho_{X}=9
and
(iii)
.

If instead
X
has a small elementary contraction, then
by Rem.
9.1

X
has a fixed prime divisor of type
(3,0)^{\text{\it sm}}
,

(3,1)^{\text{\it sm}}
, or
(3,0)^{Q}
.
If
X
has a fixed prime divisor of type
(3,0)^{\text{\it sm}}
, then we apply Th.
9.2
and get
\rho_{X}=9
and
(i)
.

Finally, if
X
has a fixed prime divisor of type
(3,1)^{\text{\it sm}}
or
(3,0)^{Q}
, by Th.
10.1
we get that
\rho_{X}=9
and
X
has a rational contraction onto a
3
-fold. Then we apply Th.
1.5
, and get
(i)
or
(ii)
.
∎

Corollary 10.6
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{X}=8
. Then one of the following holds:

(i)

X
has a rational contraction onto a
3
-fold;

(ii)

every fixed prime divisor of
X
is of type
(3,1)^{\text{\it sm}}
or
(3,2)
;

(iii)

every fixed prime divisor of
X
is of type
(3,0)^{Q}
or
(3,2)
, and two divisors of type
(3,2)
cannot be adjacent.

Proof.

Let us assume that
(i)
does not hold, namely
X
has no rational contraction onto a
3
-fold; in particular
X
is not isomorphic to a product of surfaces.
By Th.
9.2
,

X
has no fixed prime divisors of type
(3,0)^{\text{\it sm}}
, hence
every fixed prime divisor of
X
is of type
(3,2)
,
(3,1)^{\text{\it sm}}
, or
(3,0)^{Q}

(see Th.-Def.
5.1
). By Prop.
10.2
, in
X
a divisor of type
(3,1)^{\text{\it sm}}
and one of type
(3,0)^{Q}
cannot be adjacent. Moreover by Lemma
7.8
a divisor of type
(3,0)^{Q}
can be adjacent to at most one divisor of type
(3,2)
. Let us also note that every face of
\operatorname{Eff}(X)
of dimension
\leq 3
is fixed, by Cor.
4.10
, and hence simplicial (see
[
Cas22
, Lemma 4.2]
).

Let
E
be a fixed prime divisor of type
(3,2)
, and suppose that
E
is adjacent to a fixed prime divisor
D
of type
(3,0)^{Q}
. Then
every
3
-dimensional face of
\operatorname{Eff}(X)
containing
\langle[E],[D]\rangle
must be
\langle[E],[D],[D^{\prime}]\rangle
with
D^{\prime}
of type
(3,0)^{Q}
. This implies that every fixed prime divisor adjacent to
E
must be of type
(3,0)^{Q}
.

Consider now a sequence of fixed prime divisors
D_{1},\dotsc,D_{r}
such that
D_{i}
is adjacent to
D_{i+1}
for every
i=1,\dotsc,r-1
, with
D_{1}
of type
(3,0)^{Q}
. Then
D_{2}
can be either
(3,0)^{Q}
or
(3,2)
, and in this last case,
D_{3}
must be
(3,0)^{Q}
. Proceeding in this way, we see that no
D_{i}
can be of type
(3,1)^{sm}
. Since
\operatorname{Eff}(X)
is connected, we must have
(ii)
or
(iii)
.
∎

11.
Examples

11.1.
Fano models of the blow-ups of
\mathbb{P}^{4}
in points

Let
X^{\prime}
be the blow-up of
\mathbb{P}^{4}
at
r
general points, with
r\leq 8
. Then there is a SQM
X^{\prime}\dasharrow X
such that
X
is smooth and Fano, see
[
CS24
, Ex. 7.2 and references therein]
; we have

\rho_{X}=1+r\leq 9
.

If
r\geq 1
, there is an elementary rational contraction
f\colon X\dasharrow Y:=\operatorname{Bl}_{r-1\mskip 2.0mu\text{\it pts}}\mathbb{P}^{3}
.

Moreover (for
r\geq 2
) there is also
an
elementary rational contraction
\varphi\colon Y\dasharrow S:=\operatorname{Bl}_{r-2\mskip 2.0mu\text{\it pts}}\mathbb{P}^{2}
, and the composition
g:=\varphi\circ f\colon X\dasharrow S
is a quasi-elementary rational contraction with
d_{g}=\rho_{X}-\rho_{S}=2
.

Finally, by composing
g
with a conic bundle
S\to\mathbb{P}^{1}
(for
r\geq 3
), we get a rational contraction
h\colon X\dasharrow\mathbb{P}^{1}
with
d_{h}=3
.

We also note that every fixed prime divisor of
X
is of type
(3,0)^{\text{\it sm}}
.

11.2.
Two examples with
\rho=7
from
[
CS24
]

Let
W
be the Fano model of
\operatorname{Bl}_{q_{1},\dotsc,q_{5}}\mathbb{P}^{4}
(see §
11.1
), and for
i=1,2
let

S_{i}\subset W
be the transform of the following surface
A_{i}\subset\mathbb{P}^{4}
:

\bullet

A_{1}
is a general cubic rational normal scroll containing
q_{1},\dotsc,q_{5}
;

\bullet

A_{2}
is a sextic K3 surface with
\operatorname{Sing}(A_{2})=\{q_{1},\dotsc,q_{5}\}
and having ordinary double points at each
q_{j}
; more precisely

A_{2}=Q\cap M
where
Q
is a general quadric hypersurface containing
q_{1},\dotsc,q_{5}
, and
M
is a general cubic hypersurface with ordinary double points at
q_{1},\dotsc,q_{5}
.

Let
\sigma_{i}\colon X_{i}\to W
be the blow-up along
S_{i}
;
then
X_{i}
is a smooth Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{X_{i}}=7

(see
[
CS24
, §7.2 and §7.3]
).

There is an elementary rational contraction
\psi\colon W\dasharrow Y:=\operatorname{Bl}_{4\mskip 1.0mu\text{\it pts}}\mathbb{P}^{3}
, and the composition
f_{i}:=\psi\circ\sigma_{i}\colon X_{i}\dasharrow Y

is a
special rational contraction with
\rho_{X_{i}}-\rho_{Y}=2
.

Moreover there is an elementary rational contraction
\varphi\colon Y\dasharrow S:=\operatorname{Bl}_{3\mskip 1.0mu\text{\it pts}}\mathbb{P}^{2}
, and
the composition
g_{i}:=\varphi\circ f_{i}\colon X_{i}\dasharrow S
is a quasi-elementary rational contraction with
d_{g_{i}}=\rho_{X_{i}}-\rho_{S}=3
.

Finally, by composing
g_{i}
with a conic bundle
S\to\mathbb{P}^{1}
, we get a rational contraction
h_{i}\colon X_{i}\dasharrow\mathbb{P}^{1}
with
d_{h_{i}}=4
.

We also note that
X_{i}
contains (at least) six fixed prime divisors of type
(3,2)
: one is
\operatorname{Exc}(\sigma_{i})
, and the others are the transforms in
X_{i}
of the cones in
\mathbb{P}^{4}
over
A_{i}
with vertex
q_{j}
, for
j=1,\dotsc,5
.

Moreover, for
j=1,\dotsc,5
, let
D_{j}\subset X_{i}
be the transform of the exceptional divisor over
q_{j}
in
\operatorname{Bl}_{q_{1},\dotsc,q_{5}}\mathbb{P}^{4}\to\mathbb{P}^{4}
. Then
D_{j}
is a fixed prime divisor
of type
(3,1)^{\text{\it sm}}
in
X_{1}
, of type
(3,0)^{Q}
in
X_{2}
(see [
ibid.
, Section 5, in particular Lemmas 5.39, 5.46, 5.68, 5.74]).

11.3.
A family with an elementary rational contraction onto a surface

Let
p_{1},\dotsc,p_{m}\in\mathbb{P}^{2}
be general points,
C_{1}^{\prime},\dotsc,C_{m}^{\prime}\subset\mathbb{P}^{2}
general lines,
C_{i}:=\{p_{i}\}\times C_{i}^{\prime}\subset\mathbb{P}^{2}\times\mathbb{P}^{2}
, and
h\colon\mathbb{P}^{2}\times\mathbb{P}^{2}\to\mathbb{P}^{2}
the first projection.

Let
\beta\colon\widehat{X}\to\mathbb{P}^{2}\times\mathbb{P}^{2}
be the blow-up of
C_{1},\dotsc,C_{m}
,
\widehat{D}_{i}\subset\widehat{X}
the exceptional divisor over
C_{i}
, and
L_{i}\subset\widehat{X}
the transform of the fiber
h^{-1}(p_{i})
, containing
C_{i}
.

Since
\mathcal{N}_{C_{i}/\mathbb{P}^{2}\times\mathbb{P}^{2}}\cong\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(1)\oplus\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}^{\oplus 2}
, we have
\widehat{D}_{i}\cong\mathbb{P}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(\mathcal{O}\oplus\mathcal{O}(1)^{\oplus 2})
, and
\widehat{D}_{i}\cap L_{i}
is the flopping curve in
\widehat{D}_{i}
.
Moreover
L_{i}\cong\mathbb{P}^{2}
and
\mathcal{N}_{L_{i}/\widehat{X}}\cong\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{2}}(-1)^{\oplus 2}
, namely
L_{i}
is an exceptional plane in
\widehat{X}
.

Let us consider the composition
h\circ\beta\colon\widehat{X}\to\mathbb{P}^{2}
.
The class of every curve in
\widehat{D}_{i}\cup L_{i}
is in
\langle[C_{L_{i}}]\rangle+\operatorname{NE}(\beta)
, so that

\operatorname{NE}(h\circ\beta)=\operatorname{NE}(\beta)+\langle[C_{L_{1}}],\dotsc,[C_{L_{m}}]\rangle,

and
h\circ\beta
is
K
-negative.
One can also check that
-K_{\widehat{X}}-\sum_{i=1}^{m}\widehat{D}_{i}
has positive intersection with every non-zero class in
\operatorname{NE}(\beta)
, while it has zero intersection with every
C_{L_{i}}
, so that
\langle[C_{L_{1}}],\dotsc,[C_{L_{m}}]\rangle
is an
m
-dimensional face of
\operatorname{NE}(h\circ\beta)
, whose contraction is small.
There is a SQM
\xi\colon\widehat{X}\dasharrow\widetilde{X}
, relative to
\beta\circ h
:

\textstyle{{\widehat{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\beta}
\scriptstyle{\xi}
\textstyle{{\widetilde{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{h}}
\scriptstyle{g}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{P}^{2}\times\mathbb{P}^{2}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{h}
\textstyle{\mathbb{P}^{2}}
\textstyle{S\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}

where
\widetilde{X}
is smooth, the indeterminacy locus of
\xi
is
L_{1}\cup\cdots\cup L_{m}
, and that of
\xi^{-1}
is the disjoint union of
m
exceptional lines
\ell_{1},\dotsc,\ell_{m}
.

Let
D_{i}\subset\widetilde{X}
be the transform of
\widehat{D}_{i}
; we have

\ell_{i}\subset D_{i}
and
D_{i}=(\tilde{h})^{-1}(p_{i})
.
The restriction
(\xi)_{|\widehat{D}_{i}}\colon\widehat{D}_{i}\dasharrow D_{i}
is a flop, we still have
D_{i}\cong\mathbb{P}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(\mathcal{O}\oplus\mathcal{O}(1)^{\oplus 2})
, but now the flopping curve in
D_{i}
is the exceptional line
\ell_{i}
(see Fig.
11.1
).
It is not difficult to check that the transform
\Gamma\subset\widetilde{X}
of a line in a general fiber of
h\colon\mathbb{P}^{2}\times\mathbb{P}^{2}\to\mathbb{P}^{2}
is numerically equivalent to a line in a fiber of the
\mathbb{P}^{2}
-bundle
D_{i}\to\mathbb{P}^{1}
, and that

\operatorname{NE}(\tilde{h})=\langle[\Gamma],[\ell_{1}],\dotsc,[\ell_{m}]\rangle.

Let
\alpha\colon S\to\mathbb{P}^{2}
be the blow-up of
p_{1},\dotsc,p_{m}
. Then
\tilde{h}
factors as
\alpha\circ g
where
g\colon\widetilde{X}\to S
is a
\mathbb{P}^{2}
-bundle, and
g(D_{i})\subset S
is the exceptional curve over
p_{i}
.

In conclusion, the composition
g\circ\xi\colon\widehat{X}\dasharrow S
is an elementary rational contraction onto a surface. We have
\rho_{\widehat{X}}=m+2
.

Figure 11.1.
The flip over the point
p_{i}\in\mathbb{P}^{2}
, in §
11.3
.

The case
m\leq 3
.

If
m\leq 3
, then all these varieties are toric, and they can be described explicitly using Batyrev’s
language of primitive collections and primitive relations
[
Bat91
]
, and Sato’s description of how primitive relations change after smooth toric blow-ups and blow-downs
[
Sat00
, §4]
.
Let us consider in detail each case.

For
m=1
,
X:=\widehat{X}
is Fano with
\rho_{X}=3
; it is
G_{5}
in Batyrev’s classification of toric Fano
4
-folds
[
Bat99
]
.

For
m=2
,
\widehat{X}
is not Fano: it contains one exceptional line

\ell
whose image in
\mathbb{P}^{2}
is the line
\overline{p_{1}p_{2}}
. More precisely,
\ell
is the transform of the unique curve
\overline{p_{1}p_{2}}\times\{q\}\subset\mathbb{P}^{2}\times\mathbb{P}^{2}
that intersects both
C_{1}
and
C_{2}
(namely
q=C_{1}^{\prime}\cap C_{2}^{\prime}
).
The class
[\ell]
generates a small extremal ray of
\operatorname{NE}(\widehat{X})
; the associated flip
\widehat{X}\dasharrow X
gives a smooth toric Fano
4
-fold
X
with
\rho_{X}=4
, which is
Z_{2}
in
[
Bat99
]
.

\textstyle{X}
\textstyle{{\widehat{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\beta}
\scriptstyle{\xi}
\textstyle{{\widetilde{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{h}}
\scriptstyle{g}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{P}^{2}\times\mathbb{P}^{2}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{h}
\textstyle{\mathbb{P}^{2}}
\textstyle{S\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}

Finally let us consider the case
m=3
. Now

\widehat{X}
contains three exceptional lines
\ell_{ij}
, whose images in
\mathbb{P}^{2}
are the lines
\overline{p_{i}p_{j}}
, for
1\leq i<j\leq 3
. As before,
\ell_{ij}
is the transform of the curve
\overline{p_{i}p_{j}}\times\{q_{ij}\}\subset\mathbb{P}^{2}\times\mathbb{P}^{2}
with
q_{ij}=C_{i}^{\prime}\cap C_{j}^{\prime}
.
Again one can consider the flips of these three exceptional lines
\widehat{X}\dasharrow X
, and get a smooth toric Fano
4
-fold
X
with
\rho_{X}=5
; this is Sato’s example
[
Sat00
, Ex. 4.7]
.

Therefore for
m\in\{1,2,3\}
this construction gives a Fano
4
-fold
X
with
\rho_{X}=m+2\in\{3,4,5\}
and an elementary rational contraction
X\dasharrow S
onto a surface.

The case
m\geq 4
.

Lemma 11.1
.

If there exists a SQM
\widehat{X}\dasharrow X
such that
X
is a smooth Fano
4
-fold, then
m\leq 6
.

Proof.

Assume that
X
exists. We have

h^{0}(X,-K_{X})=\chi(X,-K_{X})
because
X
is Fano, and
\chi(X,-K_{X})=\chi(\widehat{X},-K_{\widehat{X}})
by
[
Cas17
, Cor. 3.10]
. Since
C_{i}\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}
and
-K_{\mathbb{P}^{2}\times\mathbb{P}^{2}}\cdot C_{i}=3
for every
i=1,\dotsc,m
, by [
ibid.
, Lemma 3.8] we get

0\leq h^{0}(X,-K_{X})=\chi(\widehat{X},-K_{\widehat{X}})=\chi(\mathbb{P}^{2}\times\mathbb{P}^{2},\mathcal{O}(3,3))-15m=100-15m

which implies that
m\leq 6
.
∎

Question 11.2
.

For
m\in\{4,5,6\}
, does there exist a SQM
\widehat{X}\dasharrow X
such that
X
is a smooth Fano
4
-fold?

This would give a Fano
4
-fold
X
with
\rho_{X}=m+2\in\{6,7,8\}
and an elementary rational contraction
X\dasharrow S
onto a surface. This construction shows up in the proof of Th.
6.1
, see
6.19
.

11.4.
A family with
\rho=3
and a quasi-elementary contraction onto
\mathbb{P}^{2}

Let
A\subset\mathbb{P}^{2}\times\mathbb{P}^{2}
be a surface obtained as a complete intersection of two general divisors,
B
of degree
(2,1)
and
D
of degree
(0,2)
; then
A
is a del Pezzo surface with
K_{A}^{2}=2
.
Let
\sigma\colon X\to\mathbb{P}^{2}\times\mathbb{P}^{2}
be the blow-up along
A
; we show that
X
is Fano. This example is inspired by the proof of Th.
6.1
, see
6.24
.

We have

K_{X}^{4}=(K_{\mathbb{P}^{2}\times\mathbb{P}^{2}})^{4}-3(K_{{\mathbb{P}^{2}\times\mathbb{P}^{2}}|A})^{2}-2K_{A}\cdot K_{{\mathbb{P}^{2}\times\mathbb{P}^{2}}|A}+c_{2}(\mathcal{N}_{A/\mathbb{P}^{2}\times\mathbb{P}^{2}})-K_{A}^{2}

(see
[
CS24
, Lemma 2.4]
). One can compute that
(K_{\mathbb{P}^{2}\times\mathbb{P}^{2}})^{4}=486
,
(K_{{\mathbb{P}^{2}\times\mathbb{P}^{2}}|A})^{2}=90
,
K_{A}\cdot K_{{\mathbb{P}^{2}\times\mathbb{P}^{2}}|A}=18
, and
c_{2}(\mathcal{N}_{A/\mathbb{P}^{2}\times\mathbb{P}^{2}})=16
,
thus
K_{X}^{4}=194
.

Let
E\subset X
be the exceptional divisor, and
\widetilde{B}\subset X
the transform of
B
; note that
\sigma_{|\widetilde{B}}\colon\widetilde{B}\to B
is an isomorphism. By adjunction we have

\displaystyle-K_{X|\widetilde{B}}=-K_{\widetilde{B}}+\widetilde{B}_{|\widetilde{B}}=-K_{\widetilde{B}}+(\sigma^{*}B-E)_{|\widetilde{B}}=\sigma_{|\widetilde{B}}^{*}(-K_{B}+B_{|B}-A)

\displaystyle=\sigma_{|\widetilde{B}}^{*}\bigl((-K_{\mathbb{P}^{2}\times\mathbb{P}^{2}}-D)_{|B}\bigr)

and
-K_{\mathbb{P}^{2}\times\mathbb{P}^{2}}-D
is ample, thus
-K_{X|\widetilde{B}}
is ample.

Let
C\subset X
be an irreducible curve; we show that
-K_{X}\cdot C>0
. This is clear if
C\subset\widetilde{B}
or if
\sigma(C)=\{\text{\it pt}\}
. Otherwise, let

H\in|\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{2}\times\mathbb{P}^{2}}(1,2)|
, so that
\widetilde{B}+\sigma^{*}H\in|-K_{X}|
. Then

\widetilde{B}\cdot C\geq 0
and
\sigma^{*}(H)\cdot C>0
, hence
-K_{X}\cdot C>0
.

We conclude that
-K_{X}
is strictly nef and big, hence ample by the base-point-free theorem. Here are the invariants of
X
:

K_{X}^{4}=194,\ \rho_{X}=3,\ b_{4}(X)=h^{2,2}(X)=11,\ b_{3}(X)=0,\ h^{0}(X,-K_{X})=45.

Figure 11.2.
A section of the cone of effective divisors of
X
in §
11.4
.
Eff
[formula]
(Nef
[formula]
)
Nef(
[formula]
)=Mov(
[formula]
)

Let
\pi_{i}\colon\mathbb{P}^{2}\times\mathbb{P}^{2}\to\mathbb{P}^{2}
be the two projections, and
f_{i}:=\pi_{i}\circ\sigma\colon X\to\mathbb{P}^{2}
,
i=1,2
.

Since
\pi_{1}(A)=\mathbb{P}^{2}
and
(\pi_{1})_{|A}
has degree
2
,
f_{1}
is a quasi-elementary contraction with general fiber
F\cong\operatorname{Bl}_{2\mskip 1.0mu\text{\it pts}}\mathbb{P}^{2}
, and
d_{f_{1}}=\dim\mathcal{N}_{1}(F,X)=2
.

We also have
\widetilde{B}\cong B
and
f_{1|\widetilde{B}}
is a
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-bundle. If
C_{\widetilde{B}}\subset\widetilde{B}
is a fiber of such
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-bundle, we have
-K_{X}\cdot C_{\widetilde{B}}=1
and
\widetilde{B}\cdot C_{\widetilde{B}}=-1
, and
\widetilde{B}
is the exceptional divisor of a divisorial elementary contraction
\alpha\colon X\to Y
of type
(3,2)^{\text{\it sm}}
, such that
\operatorname{NE}(f_{1})=\operatorname{NE}(\sigma)+\operatorname{NE}(\alpha)
.

\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\scriptstyle{f_{1}}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{P}^{2}\times\mathbb{P}^{2}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{1}}
\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{P}^{2}}}

Here
Y
is a smooth Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{Y}=2
, and the map
Y\to\mathbb{P}^{2}
is a quadric bundle.

On the other hand, since
\pi_{2}(A)\subset\mathbb{P}^{2}
is a conic,
f_{2}\colon X\to\mathbb{P}^{2}
is equidimensional but not quasi-elementary; the general fiber is
\mathbb{P}^{2}
. Moreover
f_{2}^{*}(\pi_{2}(A))=\widetilde{D}+E
where
\widetilde{D}\subset X
is the transform of
D
, and
\widetilde{D}\cong\mathbb{P}^{2}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
with normal bundle
\mathcal{N}_{\widetilde{D}/X}\cong\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{2}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}}(-2,2)
. In fact
\widetilde{D}
is the exceptional divisor of a divisorial elementary contraction
\beta\colon X\to Z
of type
(3,1)
, where
Z
is singular along the curve
\beta(\widetilde{D})
.

Finally we note that since
\widetilde{B}\cap\widetilde{D}=\emptyset
, one can check that
\operatorname{NE}(\alpha)+\operatorname{NE}(\beta)
is a face of
\operatorname{NE}(X)
, with a birational contraction
\gamma\colon X\to W
where
\rho_{W}=1
.
If
H_{i}=f_{i}^{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{2}}(1)
, we have
\operatorname{Nef}(X)=\operatorname{Mov}(X)=\langle H_{1},H_{2},\gamma^{*}\operatorname{Nef}W\rangle
and
\operatorname{Eff}(X)=\langle H_{1},\widetilde{B},\widetilde{D},E\rangle
, see Fig.
11.2
.

11.5.
Blow-ups of
\mathbb{P}^{4}
along planes

Let
\sigma\colon X_{s}\to\mathbb{P}^{4}
be the blow-up of
s
general planes
A_{1},\dotsc,A_{s}\subset\mathbb{P}^{4}
, in the following sense: we blow-up one plane
A_{i}
and successively the transforms of the other ones.
Note that each pair of planes intersect pairwise in a point, and
\sigma
does not depend on the order of the blow-ups, see Lemma
2.10
. We will refer to such a map simply as the blow-up of
A_{1},\dotsc,A_{s}
.

We have
\rho_{X_{s}}=1+s
, and for
s=1,2
the
4
-fold
X_{s}
is toric and Fano:
X_{1}\cong\mathbb{P}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(\mathcal{O}^{\oplus 3}\oplus\mathcal{O}(1))
, and
X_{2}
is
D_{17}
in
[
Bat99
]
.

Let
\pi\colon X_{1}\to\mathbb{P}^{1}
be the
\mathbb{P}^{3}
-bundle, and
\sigma^{\prime}\colon X_{s}\to X_{1}
the remaining blow-ups. Let also
\widetilde{A}_{i}\subset X_{1}
be the transform of
A_{i}
for
i=2,\dotsc,s
. Then
\widetilde{A}_{i}\cong\mathbb{F}_{1}
,
\pi_{|\widetilde{A}_{i}}
is the
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-bundle, and
\widetilde{A}_{i}
intersects the general fiber of
\pi
in a line. The composition
\pi\circ\sigma^{\prime}\colon X_{s}\to\mathbb{P}^{1}
is a quasi-elementary contraction with general fiber the blow-up
F_{s}
of
\mathbb{P}^{3}
along
s-1
general lines.
If
X_{s}
is Fano, then
F_{s}
must be Fano too;
by classification
F_{s}
is Fano if and only if
s\leq 4
(see
[
IP99
, Ch. 12]
), hence we get the following.

Lemma 11.3
.

If
X_{s}
is Fano, then
s\leq 4
and
\rho_{X_{s}}\leq 5
.

\begin{array}[]{||c|c|c|c|c|c|c||}\hline\cr\hline\cr s&\rho_{X_{s}}&K_{X_{s}}^{4}&b_{4}(X_{s})=h^{2,2}(X_{s})&b_{3}(X_{s})&\chi(X_{s},-K_{X_{s}})&\text{Fano}\\
\hline\cr\hline\cr 0&1&625&1&0&126&\mathbb{P}^{4}\\
\hline\cr 1&2&512&2&0&105&\text{yes, toric}\\
\hline\cr 2&3&405&4&0&85&\text{yes, toric}\\
\hline\cr 3&4&304&7&0&66&?\\
\hline\cr 4&5&209&11&0&48&?\\
\hline\cr\hline\cr\end{array}

Table 11.1.
Invariants of the blow-ups of
\mathbb{P}^{4}
along
s
planes

Question 11.4
.

Is
X_{s}
Fano for
s\in\{3,4\}
?

11.6.
Blow-ups of quadrics along planes

Let
Q\subset\mathbb{P}^{5}
be a smooth quadric. Recall that
Q
contains two families of planes, and distinct planes in the same family intersect pairwise in a point. Let
A_{1},\dotsc,A_{s}\subset Q
be general planes in the same family, and
\sigma\colon X_{s}\to Q
the blow-up of
A_{1},\dotsc,A_{s}
(see Lemma
2.10
), so that
\rho_{X_{s}}=1+s
.

It is shown in

[
Man24
]
that
X_{5}
is Fano; this implies that
X_{s}
is Fano for every
s\leq 5
.
We have
X_{1}\cong\mathbb{P}_{\mathbb{P}^{2}}(T_{\mathbb{P}^{2}}\oplus\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{2}}(2))
(see
[
SW90
, Theorem on first page]
), and we also note that
X_{2}
is
[
FTT24
, Fano 3-2]
and
X_{3}
is
[
FTT24
, Fano 4-2]
.

For
s\geq 9
we have
\rho_{X_{s}}\geq 10
, hence
X_{s}
cannot be Fano by Th.
1.1
.

Question 11.5
.

Is
X_{s}
Fano for
s\in\{6,7,8\}
?

For
i,j\in\{1,\dotsc,s\}
,
i<j
, let
H_{ij}\subset\mathbb{P}^{5}
be the hyperplane spanned by
A_{i}
and
A_{j}
; then
H_{ij|Q}
is
a cone over a smooth quadric surface.
Moreover let
E_{i}\subset X_{s}
be the exceptional divisor over
A_{i}\subset Q
, for
i=1,\dotsc,s
, and
H\subset X_{s}
the pullback of a general hyperplane section.

The case
s=2
.

Let
\sigma_{1}\colon X_{1}\to Q
be the blow-up of
A_{1}
, and
\sigma_{2}\colon X_{2}\to X_{1}
the blow-up of the transform
\widetilde{A}_{2}\subset X_{1}
of
A_{2}
.
Moreover let
D_{12}^{\prime}\subset X_{1}
and

D_{12}\subset X_{2}
be the transforms of
H_{12|Q}
.

Figure 11.3.
A section of the effective cone of
X_{2}
, blow-up of a quadric
Nef
[formula]
=Mov
[formula]

Under the
\mathbb{P}^{2}
-bundle
\pi_{1}\colon X_{1}\to\mathbb{P}^{2}
, the image of
\widetilde{A}_{2}\subset X_{1}
is a line
\Gamma\subset\mathbb{P}^{2}
, and
\pi_{1}^{-1}(\Gamma)=D^{\prime}_{12}
. The composition
f_{1}:=\pi_{1}\circ\sigma_{2}\colon X_{2}\to\mathbb{P}^{2}
is an equidimensional contraction with
f_{1}^{-1}(\Gamma)=E_{2}\cup D_{12}
, both fixed prime divisors, and
\tau_{f_{1}}=\langle[E_{2}],[D_{12}]\rangle
(see Def.-Rem.
3.2
and Rem.
3.4
).

Symmetrically, we can also factor
\sigma\colon X_{2}\to Q
as
X_{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\alpha}}{{\to}}X_{1}^{\prime}\to Q
where
X_{1}^{\prime}\to Q
is the blow-up of
A_{2}
, and
\alpha\colon X_{2}\to X_{1}^{\prime}
the blow-up of the transform of
A_{1}
. We have a
\mathbb{P}^{2}
-bundle
\pi_{2}\colon X_{1}^{\prime}\to\mathbb{P}^{2}
, and the composition
f_{2}:=\pi_{2}\circ\alpha\colon X_{2}\to\mathbb{P}^{2}
is an equidimensional contraction with
\tau_{f_{2}}=\langle[E_{1}],[D_{12}]\rangle
.

See Fig.
11.3
for a section of
\operatorname{Eff}(X_{2})
; we have
H\equiv D_{12}+H_{1}+H_{2}
,
f_{i}^{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{2}(1)}\equiv H-E_{i}
for
i=1,2
, and
-K_{X_{2}}=4H-E_{1}-E_{2}
.

The restriction
\sigma_{1|D^{\prime}_{12}}\colon D^{\prime}_{12}\to H_{12|Q}
is a small resolution of the quadric cone, and
D^{\prime}_{12}\cong\mathbb{P}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(\mathcal{O}\oplus\mathcal{O}(1)^{\oplus 2})
. Then
D^{\prime}_{12}
is smooth and contains
\widetilde{A}_{2}
, so that
D_{12}\cong D^{\prime}_{12}
, and if
C_{D_{12}}\subset D_{12}
is a line in a fiber of the
\mathbb{P}^{2}
-bundle, we have
D_{12}\cdot C_{D_{12}}=-1
. In fact
D_{12}
is the exceptional divisor of a blow-up
\tau\colon X_{2}\to\mathbb{P}^{2}\times\mathbb{P}^{2}
along a smooth rational curve
C
, which is a complete intersection of divisors of degrees
(1,0),(0,1),(1,1)
.

This can be seen via the embedding
X_{2}\subset P_{2}
where
P_{2}\to\mathbb{P}^{5}
is the blow-up of
A_{1}
and then of
\widetilde{A}_{2}
;

P_{2}
is a toric variety,
and it can be described explicitly using Batyrev’s
language of primitive collections and primitive relations
[
Bat91
]
, and Sato’s description of how primitive relations change after smooth blow-ups and blow-downs
[
Sat00
, §4]
.
There is a blow-up
\tau_{P}\colon P_{2}\to Y:=\mathbb{P}_{\mathbb{P}^{2}}(\mathcal{O}\oplus\mathcal{O}(1)^{\oplus 3})
along a surface
S\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
, with exceptional divisor the transform in
P_{2}
of the hyperplane
H_{12}\subset\mathbb{P}^{5}
spanned by
A_{1}
and
A_{2}
. We have
X_{2}\cdot\operatorname{NE}(\tau_{P})=0
,
\tau_{P|X_{2}}=\tau
,
\tau_{P}(X_{2})\in|\mathcal{O}_{Y}(1)|
, and
\tau_{P}(X_{2})\cong\mathbb{P}^{2}\times\mathbb{P}^{2}
. Moreover the curve
C:=S\cap\tau_{P}(X_{2})\subset\mathbb{P}^{2}\times\mathbb{P}^{2}
is the complete intersection of the divisors
\tau(E_{1})\in\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{2}\times\mathbb{P}^{2}}(1,0)
,
\tau(E_{2})\in\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{2}\times\mathbb{P}^{2}}(0,1)
, and a third divisor in
|\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{2}\times\mathbb{P}^{2}}(1,1)|
.

The case
s=3
.

For
i,j\in\{1,2,3\}
,
i<j
, let
D_{ij}\subset X_{3}
be the transform of
H_{ij|Q}
.
Moreover
let
\Lambda\subset\mathbb{P}^{5}
be the plane spanned by the three points
A_{1}\cap A_{2}
,
A_{1}\cap A_{3}
, and
A_{2}\cap A_{3}
; we note that
\Lambda
is contained in
Q
and also in every hyperplane
H_{ij}
.
Let
L\subset X_{3}
be the transform of
\Lambda
; then
L
is an exceptional plane contained in
D_{ij}
for every
i,j
, and
[C_{L}]
generates a small extremal ray of
\operatorname{NE}(X_{3})
with locus
L
.

Let us consider the flip
X_{3}\dasharrow Y
of
\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}[C_{L}]
. We claim that
Y
is the blow-up of
\mathbb{P}^{4}
at three general lines, with exceptional divisors (the transforms of)
D_{12}
,
D_{13}
,
D_{23}
(see
[
PPS21
]
for a description of
\operatorname{NE}(Y)
and
\operatorname{Eff}(Y)
). The exceptional line
\ell\subset Y
given by the flip is the transform of the unique line in
\mathbb{P}^{4}
intersecting the three blown-up lines. Moreover the exceptional divisors
E_{1},E_{2},E_{3}
of
\sigma\colon X_{3}\to Q
are the transforms of the hyperplanes in
\mathbb{P}^{4}
spanned by two blown-up lines.

This can be seen again by consider the toric variety
P_{3}
obtained by blowing-up
P_{2}
along the transform
\widetilde{A}_{3}
of the third plane
A_{3}\subset\mathbb{P}^{5}
; we have
X_{3}\subset P_{3}
, and
[C_{L}]
still generates a small extremal ray of
\operatorname{NE}(P_{3})
, with locus
L
,
K_{P_{3}}\cdot C_{L}=0
, and
X_{3}\cdot C_{L}=-1
. The flop of this small extremal ray yields a variety isomorphic to
P_{3}
:

\mathbb{P}^{5}\longleftarrow P_{3}\dasharrow P_{3}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\psi}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathbb{P}^{5}.

Then
\psi\colon P_{3}\to\mathbb{P}^{5}
is again a sequence of three blow-up of planes, with exceptional divisors the transforms of first
H_{12}
, then
H_{13}
, and finally
H_{23}
. The image of
X_{3}
is a hyperplane in
\mathbb{P}^{5}
, and
\psi
blows it up in three disjoint lines.

A section of the effective cone of
X_{3}
, blow-up of a quadric

As in the case
r=2
,
for
i\in\{1,2,3\}
there is an equidimensional contraction
f_{i}\colon X_{3}\to\mathbb{P}^{2}
given by the
\mathbb{P}^{2}
-bundle obtained blowing-up
Q
along
A_{i}
.
We have
E_{2}+D_{12}\equiv E_{3}+D_{13}\equiv f_{1}^{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{2}}(1)
and
\tau_{f_{1}}=\langle[E_{2}],[E_{3}],[D_{12}],[D_{13}]\rangle
, and similarly for
f_{2}
and
f_{3}
. See Fig.
11.4
for a section of
\operatorname{Eff}(X_{3})
.

For
s\geq 3
, we still get
\binom{s}{2}
fixed prime divisors
D_{ij}\subset X_{s}
of type
(3,1)^{\text{\it sm}}
, given by the transforms of
H_{ij|Q}
, for
i,j\in\{1,\dotsc,s\}
,
i<j
. Moreover for
i,j,h\in\{1,\dotsc,s\}
,
i<j<h
,
the plane
\Lambda_{ijh}\subset\mathbb{P}^{5}
spanned by the three points
A_{i}\cap A_{j}
,
A_{i}\cap A_{h}
, and
A_{j}\cap A_{h}
, is contained in
Q
and in
H_{ij},H_{ih},H_{jh}
; its transform
L_{ijh}\subset D_{ij}\cap D_{ih}\cap D_{jh}
is an exceptional plane.

11.7.
Blow-ups of cubic
4
-folds along planes

Let
Z\subset\mathbb{P}^{5}
be a smooth cubic
4
-fold containing
s
planes

A_{1},\dotsc,A_{s}\subset Z
that intersect pairwise in a point, and
A_{i}\cap A_{j}\cap A_{k}=\emptyset
for
i<j<k
.

Let
X_{s}\to Z
the blow-up of
A_{1},\dotsc,A_{s}
(see Lemma
2.10
);
then
X_{s}
is a smooth projective
4
-fold with
\rho_{X_{s}}=1+s
, compare Th.
8.1
and Rem.
8.3
.

For
s=1
,
X_{1}
has two elementary contractions, the blow-up
X_{1}\to Z
, and a quadric bundle
X_{1}\to\mathbb{P}^{2}
, so that
X_{1}
is Fano. For
s=2
, we show below that
X_{2}
is still Fano.
On the other hand
for
s\geq 9
we have
\rho_{X_{s}}\geq 10
, hence
X_{s}
cannot be Fano by Th.
1.1
.

Question 11.6
.

For which values of
s\in\{3,\dotsc,8\}
is
X_{s}
Fano?

The case
s=2
.
We keep the same notation as in §
11.6
. We still have
X_{2}\subset P_{2}
, where
P_{2}
is the toric
5
-fold obtained by blowing-up
\mathbb{P}^{5}
along first
A_{1}
and then
\widetilde{A}_{2}
. One can check that
-K_{P_{2}}-X_{2}
is nef and big on
P_{2}
, and
(-K_{P_{2}}-X_{2})^{\perp}\cap\operatorname{NE}(P_{2})=R
where
R
is a small extremal ray with
K_{P_{2}}\cdot R=X_{2}\cdot R=0
. The locus of
R
is a surface
T\cong\mathbb{P}^{2}
, thus either
T\subset X_{2}
, or
T\cap X_{2}=\emptyset
.

Let us consider the blow-up
P_{1}\to\mathbb{P}^{5}
of
A_{1}
. The surface
T\subset P_{2}
is the transform of the fiber
F\subset P_{1}
of the blow-up over the point
A_{1}\cap A_{2}\in\mathbb{P}^{5}
. Then in
P_{1}
we have
X_{1}\cap F=\widetilde{A}_{2}\cap F
and this is a line in
F\cong\mathbb{P}^{2}
; therefore we see that
X_{2}
can intersect
T
at most in a curve, and we conclude that
X_{2}\cap T=\emptyset
. This shows that
-K_{X_{2}}=(-K_{P_{2}}-X_{2})_{|X_{2}}
is ample, and
X_{2}
is Fano.

Let us also consider the blow-up
\tau_{P}\colon P_{2}\to Y=\mathbb{P}_{\mathbb{P}^{2}}(\mathcal{O}\oplus\mathcal{O}(1)^{\oplus 3})
as in §
11.6
, and its restriction
\tau:=\tau_{P|X_{2}}\colon X_{2}\to W:=\tau_{P}(X_{2})
.
If
C\subset P_{2}
is a line in a general non-trivial fiber
F_{\tau}\cong\mathbb{P}^{2}
of
\tau_{P}
, we have

X_{2}\cdot C=1
, therefore
X_{2}
intersects
F_{\tau}
in a line, and
W
contains the surface
S\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
blown-up by
\tau_{P}
. Thus we see that
\tau
is an elementary divisorial contraction of type
(3,2)
with exceptional divisor
D_{12}
, the transform of
H_{12|Z}
.

The tautological class
\mathcal{O}_{Y}(1)
is nef and big, and it is zero on a small extremal ray
R_{Y}
of
\operatorname{NE}(Y)
with locus
T_{Y}:=\tau_{P}(T)
, which is the negative section of the
\mathbb{P}^{3}
-bundle
Y\to\mathbb{P}^{2}
. In
P_{2}
we have
T\cap X_{2}=\emptyset
and
T\cap\operatorname{Exc}(\tau_{P})=\emptyset
, thus
W\cap T_{Y}=\emptyset
in
Y
.
Moreover

W\in|\mathcal{O}_{Y}(2)|
,
-K_{Y}\sim\mathcal{O}_{Y}(4)
, and again we see that
-K_{W}=\mathcal{O}_{Y}(2)_{|W}
is ample, so that
W
is Fano of index
2
.
It is not difficult to see that
W
is a double cover of
\mathbb{P}^{2}\times\mathbb{P}^{2}
with branch divisor of degree
(2,2)
(compare
8.34
), and it has two elementary contractions
W\to\mathbb{P}^{2}
that are quadric bundles. By composing with
\tau
, we get two equidimensional contractions
f_{i}\colon X_{2}\to\mathbb{P}^{2}
,
i=1,2
. Fig.
11.3
still gives a section of
\operatorname{Eff}(X_{2})
; here
H
is the pullback of a general hyperplane section of
Z
, and
-K_{Z}=3H-E_{1}-E_{2}
.
